# More adjectives: J–M

> Adjectives J–M beyond the grammar groups — the everyday adjectives that a triage of WordNet’s adjective lemmas showed a learner needs.

[← The grammar of adjectives](../README.md) &middot; [All groups](README.md)

Most English adjectives behave: they go before a noun or after *be*, and they compare with *-er* or *more*. These are those — but each still says how it compares, where it can stand, and what it takes. WordNet lists about twenty thousand adjectives; most of the rest are technical, archaic or British-only, and a triage pass left them out on purpose.

---
### jaded

**Pronunciation:** /ˈdʒeɪdɪd/ &middot; *JAY-did*
**Русский:** пресыщенный, утративший интерес
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more jaded*, *most jaded*
**Takes:** *about* or *with* + noun

Bored and no longer excited, after too much of something.

1. After twenty years in the business he is pretty *jaded*.
2. *Jaded* voters stayed home on election day.
3. She was *jaded* about dating apps long before she quit them.

---

### jagged

**Pronunciation:** /ˈdʒæɡɪd/ &middot; *JAG-id*
**Русский:** зазубренный, неровный
**Type:** shape
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more jagged*, *most jagged*
**Opposite:** *smooth*
**Contrast:** The *-ed* is a full syllable — *JAG-id*, not *jagd*.

Having a rough, uneven edge with sharp points.

1. He cut his foot on a *jagged* piece of glass.
2. The coastline here is rocky and *jagged*.
3. A *jagged* line ran down the middle of the screen.

---

### jam-packed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌdʒæmˈpækt/ &middot; *jam-PAKT*
**Русский:** битком набитый
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* + noun
**Form:** compound — noun + participle

Completely full of people or things.

1. The stadium was *jam-packed* an hour before kickoff.
2. The book is *jam-packed* with photographs.
3. We rode a *jam-packed* subway car to the game.

---

### Jamaican

**Pronunciation:** /dʒəˈmeɪkən/ &middot; *juh-MAY-kuhn*
**Русский:** ямайский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun for the person: *she's a Jamaican*.

From Jamaica, or belonging to it.

1. *Jamaican* coffee is expensive for a reason.
2. The restaurant serves *Jamaican* food.
3. Her mother is *Jamaican*.

---

### jammed

**Pronunciation:** /dʒæmd/ &middot; *JAMD*
**Русский:** заклинивший; забитый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* + noun, in the crowded sense
**Form:** -ed from *jam*

Jammed has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Stuck so that it will not move or work.** (заклинивший, застрявший)

1. The copier is *jammed* again.
2. He could not get the *jammed* window open.

**2. Filled with too many people or things.** (забитый, переполненный)

3. The highway was *jammed* with traffic.
4. We squeezed into a *jammed* elevator.

---

### jarring

**Pronunciation:** /ˈdʒɑːrɪŋ/ &middot; *JAR-ing*
**Русский:** режущий слух, коробящий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more jarring*, *most jarring*
**Form:** -ing from *jar* — to give an unpleasant shock

Unpleasantly surprising because it does not fit.

1. The ending was *jarring*.
2. There is a *jarring* shift in tone halfway through the book.
3. It is *jarring* to hear your own voice recorded.

---

### jaundiced

**Pronunciation:** /ˈdʒɔːndɪst/ &middot; *JAWN-dist*
**Русский:** предвзятый, циничный; желтушный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — the figurative sense almost always before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable

Jaundiced has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Seeing things bitterly and suspiciously after bad experience.** (предвзятый, циничный)

1. He takes a *jaundiced* view of campaign promises.
2. She looked at the new plan with a *jaundiced* eye.

**2. Having yellow skin and eyes because of liver trouble.** (желтушный)

3. The newborn was slightly *jaundiced* for a few days.
4. His skin looked *jaundiced* under the lamp.

---

### jaunty

**Pronunciation:** /ˈdʒɔːnti/ &middot; *JAWN-tee*
**Русский:** бойкий, щеголеватый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *jauntier*, *jauntiest*

Cheerful and confident in a lively, stylish way.

1. He wore his hat at a *jaunty* angle.
2. She came in with a *jaunty* step.
3. His mood was *jauntier* than it had been all week.

---

### jazzy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈdʒæzi/ &middot; *JAZ-ee*
**Русский:** джазовый; броский, яркий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *jazzier*, *jazziest*
**Form:** -y from *jazz*

Jazzy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Sounding like jazz.** (джазовый, в стиле джаз)

1. The soundtrack has a *jazzy* feel.
2. They played a *jazzy* version of the old hymn.

**2. Bright and showy.** (броский, яркий, пёстрый)

3. He showed up in a *jazzy* orange tie.
4. The new logo is a lot *jazzier* than the old one.

---

### Jeffersonian

**Pronunciation:** /ˌdʒɛfərˈsoʊniən/ &middot; *jef-ur-SOH-nee-uhn*
**Русский:** джефферсоновский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ian from *Jefferson*

Belonging to Thomas Jefferson's ideas of limited government and independent citizens.

1. He appealed to *Jeffersonian* ideals of small government.
2. *Jeffersonian* democracy rested on the independent farmer.
3. The essay traces the *Jeffersonian* strain in American politics.

---

### jerky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈdʒɜːrki/ &middot; *JUR-kee*
**Русский:** резкий, дёрганый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *jerkier*, *jerkiest*
**Opposite:** *smooth*
**Contrast:** Also a noun, the dried meat: *beef jerky*.

Moving in sudden, uneven starts and stops.

1. The camera work is *jerky* and hard to watch.
2. The old elevator starts with a *jerky* motion.
3. His movements got *jerkier* as he tired.

---

### jet-black

**Pronunciation:** /ˌdʒɛtˈblæk/ &middot; *jet-BLAK*
**Русский:** иссиня-чёрный, чёрный как смоль
**Type:** color
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *jet*, a black stone, + *black*

Deep, shiny black.

1. She has *jet-black* hair.
2. The horse was *jet-black* except for one white foot.
3. He drives a *jet-black* pickup.

---

### jeweled

**Pronunciation:** /ˈdʒuːəld/ &middot; *JOO-uhld*
**Русский:** украшенный драгоценными камнями
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *jewel*
**Contrast:** American spelling has one *l*; British writing has *jewelled*.

Decorated with jewels.

1. She wore a *jeweled* bracelet.
2. The crown has a *jeweled* cross on top.
3. A *jeweled* dagger sat in the display case.

---

### jihadi

**Pronunciation:** /dʒɪˈhɑːdi/ &middot; *ji-HAH-dee*
**Русский:** джихадистский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun for the person, and also written *jihadist*.

Belonging to armed Islamist groups that call their fight a holy war.

1. The group ran a network of *jihadi* websites.
2. He was tried on charges of *jihadi* recruitment.
3. *Jihadi* propaganda spread through the app.

---

### jilted

**Pronunciation:** /ˈdʒɪltɪd/ &middot; *JIL-tid*
**Русский:** брошенный, покинутый (о возлюбленном)
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *jilt*

Suddenly left by a lover or a fiancé.

1. The story is told by a *jilted* bride.
2. A *jilted* boyfriend keyed her car.
3. He plays the *jilted* lover in the second act.

---

### jingoistic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌdʒɪŋɡoʊˈɪstɪk/ &middot; *jing-goh-IS-tik*
**Русский:** ура-патриотический, шовинистический
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more jingoistic*, *most jingoistic*

Loudly patriotic in an aggressive way toward other countries.

1. The coverage turned *jingoistic* once the war started.
2. He gave a *jingoistic* speech about national greatness.
3. Critics called the movie *jingoistic*.

---

### jinxed

**Pronunciation:** /dʒɪŋkst/ &middot; *JINKST*
**Русский:** невезучий, сглазенный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *jinx*

Seeming to bring bad luck, or to have it.

1. That car is *jinxed* — it has broken down four times.
2. I felt *jinxed* after losing three games in a row.
3. The whole project seemed *jinxed* from the start.

---

### jittery

**Pronunciation:** /ˈdʒɪtəri/ &middot; *JIT-uh-ree*
**Русский:** нервный, взвинченный
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more jittery*, *most jittery*
**Takes:** *about* + noun

Nervous and unable to keep still.

1. Three cups of coffee made me *jittery*.
2. Investors are *jittery* about the election.
3. She gets *jittery* before a presentation.

---

### jobless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈdʒɑːbləs/ &middot; *JOB-luhs*
**Русский:** безработный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -less from *job*
**Opposite:** *employed*
**Contrast:** *Jobless* is the newspaper word and also describes figures (*jobless claims*); *unemployed* is the more formal one for a person.

Without paid work.

1. He has been *jobless* since the plant closed.
2. *Jobless* claims fell again last month.
3. Thousands of workers were left *jobless*.

---

### joint

**Pronunciation:** /dʒɔɪnt/ &middot; *JOYNT*
**Русский:** совместный, общий
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a common noun: *a knee joint*, and informally a place — *a pizza joint*.

Done, owned or shared by two or more people or groups.

1. They filed a *joint* tax return.
2. The two countries issued a *joint* statement.
3. It was a *joint* effort by three departments.

---

### Jordanian

**Pronunciation:** /dʒɔːrˈdeɪniən/ &middot; *jor-DAY-nee-uhn*
**Русский:** иорданский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun for the person: *two Jordanians were on the flight*.

From Jordan, or belonging to it.

1. The *Jordanian* government denied the report.
2. We met a *Jordanian* student at the conference.
3. Her husband is *Jordanian*.

---

### journalistic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌdʒɜːrnəˈlɪstɪk/ &middot; *jur-nuh-LIS-tik*
**Русский:** журналистский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable

Having to do with the work of reporters and news writing.

1. The piece fails basic *journalistic* standards.
2. She won the prize for *journalistic* integrity.
3. *Journalistic* sources are protected by law in most states.

---

### jovial

**Pronunciation:** /ˈdʒoʊviəl/ &middot; *JOH-vee-uhl*
**Русский:** весёлый, добродушный
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more jovial*, *most jovial*

Cheerful and friendly, often loudly so.

1. Our host was loud and *jovial*.
2. He was in a *jovial* mood all evening.
3. A *jovial* man in a red sweater handed out cider.

---

### joyful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈdʒɔɪfəl/ &middot; *JOY-fuhl*
**Русский:** радостный
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more joyful*, *most joyful*
**Form:** -ful from *joy*
**Opposite:** *sorrowful*

Full of happiness, or showing it.

1. It was a *joyful* reunion.
2. The choir sang a *joyful* hymn.
3. Everyone looked *joyful* when the news came through.

---

### joyless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈdʒɔɪləs/ &middot; *JOY-luhs*
**Русский:** безрадостный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more joyless*, *most joyless*
**Form:** -less from *joy*
**Opposite:** *joyful*

Giving or showing no pleasure at all.

1. It was a *joyless* meal in a silent kitchen.
2. The work became *joyless* after the reorganization.
3. She described her childhood as *joyless*.

---

### joyous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈdʒɔɪəs/ &middot; *JOY-uhs*
**Русский:** радостный, ликующий
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more joyous*, *most joyous*
**Contrast:** Formal or literary; in ordinary speech Americans say *joyful* or just *happy*.

Full of great happiness.

1. It was a *joyous* occasion for the whole family.
2. The crowd let out a *joyous* shout.
3. The entire weekend was *joyous*.

---

### jubilant

**Pronunciation:** /ˈdʒuːbɪlənt/ &middot; *JOO-buh-luhnt*
**Русский:** ликующий, торжествующий
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more jubilant*, *most jubilant*

Openly delighted, usually after a win or good news.

1. *Jubilant* fans poured onto the field.
2. She was *jubilant* after the verdict.
3. A *jubilant* crowd filled the square until midnight.

---

### Judeo-Christian

**Pronunciation:** /dʒuːˌdeɪoʊˈkrɪstʃən/ &middot; *joo-day-oh-KRIS-chuhn*
**Русский:** иудео-христианский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *Judeo-* + *Christian*

Shared by the Jewish and Christian traditions.

1. He appealed to *Judeo-Christian* values.
2. The course covers the *Judeo-Christian* tradition.
3. *Judeo-Christian* ethics shaped the law of that period.

---

### judgmental

**Pronunciation:** /dʒʌdʒˈmɛntəl/ &middot; *juj-MEN-tuhl*
**Русский:** осуждающий, склонный осуждать
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more judgmental*, *most judgmental*
**Takes:** *about* + noun
**Contrast:** American spelling drops the *e*: *judgmental*, *judgment*.

Too quick to criticize other people.

1. I do not want to sound *judgmental*, but that was a mistake.
2. She is *judgmental* about how other people raise their kids.
3. He gave me a *judgmental* look over his glasses.

---

### judicial

**Pronunciation:** /dʒuːˈdɪʃəl/ &middot; *joo-DISH-uhl*
**Русский:** судебный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *Judicial* means "of the courts"; *judicious* means "showing good judgment".

Having to do with courts, judges and the law.

1. The law faces *judicial* review in the fall.
2. *Judicial* appointments to that court are for life.
3. She built her career in the *judicial* branch.

---

### judicious

**Pronunciation:** /dʒuːˈdɪʃəs/ &middot; *joo-DISH-uhs*
**Русский:** рассудительный, благоразумный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more judicious*, *most judicious*
**Takes:** *about* or *in* + noun or *-ing*
**Contrast:** Not the same word as *judicial*, which is about courts.

Showing good sense and careful judgment.

1. A *judicious* use of salt makes all the difference.
2. He was *judicious* about what he said in public.
3. The editor made a few *judicious* cuts.

---

### juicy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈdʒuːsi/ &middot; *JOO-see*
**Русский:** сочный; пикантный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *juicier*, *juiciest*
**Form:** -y from *juice*

Juicy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Full of juice.** (сочный)

1. These peaches are *juicy*.
2. This one is *juicier* than the last.

**2. Interesting because it is scandalous or full of gossip.** (пикантный, скандальный)

3. She has all the *juicy* details.
4. The book is full of *juicy* stories about the studio.

---

### jumbled

**Pronunciation:** /ˈdʒʌmbəld/ &middot; *JUM-buhld*
**Русский:** перепутанный, беспорядочный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more jumbled*, *most jumbled*
**Form:** -ed from *jumble*

Mixed together with no order.

1. The papers were *jumbled* in a cardboard box.
2. Her notes are a *jumbled* mess.
3. My memory of that night is *jumbled*.

---

### jumbo

**Pronunciation:** /ˈdʒʌmboʊ/ &middot; *JUM-boh*
**Русский:** огромный, гигантский
**Type:** size
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable

Much bigger than the usual size, especially of a product.

1. She ordered a *jumbo* box of popcorn.
2. *Jumbo* shrimp cost more per pound.
3. The store sells eggs in a *jumbo* size.

---

### jumpy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈdʒʌmpi/ &middot; *JUM-pee*
**Русский:** нервный, пугливый
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *jumpier*, *jumpiest*

Nervous and easily startled.

1. The dog is *jumpy* around strangers.
2. I have been *jumpy* ever since the break-in.
3. He gets *jumpier* the closer we get to the deadline.

---

### junior

**Pronunciation:** /ˈdʒuːniər/ &middot; *JOO-nee-ur*
**Русский:** младший, младший по должности
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *to* + noun
**Opposite:** *senior*
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *a junior in high school*, *she's the junior on the team*.

Lower in rank, or younger.

1. She is a *junior* partner at the firm.
2. Two *junior* staffers wrote the report.
3. He is *junior* to me by three years.

---

### jury-rigged

**Pronunciation:** /ˈdʒʊri rɪɡd/ &middot; *JUUR-ee rigd*
**Русский:** сооружённый на скорую руку
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + participle, from sailing, where a *jury mast* was a temporary one
**Contrast:** Often mixed up with *jerry-built*, which means badly built rather than temporary.

Put together quickly from whatever was on hand.

1. They sailed home under a *jury-rigged* mast.
2. The whole setup is *jury-rigged* with duct tape.
3. He built a *jury-rigged* antenna out of a coat hanger.

---

### just

**Pronunciation:** /dʒʌst/ &middot; *JUST*
**Русский:** справедливый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more just*, *most just*
**Opposite:** *unjust*
**Contrast:** The adjective means "fair". The everyday *just* in *I just got here* is an adverb and a different use altogether.

Fair and morally right.

1. It was a *just* decision.
2. They spent their lives fighting for a *just* society.
3. The punishment seemed *just* to everyone in the room.

---

### justifiable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈdʒʌstɪfaɪəbəl/ &middot; *JUS-tuh-fy-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** оправданный, обоснованный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more justifiable*, *most justifiable*
**Form:** -able from *justify*
**Opposite:** *unjustifiable*

Able to be defended as reasonable or right.

1. The expense is *justifiable* given the results.
2. The court ruled it was *justifiable* self-defense.
3. Her anger was entirely *justifiable*.

---

### justified

**Pronunciation:** /ˈdʒʌstɪfaɪd/ &middot; *JUS-tuh-fyd*
**Русский:** оправданный; имеющий основания
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *in* + *-ing*
**Form:** -ed from *justify*

Justified has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having a good reason; fair in the circumstances.** (оправданный, обоснованный)

1. Her complaint was fully *justified*.
2. You were *justified* in walking out of that meeting.

**2. Of text, spaced so that both margins line up evenly.** (выровненный по ширине (о тексте))

3. The columns are *justified* on both sides.
4. Set the paragraph to *justified* rather than ragged right.

---

### juvenile

**Pronunciation:** /ˈdʒuːvənaɪl/ &middot; *JOO-vuh-nyl*
**Русский:** подростковый, несовершеннолетний; инфантильный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more juvenile*, *most juvenile*
**Contrast:** Also a noun in legal and news writing: *a juvenile was arrested*.

Juvenile has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having to do with young people below the legal adult age.** (подростковый, несовершеннолетний)

1. The case went to *juvenile* court.
2. *Juvenile* crime dropped sharply in the 1990s.

**2. Childish in a way that annoys adults.** (ребяческий, инфантильный)

3. His jokes are *juvenile*.
4. Stop being so *juvenile* about it.

---

### juxtaposed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌdʒʌkstəˈpoʊzd/ &middot; *juk-stuh-POHZD*
**Русский:** сопоставленный, помещённый рядом
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *juxtapose*

Placed side by side so that the difference shows.

1. The two photographs are *juxtaposed* on facing pages.
2. The *juxtaposed* images make the point better than any caption.
3. Her calm voice, *juxtaposed* with the sirens, was eerie.

---

### Kafkaesque

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkɑːfkəˈɛsk/ &middot; *kahf-kuh-ESK*
**Русский:** кафкианский, абсурдно-бюрократический
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -esque from *Kafka*, the novelist

Nightmarishly confusing and impersonal, like the bureaucracy in Kafka's novels.

1. Getting the permit was a *Kafkaesque* ordeal.
2. The whole appeals process felt *Kafkaesque*.
3. He describes a *Kafkaesque* maze of forms and phone trees.

---

### kaleidoscopic

**Pronunciation:** /kəˌlaɪdəˈskɑːpɪk/ &middot; *kuh-ly-duh-SKOP-ik*
**Русский:** калейдоскопический, пёстрый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable

Made of many bright parts that keep shifting.

1. The film offers a *kaleidoscopic* view of the city.
2. Sunlight through the glass made a *kaleidoscopic* pattern on the floor.
3. Her novels are *kaleidoscopic* — dozens of voices at once.

---

### kaput

**Pronunciation:** /kəˈpʊt/ &middot; *kuh-PUUT*
**Русский:** сломанный, накрывшийся
**Type:** quality
**Position:** predicative only — after a verb: *the TV is kaput*, never *a kaput TV*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** from German *kaputt*
**Contrast:** Informal and a little jokey; in writing use *broken* or *out of order*.

Broken and finished, beyond repair.

1. The washing machine is *kaput*.
2. My old laptop finally went *kaput*.
3. After the storm the generator was *kaput*.

---

### Kashmiri

**Pronunciation:** /kæʃˈmɪri/ &middot; *kash-MIR-ee*
**Русский:** кашмирский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun for the person and for the language.

From Kashmir, or belonging to it.

1. She bought a *Kashmiri* shawl.
2. *Kashmiri* cooking uses a lot of saffron.
3. His grandparents were *Kashmiri*.

---

### Kenyan

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɛnjən/ &middot; *KEN-yuhn*
**Русский:** кенийский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun for the person: *three Kenyans finished in the top ten*.

From Kenya, or belonging to it.

1. The *Kenyan* runners swept the race.
2. We had *Kenyan* coffee at breakfast.
3. Her father is *Kenyan*.

---

### key

**Pronunciation:** /kiː/ &middot; *KEE*
**Русский:** ключевой, главный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *to* + noun, after a verb
**Contrast:** Also a very common noun: *house keys*, *the key to the puzzle*.

Most important; the one the rest depends on.

1. She played a *key* role in the negotiations.
2. *Key* witnesses refused to testify.
3. Timing is *key* to the whole plan.

---

### keyless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkiːləs/ &middot; *KEE-luhs*
**Русский:** бесключевой
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -less from *key*

Working without a physical key.

1. The car has *keyless* entry.
2. Our front door uses a *keyless* lock.
3. *Keyless* ignition became standard on that model.

---

### Keynesian

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkeɪnziən/ &middot; *KAYN-zee-uhn*
**Русский:** кейнсианский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ian from *Keynes*, the economist
**Contrast:** Said KAYN-, not KEEN-, because the name *Keynes* rhymes with *rains*.

Following Keynes's idea that government spending should support demand.

1. The stimulus was a classic *Keynesian* response.
2. He teaches *Keynesian* economics.
3. *Keynesian* policy favors spending in a downturn.

---

### khaki

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkæki/ &middot; *KAK-ee*
**Русский:** хаки, песочно-жёлтый
**Type:** color
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun; the plural *khakis* means the pants themselves.

Of a dull yellowish-brown color.

1. He wore a *khaki* jacket.
2. The uniform was *khaki* with brown boots.
3. She bought two pairs of *khaki* pants.

---

### kindhearted

**Pronunciation:** /ˌkaɪndˈhɑːrtɪd/ &middot; *kynd-HAR-tid*
**Русский:** добросердечный, добрый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more kindhearted*, *most kindhearted*
**Form:** compound — *kind* + *heart* + *-ed*
**Opposite:** *coldhearted*

Naturally kind and generous toward others.

1. She is the most *kindhearted* person I know.
2. He was a gruff but *kindhearted* old man.
3. A *kindhearted* neighbor shoveled our walk.

---

### kindly

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkaɪndli/ &middot; *KYND-lee*
**Русский:** добрый, доброжелательный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *kindlier*, *kindliest*
**Form:** -ly adjective, from *kind*
**Contrast:** *Kindly* is far more common as an adverb (*kindly step back*); as an adjective it is slightly old-fashioned and usually sits before a noun.

Kind and gentle in manner.

1. A *kindly* woman showed us the way to the station.
2. He had a *kindly* face and a slow way of talking.
3. She was *kindlier* to strangers than to her own family.

---

### kindred

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɪndrɪd/ &middot; *KIN-drid*
**Русский:** родственный, близкий по духу
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Nearly always in the fixed phrase *a kindred spirit*, meaning someone who thinks and feels as you do.

Of the same kind, or feeling the same way.

1. We were *kindred* spirits from the first conversation.
2. The two languages are *kindred* tongues.
3. They share a *kindred* interest in old maps.

---

### kinetic

**Pronunciation:** /kɪˈnɛtɪk/ &middot; *ki-NET-ik*
**Русский:** кинетический; динамичный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more kinetic*, *most kinetic*

Kinetic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having to do with movement, especially in physics.** (кинетический, связанный с движением)

1. *Kinetic* energy depends on mass and speed.
2. The museum has a room of *kinetic* sculpture.

**2. Full of fast, restless energy.** (динамичный, энергичный)

3. The dance number is wildly *kinetic*.
4. His prose has a *kinetic* rhythm to it.

---

### king-size

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɪŋ saɪz/ &middot; *KING-syz*
**Русский:** очень большой, «королевского» размера
**Type:** size
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + noun
**Contrast:** Also written *king-sized*; both are normal in American English.

Of the largest size made, especially of a bed.

1. We slept in a *king-size* bed.
2. He bought a *king-size* candy bar.
3. The room comes with a *king-size* mattress.

---

### kinky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɪŋki/ &middot; *KING-kee*
**Русский:** с причудами, извращённый; курчавый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *kinkier*, *kinkiest*
**Contrast:** The sexual meaning is the one most listeners hear first, so *kinky hair* can sound like a joke; many Americans say *coily* or *tightly curled* instead.

Kinky has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Involving unusual sexual tastes.** (с сексуальными причудами, извращённый)

1. The novel has a *kinky* streak running through it.
2. He had some *kinky* ideas about the costume party.

**2. Of hair, wire or cord, full of tight curls or bends.** (курчавый; перекрученный)

3. Her hair is thick and *kinky*.
4. The cord got *kinkier* every time we coiled it.

---

### kitschy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɪtʃi/ &middot; *KICH-ee*
**Русский:** китчевый, безвкусный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *kitschier*, *kitschiest*
**Form:** -y from *kitsch*

Cheap and tasteless, often in a way people enjoy on purpose.

1. The lobby is full of *kitschy* souvenirs.
2. I love how *kitschy* that diner is.
3. Their decorations get *kitschier* every Christmas.

---

### knee-deep

**Pronunciation:** /ˌniːˈdiːp/ &middot; *nee-DEEP*
**Русский:** по колено; по уши (в делах)
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *in* + noun
**Form:** compound — noun + adjective

Knee-deep has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Deep enough to reach the knees.** (по колено (о глубине))

1. We waded through *knee-deep* water.
2. The snow was *knee-deep* by morning.

**2. Deeply involved in something, usually work or trouble.** (по уши (в работе, в неприятностях))

3. He is *knee-deep* in paperwork.
4. By then she was *knee-deep* in the investigation.

---

### knee-length

**Pronunciation:** /ˈniː lɛŋθ/ &middot; *NEE-length*
**Русский:** до колена
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + noun

Long enough to reach the knee.

1. She wore a *knee-length* skirt.
2. *Knee-length* boots are back in style.
3. The coat is *knee-length*.

---

### knitted

**Pronunciation:** /ˈnɪtɪd/ &middot; *NIT-id*
**Русский:** вязаный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *knit*
**Contrast:** *Knit* works as the adjective too, and is more common in short phrases: *a knit cap*.

Made by knitting yarn.

1. She gave me a *knitted* scarf.
2. He pulled on a *knitted* hat.
3. The store sells *knitted* blankets.

---

### knobby

**Pronunciation:** /ˈnɑːbi/ &middot; *NOB-ee*
**Русский:** шишковатый, узловатый
**Type:** shape
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *knobbier*, *knobbiest*
**Form:** -y from *knob*

Covered with small round lumps.

1. The old tree has *knobby* roots.
2. He had *knobby* knees and long arms.
3. These tires are *knobbier* than the ones made for pavement.

---

### knowable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈnoʊəbəl/ &middot; *NOH-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** познаваемый, поддающийся выяснению
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -able from *know*
**Opposite:** *unknowable*

Able to be known or found out.

1. Some things are simply not *knowable*.
2. The exact number is not *knowable* from the records we have.
3. He wanted a *knowable* answer, not a guess.

---

### knowing

**Pronunciation:** /ˈnoʊɪŋ/ &middot; *NOH-ing*
**Русский:** понимающий, многозначительный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** A *knowing* look means the person is in on a secret; it does not mean "well informed" — that is *knowledgeable*.

Showing that you understand something others have not said out loud.

1. She gave me a *knowing* smile.
2. There was a *knowing* look between the two of them.
3. He answered with a *knowing* nod.

---

### knowledgeable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈnɑːlɪdʒəbəl/ &middot; *NOL-i-juh-buhl*
**Русский:** знающий, осведомлённый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more knowledgeable*, *most knowledgeable*
**Takes:** *about* + noun
**Form:** -able from *knowledge*

Knowing a lot about something.

1. The salesperson was very *knowledgeable* about bikes.
2. We need a *knowledgeable* guide for the canyon.
3. She is the most *knowledgeable* person on the team.

---

### known

**Pronunciation:** /noʊn/ &middot; *NOHN*
**Русский:** известный, установленный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Preposition patterns:** [known for](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#known-for)
**Opposite:** *unknown*
**Contrast:** Before a noun it means "recognized, on record": *a known problem*. After a verb, *known as* and *known for* are the passive verb, not this adjective.

Recognized or established as a fact.

1. He is a *known* troublemaker in the building.
2. The side effects are a *known* risk.
3. There is no *known* cure.

---

### kooky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkuːki/ &middot; *KOO-kee*
**Русский:** чудаковатый, со странностями
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *kookier*, *kookiest*

Strange in a harmless, funny way.

1. She has a *kooky* sense of humor.
2. The neighbors are friendly but a little *kooky*.
3. His theories get *kookier* every year.

---

### kosher

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkoʊʃər/ &middot; *KOH-shur*
**Русский:** кошерный; законный, честный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable

Kosher has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Prepared according to Jewish dietary law.** (кошерный)

1. The deli is strictly *kosher*.
2. We bought *kosher* salt for the recipe.

**2. Honest and above board.** (законный, честный, нормальный)

3. Something about the deal did not seem *kosher*.
4. Is it *kosher* to email him directly?

---

### Kurdish

**Pronunciation:** /ˈkɜːrdɪʃ/ &middot; *KUR-dish*
**Русский:** курдский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also the name of the language: *he speaks Kurdish*.

Belonging to the Kurds or their region.

1. *Kurdish* forces held the north of the country.
2. He comes from a *Kurdish* family.
3. The village is mostly *Kurdish*.

---

### Kuwaiti

**Pronunciation:** /kʊˈweɪti/ &middot; *kuu-WAY-tee*
**Русский:** кувейтский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun for the person: *two Kuwaitis signed the deal*.

From Kuwait, or belonging to it.

1. The *Kuwaiti* government funded the hospital.
2. He works for a *Kuwaiti* oil company.
3. Her business partner is *Kuwaiti*.

---

### labeled

**Pronunciation:** /ˈleɪbəld/ &middot; *LAY-buhld*
**Русский:** помеченный, с этикеткой
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *label*
**Contrast:** American spelling has one *l*; British writing has *labelled*.

Marked with a label saying what it is.

1. Every box was clearly *labeled*.
2. Keep the leftovers in a *labeled* container.
3. The wires were not *labeled*, so we had to guess.

---

### labor-intensive

**Pronunciation:** /ˌleɪbər ɪnˈtɛnsɪv/ &middot; *lay-bur in-TEN-siv*
**Русский:** трудоёмкий
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more labor-intensive*, *most labor-intensive*
**Form:** compound — noun + adjective
**Opposite:** *capital-intensive*
**Contrast:** American spelling *labor*; British writing has *labour-intensive*.

Needing a lot of人 work by hand.

1. Picking the fruit by hand is *labor-intensive*.
2. It is a *labor-intensive* process that cannot be automated.
3. The new method is less *labor-intensive* than the old one.

---

### labored

**Pronunciation:** /ˈleɪbərd/ &middot; *LAY-burd*
**Русский:** затруднённый; натужный, вымученный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more labored*, *most labored*
**Contrast:** American spelling *labored*; British writing has *laboured*.

Labored has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Slow and difficult, especially of breathing or movement.** (затруднённый, тяжёлый (о дыхании))

1. His breathing was *labored*.
2. She climbed the last flight with *labored* steps.

**2. Sounding forced instead of natural.** (натужный, вымученный)

3. The joke felt *labored*.
4. The essay ends on a *labored* note.

---

### laborious

**Pronunciation:** /ləˈbɔːriəs/ &middot; *luh-BOR-ee-uhs*
**Русский:** трудоёмкий, кропотливый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more laborious*, *most laborious*

Taking a lot of time and hard, dull effort.

1. Copying the records by hand was *laborious*.
2. It is a *laborious* process, but it works.
3. He described the *laborious* business of restoring the piano.

---

### labyrinthine

**Pronunciation:** /ˌlæbəˈrɪnθɪn/ &middot; *lab-uh-RIN-thin*
**Русский:** запутанный, лабиринтообразный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more labyrinthine*, *most labyrinthine*
**Form:** -ine from *labyrinth*

So complicated that it is hard to follow or to find your way through.

1. The tax code is *labyrinthine*, even for accountants.
2. We got lost in the *labyrinthine* hallways of the old hospital.
3. Her novels have *labyrinthine* plots that reward a second reading.

---

### laced

**Pronunciation:** /leɪst/ &middot; *LAYST*
**Русский:** с добавлением (чего-л.); на шнуровке
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — the *with* sense usually follows a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *lace*

Laced has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Mixed with a small amount of alcohol, a drug or something else strong.** (с добавлением (алкоголя, наркотика))

1. The punch was *laced* with rum.
2. Police said the pills were *laced* with fentanyl.
3. His apology was *laced* with sarcasm.

**2. Fastened or trimmed with laces.** (на шнуровке, со шнуровкой)

4. He pulled on a pair of heavy *laced* boots.
5. The dress has a *laced* back that ties at the waist.

---

### lackadaisical

**Pronunciation:** /ˌlækəˈdeɪzɪkəl/ &middot; *lak-uh-DAY-zih-kuhl*
**Русский:** вялый, небрежный, безразличный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more lackadaisical*, *most lackadaisical*
**Opposite:** *diligent*

Without energy, care or interest in something that matters.

1. The team's *lackadaisical* first half cost them the game.
2. He was let go for his *lackadaisical* attitude toward deadlines.
3. Nobody is *more lackadaisical* about answering email than my brother.

---

### lackluster

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlæklʌstər/ &middot; *LAK-luhs-tur*
**Русский:** тусклый, невыразительный, посредственный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more lackluster*, *most lackluster*
**Form:** compound — *lack* + *luster*
**Contrast:** American spelling *lackluster*; the British form is *lacklustre*.

Dull and without energy, excitement or quality.

1. Sales have been *lackluster* since the spring.
2. Critics called the movie *lackluster*.
3. After a *lackluster* start, the band finally woke up.

---

### laconic

**Pronunciation:** /ləˈkɑːnɪk/ &middot; *luh-KAH-nik*
**Русский:** немногословный, лаконичный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more laconic*, *most laconic*
**Contrast:** *laconic* describes a person or a remark that uses very few words, often dryly. A short text that simply says everything briefly is *concise*.

Using very few words.

1. His *laconic* reply was just: Fine.
2. The sheriff was a tall, *laconic* man who nodded a lot.
3. She gets *more laconic* the angrier she is.

---

### lactating

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlækteɪtɪŋ/ &middot; *LAK-tay-ting*
**Русский:** кормящая грудью, лактирующая
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — *a lactating mother*; after a verb it is the verb: *She is lactating*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *lactate*

Producing milk for a baby or a young animal.

1. The drug is not recommended for pregnant or *lactating* women.
2. *Lactating* cows are milked twice a day.
3. The hospital has a quiet room for *lactating* mothers.

---

### lacy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈleɪsi/ &middot; *LAY-see*
**Русский:** кружевной, ажурный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *lacier*, *laciest*
**Form:** -y from *lace*

Made of lace, or as delicate and full of small holes as lace.

1. She wore a *lacy* white blouse.
2. Frost made a *lacy* pattern on the window.
3. The second dress was even *lacier* than the first.

---

### laden

**Pronunciation:** /ˈleɪdən/ &middot; *LAY-duhn*
**Русский:** нагруженный, обременённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — before a noun it usually needs an adverb (*a heavily laden truck*); after a verb it takes *with*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* + noun
**Form:** an old participle of *lade*, to load; also the second half of compounds: *fat-laden*, *snow-laden*

Loaded or filled with a large amount of something.

1. The table was *laden* with food.
2. A heavily *laden* truck crawled up the hill.
3. His speech was *laden* with clichés.

---

### ladylike

**Pronunciation:** /ˈleɪdilaɪk/ &middot; *LAY-dee-lyk*
**Русский:** женственный, подобающий леди
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more ladylike*, *most ladylike*
**Form:** compound — *lady* + *-like*
**Contrast:** The word now sounds old-fashioned, and *not very ladylike* is usually a criticism of a woman's behavior rather than a neutral description.

Polite and graceful in the way people once expected a woman to be.

1. She sat with her ankles crossed in a *ladylike* way.
2. Burping at the table is not very *ladylike*.
3. Her grandmother's idea of *ladylike* behavior died out long ago.

---

### laid up

**Pronunciation:** /ˌleɪd ˈʌp/ &middot; *layd UHP*
**Русский:** прикованный к постели (из-за болезни, травмы)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** predicative only — after *be* or *get*: *He's laid up with the flu*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* + noun
**Contrast:** Do not confuse it with *laid off*, which means you lost your job because the company cut staff.

Stuck in bed or at home because of illness or injury.

1. He's been *laid up* with a bad back for two weeks.
2. I was *laid up* for a month after the surgery.
3. Half the team is *laid up* with the flu.

---

### laid-back

**Pronunciation:** /ˌleɪdˈbæk/ &middot; *layd-BAK*
**Русский:** расслабленный, спокойный, непринуждённый
**Type:** quality — informal
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more laid-back*, *most laid-back*
**Opposite:** *uptight*

Relaxed and not easily worried or made angry.

1. Her dad is really *laid-back* about curfews.
2. It's a *laid-back* office — nobody wears a tie.
3. Portland felt *more laid-back* than New York.

---

### laid-off

**Pronunciation:** /ˌleɪdˈɔːf/ &middot; *layd-AWF*
**Русский:** уволенный по сокращению
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only — after a verb it is the passive: *She was laid off in May*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** from the phrasal verb *lay off*
**Contrast:** *laid-off* means the job disappeared; *fired* means the person was let go for bad work or misconduct.

Having lost a job because the employer cut staff.

1. The city set up a job fair for *laid-off* factory workers.
2. *Laid-off* employees keep their health coverage for three months.
3. A group of *laid-off* engineers started their own company.

---

### laissez-faire

**Pronunciation:** /ˌlɛseɪˈfɛr/ &middot; *les-ay-FAIR*
**Русский:** невмешательский, основанный на невмешательстве
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more laissez-faire*, *most laissez-faire*
**Form:** from French, let (them) do

Laissez-faire has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of a government or an economy: leaving business alone, with few rules.** (невмешательский (об экономической политике))

1. They argued for a *laissez-faire* approach to the internet.
2. The 1920s were a *laissez-faire* decade in American banking.

**2. Of a person: leaving others to do as they like.** (попустительский, невмешивающийся)

3. Her parents were *laissez-faire* about homework.
4. He has a *more laissez-faire* management style than his predecessor.

---

### lame

**Pronunciation:** /leɪm/ &middot; *LAYM*
**Русский:** слабый, неубедительный (разг.); хромой
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *lamer*, *lamest*
**Contrast:** Used of a person, *lame* for unable to walk well now sounds offensive; people say *has trouble walking* or name the condition. The informal sense is the one you hear every day.

Lame has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Informal: weak, unconvincing or boring.** (слабый, неубедительный; скучный)

1. That's the *lamest* excuse I've ever heard.
2. The party was kind of *lame*.
3. He made a *lame* joke about my haircut.

**2. Unable to walk normally because of an injured leg or foot.** (хромой)

4. The horse went *lame* after the third race.
5. One of the puppies is *lame* in a back leg.

---

### lamentable

**Pronunciation:** /ləˈmɛntəbəl/ &middot; *luh-MEN-tuh-buhl*
**Русский:** прискорбный, плачевный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more lamentable*, *most lamentable*
**Form:** -able from *lament*

Very bad or disappointing, in a way that deserves regret.

1. The report describes a *lamentable* failure of oversight.
2. Her handwriting is *lamentable*.
3. It is *lamentable* that so few people voted.

---

### landlocked

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlændlɑːkt/ &middot; *LAND-lahkt*
**Русский:** не имеющий выхода к морю
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *land* + *locked*

Surrounded by land, with no coast on the sea.

1. Bolivia has been *landlocked* since 1884.
2. *Landlocked* countries pay more to ship their goods.
3. A dozen *landlocked* states depend on the river for trade.

---

### landscaped

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlændskeɪpt/ &middot; *LAND-skaypt*
**Русский:** благоустроенный, с ландшафтным дизайном
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *landscape*

Planted and arranged to look attractive.

1. The apartments have beautifully *landscaped* grounds.
2. A *landscaped* median runs down the middle of the boulevard.
3. The backyard is fully *landscaped*, so there is nothing left to do.

---

### languid

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlæŋɡwɪd/ &middot; *LANG-gwid*
**Русский:** томный, вялый, неторопливый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more languid*, *most languid*

Slow and relaxed, without energy, often in a pleasant way.

1. They spent a *languid* afternoon by the pool.
2. She waved a *languid* hand at the waiter.
3. The music had a *languid*, sleepy rhythm.

---

### lanky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlæŋki/ &middot; *LANG-kee*
**Русский:** долговязый
**Type:** size
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *lankier*, *lankiest*

Tall and thin, with long arms and legs.

1. A *lanky* teenager answered the door.
2. He's *lanky* like his father.
3. The *lankiest* kid on the team plays center.

---

### Laotian

**Pronunciation:** /leɪˈoʊʃən/ &middot; *lay-OH-shuhn*
**Русский:** лаосский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *a Laotian*. The language is *Lao*, and *Lao* also works as the adjective.

From Laos, or connected with Laos.

1. We ate at a *Laotian* restaurant downtown.
2. Her grandparents are *Laotian*.
3. The museum has a room of *Laotian* textiles.

---

### lapsed

**Pronunciation:** /læpst/ &middot; *LAPST*
**Русский:** отошедший (от веры); утративший силу
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *lapse*

Lapsed has 2 distinct senses.

**1. No longer practicing the religion you were raised in.** (отошедший от веры)

1. He calls himself a *lapsed* Catholic.
2. A *lapsed* Baptist, she still knows every hymn by heart.

**2. No longer valid because it was not renewed or paid for.** (просроченный, утративший силу)

3. The company sent a notice about her *lapsed* policy.
4. You can restore a *lapsed* membership for a small fee.

---

### large-cap

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɑːrdʒkæp/ &middot; *LARJ-kap*
**Русский:** с высокой капитализацией (о компании, акциях)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *large* + *capitalization*
**Opposite:** *small-cap*

Of a company or its stock: with a very large total market value.

1. The fund holds mostly *large-cap* stocks.
2. *Large-cap* companies fell less than the rest of the market.
3. He moved his savings into a *large-cap* index fund.

---

### large-scale

**Pronunciation:** /ˌlɑːrdʒˈskeɪl/ &middot; *larj-SKAYL*
**Русский:** крупномасштабный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable — though the compound *larger-scale* is common
**Opposite:** *small-scale*

Involving a lot of people, money, space or effort.

1. The city is planning *large-scale* repairs to the water system.
2. *Large-scale* farming needs heavy machinery.
3. There has been no *large-scale* violence since the election.

---

### larger-than-life

**Pronunciation:** /ˌlɑːrdʒərðənˈlaɪf/ &middot; *lar-jur-thuhn-LYF*
**Русский:** колоритный, яркий; больше натуральной величины
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable

Larger-than-life has 2 distinct senses.

**1. More colorful, exciting or exaggerated than ordinary people.** (колоритный, яркий (о человеке))

1. Her father was a *larger-than-life* character who knew everyone in town.
2. The coach's personality is *larger-than-life*.

**2. Bigger than the real thing.** (больше натуральной величины)

3. A *larger-than-life* statue of the founder stands in the lobby.
4. The mural shows him *larger-than-life*, twenty feet tall.

---

### lascivious

**Pronunciation:** /ləˈsɪviəs/ &middot; *luh-SIV-ee-uhs*
**Русский:** похотливый, сладострастный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more lascivious*, *most lascivious*

Showing open and offensive sexual interest.

1. He was fired for *lascivious* comments about a coworker.
2. The town council covered the statue up because they thought it *lascivious*.
3. She caught his *lascivious* grin in the mirror.

---

### last-ditch

**Pronunciation:** /ˌlæstˈdɪtʃ/ &middot; *last-DICH*
**Русский:** отчаянный, последний (о попытке)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — from defending the last ditch of a fortification

Made as a final attempt after everything else has failed.

1. The governor made a *last-ditch* effort to save the plant.
2. In a *last-ditch* attempt to pass the class, he took the makeup exam.
3. *Last-ditch* talks broke down after midnight.

---

### last-place

**Pronunciation:** /ˌlæstˈpleɪs/ &middot; *last-PLAYS*
**Русский:** занимающий последнее место
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Finishing at the bottom of a competition or a ranking.

1. The *last-place* team gets the first pick in the draft.
2. He went from a *last-place* finish to the podium in one season.
3. Their *last-place* showing in the polls surprised no one.

---

### lasting

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlæstɪŋ/ &middot; *LAS-ting*
**Русский:** длительный, прочный, надолго
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more lasting*, *most lasting*
**Form:** -ing from *last*
**Opposite:** *temporary*

Continuing for a long time, often for good.

1. The trip made a *lasting* impression on her.
2. After ten years of war they finally reached a *lasting* peace.
3. Kindness leaves a *more lasting* mark than money does.

---

### latent

**Pronunciation:** /ˈleɪtənt/ &middot; *LAY-tuhnt*
**Русский:** скрытый, латентный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *latent* is present but not showing yet; *hidden* is something deliberately kept out of sight.

Present but not yet active, visible or developed.

1. The virus can stay *latent* for years.
2. Good teachers find *latent* talent.
3. The crisis exposed *latent* tensions in the coalition.

---

### lateral

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlætərəl/ &middot; *LAT-ur-uhl*
**Русский:** боковой; горизонтальный (о перемещении)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Lateral has 2 distinct senses.

**1. At, from or toward the side.** (боковой)

1. The knee has very little *lateral* movement.
2. A *lateral* line of sensors runs along the fish's body.

**2. Of a job change: to a similar level, not a promotion.** (горизонтальный (о переводе, карьере))

3. He made a *lateral* move to another department.
4. The new title looks better, but it's really a *lateral* step.

---

### Latin-American

**Pronunciation:** /ˌlætɪnəˈmɛrɪkən/ &middot; *lat-in-uh-MER-ih-kuhn*
**Русский:** латиноамериканский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Usually written without the hyphen before a noun: *Latin American literature*. The hyphen survives mostly in older writing and in phrases like *Latin-American relations*.

From the Spanish- and Portuguese-speaking countries of Central and South America, or connected with them.

1. She teaches *Latin-American* history.
2. The festival shows *Latin-American* films all week.
3. Most of the players on the roster are *Latin-American*.

---

### Latino

**Pronunciation:** /ləˈtiːnoʊ/ &middot; *luh-TEE-noh*
**Русский:** латиноамериканский (о людях в США)
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *a Latino*, *Latinos*; the feminine is *Latina*, and *Latinx* is a newer gender-neutral form. *Hispanic* points to the Spanish language, *Latino* to Latin America — Brazilians are *Latino* but not *Hispanic*.

Of people in the United States whose families come from Latin America.

1. The district has a large *Latino* population.
2. She is the first *Latino* mayor in the city's history.
3. *Latino* voters split almost evenly in the last election.

---

### latter-day

**Pronunciation:** /ˌlætərˈdeɪ/ &middot; *lat-ur-DAY*
**Русский:** современный, новоявленный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Modern, or a modern version of someone or something from the past.

1. He styles himself a *latter-day* Robin Hood.
2. The band is a *latter-day* version of the 1970s original.
3. It's a *latter-day* gold rush, with data instead of gold.

---

### Latvian

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlætviən/ &middot; *LAT-vee-uhn*
**Русский:** латышский, латвийский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun — a person (*a Latvian*) and the language (*She speaks Latvian*).

From Latvia, or connected with Latvia.

1. She sings in a *Latvian* choir.
2. The *Latvian* team took bronze.
3. His grandparents are *Latvian*.

---

### laudable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɔːdəbəl/ &middot; *LAW-duh-buhl*
**Русский:** похвальный, достойный похвалы
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more laudable*, *most laudable*
**Form:** -able from *laud*, to praise

Deserving praise, even if it does not succeed.

1. The program has a *laudable* goal and a tiny budget.
2. Her effort to learn Japanese in a year was *laudable*.
3. It is *laudable* that the company published the numbers at all.

---

### laughable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlæfəbəl/ &middot; *LAF-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** смехотворный, нелепый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more laughable*, *most laughable*
**Form:** -able from *laugh*
**Contrast:** *laughable* means so bad or silly that people laugh at it. Something funny on purpose is *funny* or *hilarious*.

So poor, small or absurd that it cannot be taken seriously.

1. The offer was *laughable* — half of what the house is worth.
2. Their security was *laughable*; the back door was propped open.
3. He made a *laughable* attempt at an Irish accent.

---

### lavender

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlævəndər/ &middot; *LAV-uhn-dur*
**Русский:** бледно-лиловый, лавандовый
**Type:** color
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun — the plant and its scent: *a field of lavender*, *lavender soap*.

Of a pale purple color, like the flower.

1. She painted the bedroom *lavender*.
2. He wore a *lavender* tie to the wedding.
3. The sky turned *lavender* just after sunset.

---

### lavish

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlævɪʃ/ &middot; *LAV-ish*
**Русский:** щедрый, роскошный; расточительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more lavish*, *most lavish*
**Takes:** *with* or *in* + noun, of a person

Lavish has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Very large, expensive or generous in amount.** (роскошный, щедрый)

1. They threw a *lavish* wedding for four hundred guests.
2. The reviews were *lavish*.
3. The director's *lavish* sets cost more than the cast.

**2. Giving a great deal of something, often too much.** (щедрый на что-л., расточительный)

4. Her boss is *lavish* with compliments and stingy with raises.
5. Critics were *lavish* in their praise of the album.

---

### law-abiding

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɔːəbaɪdɪŋ/ &middot; *LAW-uh-by-ding*
**Русский:** законопослушный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more law-abiding*, *most law-abiding*
**Form:** compound — noun + participle, from *abide by the law*

Obeying the law.

1. He described himself as a *law-abiding* citizen.
2. Most gun owners in the county are *law-abiding*.
3. It's a quiet, *law-abiding* neighborhood.

---

### lawful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɔːfəl/ &middot; *LAW-fuhl*
**Русский:** законный, правомерный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ful from *law*
**Opposite:** *unlawful*
**Contrast:** *lawful* and *legal* overlap, but *lawful* sounds formal and is fixed in phrases like *lawful permanent resident*, *a lawful order*, *his lawful wife*.

Allowed or recognized by law.

1. Officers may use force only for a *lawful* purpose.
2. She is a *lawful* permanent resident.
3. The judge ruled that the strike was *lawful*.

---

### lawless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɔːləs/ &middot; *LAW-luhs*
**Русский:** беззаконный, не признающий законов
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more lawless*, *most lawless*
**Form:** -less from *law*

Without law and order, or ignoring the law.

1. The border towns were *lawless* for a decade.
2. *Lawless* gangs controlled the port.
3. The internet is less *lawless* than it was in 1995.

---

### lax

**Pronunciation:** /læks/ &middot; *LAKS*
**Русский:** нестрогий, небрежный, халатный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** -er/-est or more/most — *laxer* or *more lax*, *laxest* or *most lax*
**Takes:** *about* or *in* + noun or *-ing*
**Opposite:** *strict*

Not strict or careful enough.

1. Security at the airport was *lax*.
2. The school has been *lax* about enforcing the dress code.
3. Standards got *more lax* every year.

---

### lay

**Pronunciation:** /leɪ/ &middot; *LAY*
**Русский:** мирской, светский; непрофессиональный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Three other words look the same: the verb *lay* (*lay the book down*), the past of *lie* (*He lay on the couch*), and the noun in *the lay of the land*. The adjective lives in *a lay person*, *the lay reader*, *a lay preacher*.

Lay has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not a member of the clergy.** (мирской, светский)

1. *Lay* volunteers run most of the parish programs.
2. A *lay* preacher led the service that Sunday.

**2. Not trained in a particular profession; ordinary.** (непрофессиональный, неспециалист)

3. The book explains the science for a *lay* reader.
4. To a *lay* audience the two diseases look the same.

---

### layered

**Pronunciation:** /ˈleɪərd/ &middot; *LAY-urd*
**Русский:** многослойный; многоплановый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more layered*, *most layered*
**Form:** -ed from *layer*

Layered has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Made or arranged in layers.** (многослойный, слоёный)

1. Dress in *layered* clothing — it gets cold at night.
2. She got her hair cut short and *layered*.

**2. Having several levels of meaning or detail.** (многоплановый, глубокий)

3. It's a *layered* performance that gets better on a second viewing.
4. The report gives a *more layered* picture than the headlines did.

---

### lead-free

**Pronunciation:** /ˌlɛdˈfriː/ &middot; *led-FREE*
**Русский:** не содержащий свинца, бессвинцовый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + *-free*
**Contrast:** The *lead* here is the metal, and it rhymes with *bed*, not with *need*.

Containing no lead.

1. All the paint in the house is *lead-free*.
2. *Lead-free* solder is standard in electronics now.
3. The school replaced the old fountains with *lead-free* ones.

---

### leaded

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɛdɪd/ &middot; *LED-id*
**Русский:** содержащий свинец; со свинцовым переплётом (о стекле)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *lead*, the metal
**Contrast:** Two syllables, LED-id. *Leaded* gasoline was banned for cars in the United States in 1996; the word survives in *leaded glass* and in *unleaded*.

Leaded has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Containing lead as an additive.** (этилированный, содержащий свинец)

1. *Leaded* gasoline is still sold for some small airplanes.
2. Old pipes and *leaded* paint are the usual sources of the metal.

**2. Of glass or windows: set in strips of lead.** (витражный, со свинцовым переплётом)

3. The church has beautiful *leaded* windows.
4. A *leaded* glass door separates the two rooms.

---

### leaden

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɛdən/ &middot; *LED-uhn*
**Русский:** свинцовый; тяжёлый, гнетущий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more leaden*, *most leaden*
**Form:** -en from *lead*, the metal

Leaden has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Dull gray, the color of lead.** (свинцовый (о цвете))

1. A *leaden* sky hung over the city all week.
2. The sea was flat and *leaden*.

**2. Heavy, slow and without life.** (тяжёлый, гнетущий)

3. By mile twenty my legs felt *leaden*.
4. The dialogue is *leaden* and the jokes fall flat.

---

### leading

**Pronunciation:** /ˈliːdɪŋ/ &middot; *LEE-ding*
**Русский:** ведущий, главный; наводящий (о вопросе)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *lead*

Leading has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Most important, most successful, or in first place.** (ведущий, главный)

1. She is the country's *leading* expert on bats.
2. The company is a *leading* maker of batteries.
3. He played the *leading* role in two Broadway shows.

**2. Of a question: worded so that it pushes someone toward a particular answer.** (наводящий (вопрос))

4. The judge ruled that the question was *leading*.
5. Avoid *leading* questions when you write a survey.

---

### leafy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈliːfi/ &middot; *LEE-fee*
**Русский:** покрытый листвой, зелёный; листовой
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *leafier*, *leafiest*
**Form:** -y from *leaf*

Leafy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Full of leaves or trees.** (зелёный, утопающий в зелени)

1. They live on a *leafy* street near the park.
2. The trail is cool and *leafy* even in July.
3. The *leafiest* neighborhoods are noticeably cooler in summer.

**2. Of a vegetable: with large green leaves that you eat.** (листовой (об овощах))

4. Eat more dark *leafy* greens.
5. Spinach and other *leafy* vegetables are high in iron.

---

### leaky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈliːki/ &middot; *LEE-kee*
**Русский:** протекающий, дырявый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *leakier*, *leakiest*
**Form:** -y from *leak*

Leaky has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Letting liquid or gas escape through a hole or a crack.** (протекающий, дырявый)

1. A *leaky* faucet can waste gallons a day.
2. The roof is *leaky* in two places.
3. This is the *leakiest* tent we have ever owned.

**2. Of an organization: letting secret information get out.** (допускающий утечки (информации))

4. The agency has a *leaky* front office.
5. It's a *leaky* administration — everything ends up in the papers.

---

### lean

**Pronunciation:** /liːn/ &middot; *LEEN*
**Русский:** худощавый; постный (о мясе); скудный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *leaner*, *leanest*
**Contrast:** The verb *lean* (*lean against the wall*) sounds exactly the same but is a different word.

Lean has 4 distinct senses.

**1. Thin in a healthy, muscular way.** (худощавый, поджарый)

1. He's tall and *lean*, like a runner.
2. Six months of training left her *leaner* and faster.

**2. Of meat: with very little fat.** (постный, нежирный)

3. Use *lean* ground beef for the chili.
4. The recipe calls for a *lean* cut of pork.

**3. Of a period of time: with little money, food or success.** (скудный, голодный (о годах))

5. The restaurant survived some *lean* years.
6. Farmers put something aside for the *lean* months.

**4. Of a company or a system: using no more people or money than it needs.** (экономный, работающий без излишеств)

7. The startup stayed *lean* — eleven people and no office.
8. They run a *lean* operation with two shifts and no managers.

---

### learned

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɜːrnɪd/ &middot; *LUR-nid*
**Русский:** учёный, эрудированный; приобретённый (о поведении)
**Type:** quality; also participial
**Position:** both — the erudite sense goes before a noun (*a learned professor*); the acquired sense also follows a verb: *That fear is learned*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more learned*, *most learned*
**Contrast:** Two pronunciations. The adjective meaning full of learning has two syllables, LUR-nid. *learned* meaning gained by learning (*learned behavior*) has one syllable, LURND, like the past tense of *learn*.

Learned has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having or showing deep knowledge gained from long study.** (учёный, эрудированный)

1. He gave a *learned* lecture on Roman law.
2. The article appeared in a *learned* journal.

**2. Gained through experience or teaching rather than born in you.** (приобретённый, выученный)

3. Fear of snakes is partly *learned* behavior.
4. An accent is *learned*, not inherited.

---

### Lebanese

**Pronunciation:** /ˌlɛbəˈniːz/ &middot; *leb-uh-NEEZ*
**Русский:** ливанский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** The noun has the same form in the singular and the plural: *a Lebanese*, *the Lebanese*.

From Lebanon, or connected with Lebanon.

1. We had *Lebanese* food — hummus, kibbeh and plenty of bread.
2. Her mother is *Lebanese*.
3. The *Lebanese* pound has lost most of its value.

---

### lecherous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɛtʃərəs/ &middot; *LECH-ur-uhs*
**Русский:** похотливый, развратный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more lecherous*, *most lecherous*

Showing unpleasant, obvious sexual interest in other people.

1. Her boss was a *lecherous* old man who always stood too close.
2. He gave the waitress a *lecherous* look.
3. The character is funny, *lecherous* and completely unreliable.

---

### leery

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɪri/ &middot; *LEER-ee*
**Русский:** настороженный, недоверчивый
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** predicative only — *I'm leery of cheap flights*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more leery*, *most leery*
**Takes:** *of* + noun or *-ing*; also *about*

Careful and a little suspicious about something.

1. I'm *leery* of anything that promises fast money.
2. Investors are *leery* after last year's crash.
3. She was *leery* about lending him the car.

---

### left

**Pronunciation:** /lɛft/ &middot; *LEFT*
**Русский:** левый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — for the side itself English uses the noun: *on the left*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *right*
**Contrast:** Three other *left*s: the noun (*take the next left*), the adverb (*turn left*), and the past participle of *leave* — *There's no milk left* means none is remaining.

On the side of your body where your heart is, or on that side of something.

1. He broke his *left* wrist.
2. Take the *left* lane after the bridge.
3. The *left* side of the building gets all the afternoon sun.

---

### left-hand

**Pronunciation:** /ˌlɛftˈhænd/ &middot; *left-HAND*
**Русский:** левый, расположенный слева
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *left-hand* describes where a thing is (*the left-hand column*); *left-handed* describes a person who writes with the left hand.

On or toward the left side.

1. The totals are in the *left-hand* column.
2. Take the *left-hand* fork in the trail.
3. The spare key is in the *left-hand* drawer.

---

### left-of-center

**Pronunciation:** /ˌlɛftəvˈsɛntər/ &middot; *left-uhv-SEN-tur*
**Русский:** левоцентристский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *right-of-center*
**Contrast:** American spelling *left-of-center*; British writing has *left-of-centre*.

Slightly to the political left, but not extreme.

1. The paper's editorials are *left-of-center*.
2. She won as a *left-of-center* candidate in a conservative district.
3. The government is a coalition of two *left-of-center* parties.

---

### left-wing

**Pronunciation:** /ˌlɛftˈwɪŋ/ &middot; *left-WING*
**Русский:** левый, левого крыла (полит.)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more left-wing*, *most left-wing*
**Opposite:** *right-wing*

Supporting socialist or strongly progressive politics.

1. The bill was written by the party's *left-wing* members.
2. His views got *more left-wing* after college.
3. She writes for a *left-wing* magazine.

---

### leftist

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɛftɪst/ &middot; *LEF-tist*
**Русский:** левый, левацкий (полит.)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more leftist*, *most leftist*
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *a leftist*. In American English it often sounds disapproving; *progressive* and *liberal* are the neutral words.

Belonging to or supporting the political left.

1. A *leftist* coalition won the election.
2. The magazine has a *leftist* editorial line.
3. Her politics were *leftist* long before it was fashionable.

---

### leftover

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɛftoʊvər/ &middot; *LEFT-oh-vur*
**Русский:** оставшийся, недоеденный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** As a noun it is usually plural: *We ate leftovers all week.*

Remaining after the rest has been used or eaten.

1. We made soup with the *leftover* turkey.
2. There's *leftover* paint in the garage.
3. She used *leftover* fabric to make a pillow.

---

### legal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈliːɡəl/ &middot; *LEE-guhl*
**Русский:** юридический, правовой; законный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *to*-infinitive in *it is legal to do something*
**Opposite:** *illegal*
**Contrast:** There is no *unlegal*: the opposite of the allowed sense is *illegal*.

Legal has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Connected with the law.** (юридический, правовой)

1. You should get *legal* advice before you sign.
2. The company's *legal* department is on the fourth floor.

**2. Allowed by law.** (законный, разрешённый)

3. It is *legal* to turn right on red in most states.
4. Marijuana is *legal* here for adults over 21.

---

### legendary

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɛdʒəndɛri/ &middot; *LEJ-uhn-der-ee*
**Русский:** легендарный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more legendary*, *most legendary*
**Form:** -ary from *legend*

Legendary has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Very famous and much admired.** (легендарный, прославленный)

1. She was a *legendary* jazz singer.
2. His patience with beginners is *legendary*.

**2. Coming from an old story, and possibly not real.** (легендарный, сказочный)

3. The *legendary* founder of the city was raised by wolves.
4. They went looking for the *legendary* lost mine.

---

### leggy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɛɡi/ &middot; *LEG-ee*
**Русский:** длинноногий; вытянувшийся (о растении)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *leggier*, *leggiest*
**Form:** -y from *leg*

Leggy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having long legs.** (длинноногий)

1. A *leggy* colt followed the mare around the field.
2. She was the *leggiest* girl in the class by sixth grade.

**2. Of a plant: tall, thin and weak from too little light.** (вытянувшийся, тонкий (о рассаде))

3. The seedlings got *leggy* on the windowsill.
4. Pinch the tops back or the basil will go *leggy*.

---

### legible

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɛdʒəbəl/ &middot; *LEJ-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** разборчивый, читаемый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more legible*, *most legible*
**Opposite:** *illegible*
**Contrast:** *legible* is about handwriting or print you can make out; *readable* is about writing that is easy or enjoyable to follow.

Clear enough to read.

1. His signature is barely *legible*.
2. Print in black ink so the form stays *legible*.
3. The inscription is still *legible* after two hundred years.

---

### legislative

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɛdʒɪsleɪtɪv/ &middot; *LEJ-ih-slay-tiv*
**Русский:** законодательный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ive from *legislate*
**Contrast:** One of the three branches of American government: *legislative*, *executive*, *judicial*.

Connected with making laws, or with the body that makes them.

1. The bill died in the last *legislative* session.
2. Congress holds the *legislative* power.
3. She handles *legislative* affairs for a trade group.

---

### legitimate

**Pronunciation:** /lɪˈdʒɪtəmət/ &middot; *lih-JIT-uh-muht*
**Русский:** обоснованный; законный, легитимный
**Type:** classifying; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more legitimate*, *most legitimate*
**Opposite:** *illegitimate*
**Contrast:** The verb is spelled the same but said lih-JIT-uh-mayt, and the everyday verb is *legitimize*.

Legitimate has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Fair and reasonable; resting on good grounds.** (обоснованный, законный (о претензии, вопросе))

1. That's a *legitimate* question.
2. Parents have a *legitimate* interest in what is taught.

**2. Allowed by law or by the rules; genuine.** (законный, легальный)

3. The money came from a *legitimate* business.
4. Is this a *legitimate* website or a scam?

**3. Born to parents who are married to each other.** (законнорождённый)

5. Under the old law only *legitimate* children could inherit.
6. The court upheld the marriage and declared the children *legitimate*.

---

### lengthwise

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɛŋkθwaɪz/ &middot; *LENGTH-wyz*
**Русский:** продольный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *length* + *-wise*
**Contrast:** Far more common as an adverb: *Cut the zucchini lengthwise.* Before a noun it is an adjective: *a lengthwise cut*. *Lengthways* means the same and sounds British.

Running along the length of something rather than across it.

1. Make one *lengthwise* cut down the middle of the dough.
2. The table has a *lengthwise* groove for the cables.
3. A *lengthwise* stripe makes the room look longer.

---

### lengthy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɛŋθi/ &middot; *LENG-thee*
**Русский:** длинный, затянувшийся
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *lengthier*, *lengthiest*
**Contrast:** *lengthy* usually carries a complaint: a *lengthy* speech is one you wished were shorter. For neutral length use *long*.

Lasting or taking a long time, often longer than you want.

1. The approval process is *lengthy*.
2. After a *lengthy* delay, the flight finally boarded.
3. The second hearing was *lengthier* than the first.

---

### lenient

**Pronunciation:** /ˈliːniənt/ &middot; *LEE-nee-uhnt*
**Русский:** мягкий, снисходительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more lenient*, *most lenient*
**Takes:** *with* or *on* + noun
**Opposite:** *strict*

Less strict or less severe than expected, especially in punishing.

1. The judge was *lenient* because it was a first offense.
2. Our coach is *lenient* with freshmen.
3. Critics called the fine far too *lenient*.

---

### lesbian

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɛzbiən/ &middot; *LEZ-bee-uhn*
**Русский:** лесбийский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *She is a lesbian.* As an adjective it describes people, couples and communities: *a lesbian couple*, *lesbian writers*.

Of women who are sexually attracted to women.

1. They were the first *lesbian* couple married in the county.
2. The novel has a *lesbian* main character.
3. She writes about *lesbian* life in the 1950s.

---

### lesser

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɛsər/ &middot; *LES-ur*
**Русский:** меньший, второстепенный
**Type:** quantity; also classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** irregular — *lesser* is itself the comparative of *little*; the superlative is *least*
**Contrast:** *lesser* is already a comparative, so never *more lesser*. It goes before a noun; after a verb use *less* or *smaller*.

Lesser has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Smaller in size, amount or importance.** (меньший)

1. He pleaded guilty to a *lesser* charge.
2. It's the *lesser* of two evils.
3. The same rule applies, to a *lesser* degree, to part-time staff.

**2. Less well known or less important than others of the same kind.** (второстепенный, менее известный)

4. The show includes a few *lesser* painters of the period.
5. Even his *lesser* novels are worth reading.

---

### lethal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈliːθəl/ &middot; *LEE-thuhl*
**Русский:** смертельный, смертоносный
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more lethal*, *most lethal*
**Opposite:** *harmless*

Lethal has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Able to cause death.** (смертельный, смертоносный)

1. The snake's bite is *lethal* without treatment.
2. They gave him a *lethal* dose by mistake.
3. This strain is *more lethal* than the one from last winter.

**2. Very effective and dangerous to an opponent.** (убийственный, крайне опасный)

4. Her serve is *lethal*.
5. The two of them together are a *lethal* combination on the ice.

---

### lethargic

**Pronunciation:** /ləˈθɑːrdʒɪk/ &middot; *luh-THAR-jik*
**Русский:** вялый, апатичный, сонный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more lethargic*, *most lethargic*
**Form:** -ic from *lethargy*

Without energy; slow and sleepy.

1. The medicine made him *lethargic* all afternoon.
2. The puppy was *lethargic* and wouldn't eat, so we called the vet.
3. By August the heat makes everyone *lethargic*.

---

### level

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɛvəl/ &middot; *LEV-uhl*
**Русский:** ровный, горизонтальный; равный; спокойный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more level*, *most level*
**Takes:** *with* + noun
**Contrast:** Also a noun (*the top level*) and a verb (*level the ground*, *level with me*).

Level has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Flat and even, with no part higher than another.** (ровный, горизонтальный)

1. Make sure the shelf is *level* before you screw it in.
2. Set the tent up on *level* ground.

**2. At the same height or the same score as something else.** (вровень, наравне)

3. The water was *level* with the top of the dock.
4. The teams were *level* at halftime.

**3. Calm and steady.** (спокойный, ровный (о голосе, взгляде))

5. She answered in a *level* voice.
6. He looked at me with a *level* gaze and said nothing.

---

### level-headed
