**Pronunciation:** /ˌlɛvəlˈhɛdɪd/ &middot; *lev-uhl-HED-id*
**Русский:** уравновешенный, рассудительный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more level-headed*, *most level-headed*
**Form:** compound — adjective + noun + *-ed*

Calm and sensible, especially when things go wrong.

1. We need someone *level-headed* running the meeting.
2. She stayed *level-headed* while everyone else panicked.
3. He's the *most level-headed* person on the crew.

---

### lewd

**Pronunciation:** /luːd/ &middot; *LOOD*
**Русский:** непристойный, похабный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *lewder*, *lewdest*

Sexual in a crude, offensive way.

1. He was arrested for *lewd* behavior in public.
2. The song's *lewd* lyrics kept it off the radio.
3. Each verse is *lewder* than the last.

---

### libelous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlaɪbələs/ &middot; *LY-buh-luhs*
**Русский:** клеветнический (в печати)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ous from *libel*
**Contrast:** American spelling *libelous*; British *libellous*. *libel* is written or published; spoken defamation is *slander*, and its adjective is *slanderous*.

Containing a false written statement that damages someone's reputation.

1. The magazine pulled the article after its lawyers called it *libelous*.
2. He sued, claiming the post was *libelous*.
3. A *libelous* accusation can cost a newspaper millions.

---

### liberal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɪbərəl/ &middot; *LIB-ur-uhl*
**Русский:** либеральный; щедрый; вольный (о трактовке)
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more liberal*, *most liberal*
**Opposite:** *conservative*
**Contrast:** A false friend. In the United States a *liberal* is on the political left — for social programs and government spending. In Europe and Russia a *liberal* is often a free-market politician, closer to an American *conservative*. In *a liberal arts college* the word keeps an older sense: general education rather than training for a job.

Liberal has 3 distinct senses.

**1. On the political left; in favor of social change and government help.** (либеральный, левый (в США))

1. The district reliably elects *liberal* candidates.
2. His views got *more liberal* after he moved to the city.

**2. Generous, or given in large amounts.** (щедрый, обильный)

3. Add a *liberal* amount of butter.
4. He was *liberal* with both his time and his money.

**3. Not strict; loose.** (вольный, нестрогий)

5. That's a *liberal* reading of the rules.
6. The movie is a *liberal* adaptation of the book.

---

### liberated

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɪbəreɪtɪd/ &middot; *LIB-uh-ray-tid*
**Русский:** освобождённый; свободный от условностей
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more liberated*, *most liberated*
**Form:** -ed from *liberate*

Liberated has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Free from old rules and expectations about how to live.** (свободный от условностей, раскрепощённый)

1. She felt *liberated* after quitting the job.
2. He came out of therapy feeling *liberated*.

**2. Freed from enemy control.** (освобождённый (от оккупации))

3. The *liberated* city celebrated for three days.
4. Photographs from the *liberated* camps shocked the world.

---

### Liberian

**Pronunciation:** /laɪˈbɪriən/ &middot; *ly-BEER-ee-uhn*
**Русский:** либерийский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *a Liberian*.

From Liberia, or connected with Liberia.

1. The ship sails under a *Liberian* flag.
2. *Liberian* English carries a lot from the American South.
3. Her husband is *Liberian*.

---

### Libyan

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɪbiən/ &middot; *LIB-ee-uhn*
**Русский:** ливийский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *a Libyan*.

From Libya, or connected with Libya.

1. *Libyan* oil exports fell by half.
2. The museum's *Libyan* collection is mostly Roman.
3. Two of the students are *Libyan*.

---

### licensed

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlaɪsənst/ &middot; *LY-suhnst*
**Русский:** лицензированный, имеющий лицензию
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *to*-infinitive: *licensed to practice in two states*
**Form:** -ed from *license*
**Opposite:** *unlicensed*
**Contrast:** In American English both the noun and the verb are spelled *license*; British writing uses *licence* for the noun.

Having official permission to do or sell something.

1. She's a *licensed* electrician.
2. The bar is *licensed* to serve beer and wine.
3. Only *licensed* contractors can pull the permit.

---

### life-or-death

**Pronunciation:** /ˌlaɪfɔːrˈdɛθ/ &middot; *lyf-or-DETH*
**Русский:** критический, жизненно важный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also written *life-and-death*, with the same meaning.

So serious that someone's life depends on it.

1. Paramedics make *life-or-death* decisions in seconds.
2. For her it was a *life-or-death* question, not a debate.
3. The surgeon described a *life-or-death* struggle in the operating room.

---

### life-size

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlaɪfsaɪz/ &middot; *LYF-syz*
**Русский:** в натуральную величину
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *life-size* and *life-sized* are both standard in American English.

As big as the real person, animal or thing.

1. There's a *life-size* statue of Lincoln in the lobby.
2. He built a *life-size* model of the plane's cockpit.
3. The cardboard cutout is *life-size*, which makes it a little creepy.

---

### life-threatening

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlaɪfθrɛtənɪŋ/ &middot; *LYF-thret-uh-ning*
**Русский:** угрожающий жизни
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + participle

Serious enough to kill someone.

1. Her injuries are serious but not *life-threatening*.
2. The allergy can cause a *life-threatening* reaction.
3. He was treated for a *life-threatening* infection.

---

### lifeless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlaɪfləs/ &middot; *LYF-luhs*
**Русский:** безжизненный; вялый, скучный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more lifeless*, *most lifeless*
**Form:** -less from *life*

Lifeless has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Dead, or looking dead.** (безжизненный, мёртвый)

1. The bird lay *lifeless* on the porch.
2. Divers found the *lifeless* body an hour later.

**2. Dull and without energy.** (вялый, невыразительный)

3. The second act is *lifeless*.
4. His voice was flat and *lifeless* on the phone.

---

### lifelike

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlaɪflaɪk/ &middot; *LYF-lyk*
**Русский:** как живой, реалистичный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more lifelike*, *most lifelike*
**Form:** compound — *life* + *-like*

Looking very much like the real person or thing.

1. The wax figures are disturbingly *lifelike*.
2. She paints *lifelike* portraits from photographs.
3. The animation gets *more lifelike* every year.

---

### lifelong

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlaɪflɔːŋ/ &middot; *LYF-lawng*
**Русский:** пожизненный, на всю жизнь
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *life* + *long*

Lasting for someone's whole life.

1. They've been *lifelong* friends.
2. He's a *lifelong* Cubs fan.
3. She had a *lifelong* fear of flying.

---

### light-skinned

**Pronunciation:** /ˌlaɪtˈskɪnd/ &middot; *lyt-SKIND*
**Русский:** светлокожий
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable — the compound *lighter-skinned* does the work of a comparative
**Form:** compound — adjective + noun + *-ed*
**Opposite:** *dark-skinned*

Having pale or fair skin.

1. *Light-skinned* people burn faster in the sun.
2. The brothers look alike, though one is *light-skinned* and one is not.
3. The study followed both *light-skinned* and dark-skinned patients.

---

### lightheaded

**Pronunciation:** /ˌlaɪtˈhɛdɪd/ &middot; *lyt-HED-id*
**Русский:** испытывающий головокружение
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb: *I felt lightheaded*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more lightheaded*, *most lightheaded*
**Contrast:** Also written *light-headed*. Not the same as *lighthearted*, which is about mood.

Feeling dizzy and slightly faint.

1. I stood up too fast and felt *lightheaded*.
2. She gets *lightheaded* when she skips breakfast.
3. *Lightheaded* patients should sit down right away.

---

### lighthearted

**Pronunciation:** /ˌlaɪtˈhɑːrtɪd/ &middot; *lyt-HAR-tid*
**Русский:** беззаботный, весёлый, лёгкий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more lighthearted*, *most lighthearted*
**Contrast:** Also written *light-hearted*. Do not confuse it with *lightheaded*, which means dizzy.

Cheerful and not serious.

1. It's a *lighthearted* movie about a serious subject.
2. The mood at dinner was *lighthearted*.
3. He made a *lighthearted* remark to break the tension.

---

### lightweight

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlaɪtweɪt/ &middot; *LYT-wayt*
**Русский:** лёгкий; несерьёзный, поверхностный
**Type:** quality; also classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** As a noun it is an insult: *He's a lightweight* means he is not to be taken seriously, or cannot handle much alcohol.

Lightweight has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Weighing less than usual for its kind.** (лёгкий, облегчённый)

1. Pack a *lightweight* jacket for the evenings.
2. The frame is *lightweight* but surprisingly strong.

**2. In boxing and other sports: of the weight class below welterweight.** (лёгкого веса (о весовой категории))

3. He fought as a *lightweight* for six years.
4. She won the *lightweight* title in June.

**3. Not serious or not deep.** (несерьёзный, поверхностный)

5. It's a *lightweight* novel — perfect for the beach.
6. Critics dismissed the report as *lightweight*.

---

### likable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlaɪkəbəl/ &middot; *LY-kuh-buhl*
**Русский:** приятный, симпатичный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more likable*, *most likable*
**Form:** -able from *like*
**Opposite:** *unlikable*
**Contrast:** American English drops the *e*: *likable*. *Likeable* is the British spelling.

Pleasant and easy to like.

1. He's a *likable* guy — everybody gets along with him.
2. The main character is flawed but *likable*.
3. She's *more likable* in person than on television.

---

### like-minded

**Pronunciation:** /ˌlaɪkˈmaɪndɪd/ &middot; *lyk-MYN-did*
**Русский:** придерживающийся тех же взглядов, единомышленный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *like*, meaning similar, + *minded*

Having the same opinions, tastes or goals.

1. She found a group of *like-minded* parents online.
2. The conference brings together *like-minded* researchers.
3. He started the club with a few *like-minded* friends.

---

### lilac

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlaɪlək/ &middot; *LY-luhk*
**Русский:** сиреневый
**Type:** color
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun — the shrub and its flowers: *The lilacs bloom in May.*

Of a pale pinkish-purple color, like the flower.

1. She wore a *lilac* dress to the shower.
2. The walls are a soft *lilac*.
3. They painted the nursery *lilac* before the baby came.

---

### lilting

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɪltɪŋ/ &middot; *LIL-ting*
**Русский:** мелодичный, певучий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only — *a lilting accent*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more lilting*, *most lilting*
**Form:** -ing from *lilt*

Rising and falling in a pleasant, musical way.

1. She has a *lilting* Irish accent.
2. A *lilting* waltz played in the background.
3. He read the poem in a soft, *lilting* voice.

---

### limber

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɪmbər/ &middot; *LIM-bur*
**Русский:** гибкий, подвижный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb: *stay limber*
**Comparison:** more/most — *more limber*, *most limber*
**Contrast:** Also a verb in *limber up*, to stretch before exercise.

Able to bend and move easily.

1. Stretching every morning keeps you *limber*.
2. At seventy he's still *limber* enough to touch his toes.
3. Dancers are *more limber* than the rest of us.

---

### limited

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɪmɪtɪd/ &middot; *LIM-ih-tid*
**Русский:** ограниченный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more limited*, *most limited*
**Takes:** *to* + noun: *limited to four per customer*
**Form:** -ed from *limit*
**Opposite:** *unlimited*

Limited has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Small in amount, number or range; restricted.** (ограниченный, небольшой)

1. Seating is *limited*, so come early.
2. We have a *limited* amount of time.
3. Tickets are *limited* to four per person.

**2. Not very able; without much skill or range.** (ограниченный (о способностях))

4. He's a *limited* player with a great attitude.
5. Her English is *limited* but improving fast.

---

### limiting

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɪmɪtɪŋ/ &middot; *LIM-ih-ting*
**Русский:** ограничивающий, сдерживающий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more limiting*, *most limiting*
**Form:** -ing from *limit*
**Contrast:** An *-ing/-ed* pair: a *limiting* rule is the one that puts limits on you; a *limited* number is the small number that results. *That belief is limiting* means it holds you back.

Putting limits on what someone or something can do.

1. Water is the *limiting* factor for farming here.
2. She found the job creatively *limiting*.
3. It's a *limiting* belief, not a fact.

---

### limitless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɪmɪtləs/ &middot; *LIM-it-luhs*
**Русский:** безграничный, неограниченный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -less from *limit*
**Opposite:** *limited*

Having no end or no boundary.

1. The job offers almost *limitless* opportunities.
2. Her patience seemed *limitless*.
3. Storage space is cheap, but it is not *limitless*.

---

### limp

**Pronunciation:** /lɪmp/ &middot; *LIMP*
**Русский:** вялый, обмякший
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *limper*, *limpest*
**Opposite:** *firm*
**Contrast:** Also a verb and a noun: *he walks with a limp*.

Soft and drooping, with no stiffness or strength.

1. The lettuce had gone *limp* in the fridge.
2. He gave me a *limp* handshake.
3. The flowers looked *limper* every day.

---

### linear

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɪniər/ &middot; *LIN-ee-ur*
**Русский:** линейный; последовательный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *nonlinear*

Linear has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Going straight from start to finish, in order.** (линейный, последовательный)

1. The movie does not tell the story in a *linear* way.
2. We expected *linear* progress and got none.

**2. In math, involving a straight-line relationship.** (линейный (в математике))

3. The two variables have a *linear* relationship.
4. Solve the *linear* equation for x.

---

### lined

**Pronunciation:** /laɪnd/ &middot; *LYND*
**Русский:** на подкладке; линованный; в морщинах
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *with* + noun, in the first sense
**Form:** -ed from *line*

Lined has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Having a layer of material on the inside.** (на подкладке, утеплённый)

1. She wore a *lined* raincoat.
2. The jewelry box is *lined* with velvet.

**2. Printed with straight lines to write on.** (линованный, в линейку)

3. He writes his drafts on *lined* paper.
4. Bring a *lined* notebook to class.

**3. Marked with wrinkles.** (морщинистый, в морщинах)

5. His face was deeply *lined*.
6. She had the *lined* hands of a gardener.

---

### linguistic

**Pronunciation:** /lɪŋˈɡwɪstɪk/ &middot; *ling-GWIS-tik*
**Русский:** лингвистический, языковой
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable

Having to do with language or the study of it.

1. *Linguistic* differences made the negotiation harder.
2. She studies the *linguistic* history of the region.
3. The test measures *linguistic* ability.

---

### linked

**Pronunciation:** /lɪŋkt/ &middot; *LINGKT*
**Русский:** связанный, соединённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *to* or *with* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *link*

Joined or connected to something else.

1. The two accounts are *linked*.
2. Smoking is *linked* to heart disease.
3. *Linked* devices share the same settings.

---

### liquid

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɪkwɪd/ &middot; *LIK-wid*
**Русский:** жидкий; ликвидный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a common noun: *pour the liquid into a bowl*.

Liquid has 2 distinct senses.

**1. In the form of a liquid, not solid and not gas.** (жидкий)

1. The medicine comes in *liquid* form.
2. Add the *liquid* ingredients last.

**2. Of money or property, easy to turn into cash.** (ликвидный, легко обращаемый в деньги)

3. Most of their money is not *liquid*.
4. The fund keeps some *liquid* assets on hand.

---

### listless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɪstləs/ &middot; *LIST-luhs*
**Русский:** вялый, апатичный
**Type:** quality; also emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more listless*, *most listless*
**Form:** -less from an old noun *list* 'desire'
**Contrast:** Nothing to do with a shopping *list*.

Having no energy and no interest in doing anything.

1. The heat left everyone *listless* by mid-afternoon.
2. She spent a *listless* week on the couch after the flu.
3. He gave a *listless* answer and went back to staring out the window.

---

### lit

**Pronunciation:** /lɪt/ &middot; *LIT*
**Русский:** освещённый; зажжённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *Lit* is also the past of *light*: *she lit the candle*. The slang sense is informal and spoken.

Lit has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Burning, or with the light on.** (зажжённый, освещённый)

1. A single *lit* window showed on the third floor.
2. Keep the porch *lit* until I get home.
3. The stairway is poorly *lit*.

**2. Exciting and full of energy.** (крутой, зажигательный)

4. The party was *lit*.
5. That show gets *lit* after midnight.

---

### lite

**Pronunciation:** /laɪt/ &middot; *LYT*
**Русский:** облегчённый, лёгкий
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** an advertising respelling of *light*
**Contrast:** Used on labels and product names; everywhere else the spelling is *light*.

Low in calories, fat or content, or stripped down to a simpler version.

1. *Lite* beer has about a third fewer calories.
2. They give away a *lite* version of the software.
3. The menu has a *lite* section for smaller appetites.

---

### literal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɪtərəl/ &middot; *LIT-ur-uhl*
**Русский:** буквальный, дословный
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more literal*, *most literal*
**Contrast:** *Literal* means word for word; *literary* is about literature and *literate* means able to read.

Literal has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Following the exact words, not a free or figurative reading.** (буквальный, дословный)

1. The *literal* meaning of the phrase is quite different.
2. She gave us a *literal* translation from the Russian.
3. In the *literal* sense, he broke no rule.

**2. Actually true, not an exaggeration.** (настоящий, не фигуральный)

4. There was a *literal* inch of dust on the shelf.
5. He means it in the *literal* sense — the roof really did fall in.

---

### literary

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɪtərɛri/ &middot; *LIT-uh-rer-ee*
**Русский:** литературный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more literary*, *most literary*
**Contrast:** *Literary* is about literature; *literate* means able to read and write.

Literary has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having to do with literature and serious writing.** (литературный)

1. She writes for a *literary* magazine.
2. The novel won every major *literary* prize that year.
3. His *literary* career started with short stories.

**2. Of a word or style: formal, used in writing rather than in speech.** (книжный (о стиле))

4. *Whom* sounds *literary* in conversation.
5. The report is a little too *literary* for a news desk.

---

### literate

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɪtərət/ &middot; *LIT-ur-uht*
**Русский:** грамотный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more literate*, *most literate*
**Opposite:** *illiterate*

Literate has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Able to read and write.** (грамотный, умеющий читать и писать)

1. Only half the adult population was *literate* at the time.
2. The program aims to make every ten-year-old fully *literate*.

**2. Knowing your way around a particular subject.** (разбирающийся, подкованный)

3. Every teacher here is computer *literate*.
4. We need financially *literate* voters.

---

### lithe

**Pronunciation:** /laɪð/ &middot; *LYTH*
**Русский:** гибкий, грациозный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more lithe*, *most lithe*

Thin and moving easily and gracefully.

1. The dancer was tall and *lithe*.
2. A *lithe* cat slipped through the gap in the fence.
3. At sixty he still moves like a *lithe* young athlete.

---

### Lithuanian

**Pronunciation:** /ˌlɪθuˈeɪniən/ &middot; *lith-oo-AY-nee-uhn*
**Русский:** литовский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Always capitalized. *A Lithuanian* (a person) and *Lithuanian* (the language) are nouns.

From or having to do with Lithuania.

1. She comes from a *Lithuanian* family in Chicago.
2. The *Lithuanian* team took the bronze medal.
3. He travels on a *Lithuanian* passport.

---

### litigious

**Pronunciation:** /lɪˈtɪdʒəs/ &middot; *li-TIJ-uhs*
**Русский:** сутяжнический, склонный судиться
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more litigious*, *most litigious*
**Form:** from *litigation*

Quick to take people to court.

1. American business culture is famously *litigious*.
2. He is a *litigious* neighbor who sues over fences.
3. In such a *litigious* climate, doctors write everything down.

---

### littered

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɪtərd/ &middot; *LIT-urd*
**Русский:** усыпанный, замусоренный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more littered*, *most littered*
**Takes:** *with* + noun

Covered with things lying around — trash, or anything scattered everywhere.

1. The street was *littered* with broken glass.
2. His desk is *littered* with unopened mail.
3. The script is *littered* with typos.

---

### little-known

**Pronunciation:** /ˌlɪtəlˈnoʊn/ &middot; *lit-uhl-NOHN*
**Русский:** малоизвестный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — adverb + participle

Known by only a few people.

1. It is a *little-known* fact that he wrote poetry.
2. The museum holds several *little-known* works by Picasso.
3. The village was *little-known* outside the region.

---

### livable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɪvəbəl/ &middot; *LIV-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** пригодный для жизни; сносный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more livable*, *most livable*
**Form:** -able from *live*
**Contrast:** American spelling drops the *e*: *livable*, not the British *liveable*.

Livable has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Good enough to live in.** (пригодный для жизни)

1. They made the basement *livable* over the summer.
2. The city is ranked among the most *livable* in the country.

**2. Bearable — something you can put up with.** (терпимый, сносный)

3. The pay is low but *livable*.
4. We reached a *livable* compromise about the noise.

---

### livid

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɪvɪd/ &middot; *LIV-id*
**Русский:** взбешённый; багровый
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both — the angry sense mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more livid*, *most livid*

Livid has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Extremely angry.** (взбешённый, в ярости)

1. She was *livid* when she saw the bill.
2. He came out of the meeting *livid*.

**2. Of skin or a bruise: dark bluish-gray.** (багрово-синий)

3. A *livid* bruise ran the length of his arm.
4. Her hands were *livid* with cold.

---

### living

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɪvɪŋ/ &middot; *LIV-ing*
**Русский:** живой; живущий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — the 'alive' sense before a noun only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Before a noun use *living*, after a verb use *alive*: *the greatest living composer*, but *the fish is still alive*.

Living has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Alive now, not dead.** (живой, ныне живущий)

1. He is the greatest *living* composer.
2. No *living* creature could survive out there.

**2. Of a language or tradition: still used today.** (живой, действующий)

3. Latin is no longer a *living* language.
4. These are *living* traditions, not museum pieces.

---

### load-bearing

**Pronunciation:** /ˈloʊdˌbɛrɪŋ/ &middot; *LOHD-bair-ing*
**Русский:** несущий (о стене, конструкции)
**Type:** compound; also classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + participle

Holding up the weight of a building or structure.

1. Don't knock out a *load-bearing* wall without an engineer.
2. The two center columns are *load-bearing*.
3. A *load-bearing* frame carries the whole roof.

---

### loaded

**Pronunciation:** /ˈloʊdɪd/ &middot; *LOH-did*
**Русский:** нагруженный; заряженный; богатый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more loaded*, *most loaded*
**Takes:** *with* + noun in the 'full' sense

Loaded has 4 distinct senses.

**1. Carrying a load; full of something.** (нагруженный, гружёный)

1. The truck was *loaded* with lumber.
2. We set off with a *loaded* car and no map.

**2. Of a gun: with ammunition in it.** (заряженный)

3. He kept a *loaded* gun in the drawer.
4. Treat every weapon as if it were *loaded*.

**3. Of a question or word: carrying a hidden meaning meant to trap you.** (с подвохом, тенденциозный)

5. That's a *loaded* question and you know it.
6. The word carries a *loaded* history.

**4. Very rich.** (богатый, при деньгах)

7. Her family is *loaded*.
8. He spends like a man who is *loaded*.

---

### loath

**Pronunciation:** /loʊθ/ &middot; *LOHTH*
**Русский:** не склонный, неохотно делающий что-л.
**Type:** quality
**Position:** predicative only
**Comparison:** more/most — *more loath*, *most loath*
**Takes:** *to*-infinitive
**Contrast:** *Loath* is this adjective; *loathe* is the verb 'to hate' and ends in /ð/: *I loathe waiting*.

Unwilling to do something.

1. She was *loath* to admit she was wrong.
2. I am *loath* to spend that much on one night.
3. They seemed *loath* to give up the extra space.

---

### loathsome

**Pronunciation:** /ˈloʊðsəm/ &middot; *LOHTH-suhm*
**Русский:** отвратительный, омерзительный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more loathsome*, *most loathsome*
**Form:** -some from *loathe*

Disgusting and hateful.

1. It was a *loathsome* thing to say to a child.
2. The smell in the basement was *loathsome*.
3. He plays a *loathsome* villain in the new movie.

---

### local

**Pronunciation:** /ˈloʊkəl/ &middot; *LOH-kuhl*
**Русский:** местный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *A local* (a person who lives in the area) is a noun.

Local has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Belonging to the area right around you.** (местный, здешний)

1. We shop at the *local* farmers' market.
2. The story made the *local* news.
3. Call your *local* library and ask.

**2. Affecting only one part of the body.** (местный (о наркозе, боли))

4. The dentist used a *local* anesthetic.
5. The swelling stayed *local*.

---

### localized

**Pronunciation:** /ˈloʊkəlaɪzd/ &middot; *LOH-kuh-lyzd*
**Русский:** локализованный, ограниченный одним местом
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more localized*, *most localized*
**Contrast:** American spelling is *-ized*; the British form is *localised*.

Localized has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Limited to one small area.** (локализованный, местный)

1. The damage was *localized* to the north wing.
2. *Localized* flooding closed two streets.
3. The infection stayed *localized*.

**2. Of software or a product: adapted for another language and market.** (локализованный (о программе))

4. They released a *localized* version for Japan.
5. The menus are fully *localized*, the help files are not.

---

### located

**Pronunciation:** /ˈloʊkeɪtɪd/ &middot; *LOH-kay-tid*
**Русский:** расположенный, находящийся
**Type:** participial
**Position:** predicative only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *in*, *on*, *at* or *near* + place
**Contrast:** *Located* can usually be dropped: *the hotel is on the beach* says the same thing.

Standing or built in a particular place.

1. The hotel is *located* right on the beach.
2. Their offices are *located* in three countries.
3. Conveniently *located* near the airport, the motel fills up by six.

---

### loco

**Pronunciation:** /ˈloʊkoʊ/ &middot; *LOH-koh*
**Русский:** чокнутый, псих
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** predicative only
**Comparison:** more/most — *more loco*, *most loco*
**Form:** from Spanish *loco* 'crazy'
**Contrast:** Informal and joking; in writing use *crazy*.

Crazy.

1. That guy is *loco*.
2. You'd have to be *loco* to swim out there.
3. After a week without sleep I was going *loco*.

---

### lofty

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɔːfti/ &middot; *LAWF-tee*
**Русский:** высокий; возвышенный; надменный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *loftier*, *loftiest*

Lofty has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Very high and impressive.** (высокий, величественный)

1. *Lofty* pines surrounded the cabin.
2. The hall has a *lofty* ceiling and terrible acoustics.

**2. Of aims or ideas: noble and ambitious.** (возвышенный, амбициозный)

3. They set *loftier* goals for the second year.
4. It is a *lofty* ambition for a college this size.

**3. Proud, as if better than everyone else.** (надменный, высокомерный)

5. He answered in a *lofty* tone.
6. She has a *lofty* way of dismissing other people's work.

---

### logarithmic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌlɔːɡəˈrɪðmɪk/ &middot; *law-guh-RITH-mik*
**Русский:** логарифмический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable

Using or described by logarithms.

1. The chart uses a *logarithmic* scale.
2. Plot it on a *logarithmic* axis so the small values show.
3. The curve is *logarithmic*, not linear.

---

### logical

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɑːdʒɪkəl/ &middot; *LAH-ji-kuhl*
**Русский:** логичный; логический
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more logical*, *most logical*
**Opposite:** *illogical*

Logical has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Following sensibly from what came before.** (логичный, закономерный)

1. Buying the building was the *logical* next step.
2. Her argument is perfectly *logical*.
3. There is a *logical* explanation for the noise.

**2. Thinking clearly and reasonably.** (рассудительный, мыслящий логически)

4. He is a *logical* thinker, which helps in a crisis.
5. Try to be *logical* about this instead of panicking.

---

### logistical

**Pronunciation:** /ləˈdʒɪstɪkəl/ &middot; *luh-JIS-ti-kuhl*
**Русский:** логистический, организационный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable

Having to do with organizing people, supplies and timing.

1. The tour ran into *logistical* problems in the second week.
2. Feeding ten thousand people is a *logistical* nightmare.
3. The reasons for the delay were *logistical*, not financial.

---

### lonesome

**Pronunciation:** /ˈloʊnsəm/ &middot; *LOHN-suhm*
**Русский:** одинокий, тоскливый
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more lonesome*, *most lonesome*
**Form:** -some from *lone*
**Contrast:** American and warmer in tone than *lonely*; *on your lonesome* means 'on your own'.

Sad from being alone, or making you feel that way.

1. He gets *lonesome* out on the ranch in winter.
2. It is a *lonesome* stretch of highway after dark.
3. She sang a *lonesome* old song about leaving home.

---

### long

**Pronunciation:** /lɔːŋ/ &middot; *LAWNG*
**Русский:** длинный; долгий
**Type:** size; also age
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *longer*, *longest*
**Opposite:** *short*
**Contrast:** *Long* is also an adverb: *don't stay long*. For distance use *far*, not *long*.

Long has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Measuring a lot from one end to the other.** (длинный)

1. She has *long* dark hair.
2. The bridge is two miles *long*.
3. Take the *longer* route along the river.

**2. Lasting a lot of time.** (долгий, продолжительный)

4. It was a *long* winter.
5. We had a *long* talk about the money.
6. That was the *longest* meeting of my life.

---

### long-haired

**Pronunciation:** /ˌlɔːŋˈhɛrd/ &middot; *lawng-HAIRD*
**Русский:** длинноволосый; длинношёрстный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — adjective + noun + *-ed*

With long hair or long fur.

1. A *long-haired* kid answered the door.
2. *Long-haired* cats need daily brushing.
3. Their dog is *long-haired* and sheds all over the couch.

---

### long-lasting

**Pronunciation:** /ˌlɔːŋˈlæstɪŋ/ &middot; *lawng-LAS-ting*
**Русский:** долговременный, стойкий
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more long-lasting*, *most long-lasting*
**Form:** compound — adjective + participle

Continuing or working for a long time.

1. The treaty brought a *long-lasting* peace to the region.
2. This paint is more *long-lasting* than the cheap brand.
3. The effects of the drought were *long-lasting*.

---

### long-lived

**Pronunciation:** /ˌlɔːŋˈlaɪvd/ &middot; *lawng-LYVD*
**Русский:** долгоживущий, долговечный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more long-lived*, *most long-lived*
**Contrast:** Americans usually say it with the vowel of *life*, not of *live*.

Living or lasting for many years.

1. Parrots are surprisingly *long-lived*.
2. It was the most *long-lived* show on the network.
3. *Long-lived* batteries cost more up front.

---

### long-range

**Pronunciation:** /ˌlɔːŋˈreɪndʒ/ &middot; *lawng-RAYNJ*
**Русский:** дальнего действия; долгосрочный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Long-range has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Able to travel or reach a long way.** (дальнего действия, дальнобойный)

1. *Long-range* missiles were moved to the border.
2. The plane carries *long-range* fuel tanks.

**2. Looking far into the future.** (долгосрочный, перспективный)

3. The *long-range* forecast calls for rain all week.
4. We need a *long-range* plan, not another quick fix.

---

### long-suffering

**Pronunciation:** /ˌlɔːŋˈsʌfərɪŋ/ &middot; *lawng-SUHF-ur-ing*
**Русский:** многострадальный, долготерпеливый
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more long-suffering*, *most long-suffering*
**Form:** compound — adverb + participle

Putting up with trouble or annoyance patiently for a long time.

1. His *long-suffering* wife drove him to every game.
2. *Long-suffering* fans finally got a winning season.
3. She has been remarkably *long-suffering* about the noise upstairs.

---

### long-winded

**Pronunciation:** /ˌlɔːŋˈwɪndɪd/ &middot; *lawng-WIN-did*
**Русский:** многословный, нудный
**Type:** compound; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more long-winded*, *most long-winded*
**Opposite:** *concise*

Going on far too long in speech or writing.

1. He gave a *long-winded* explanation nobody asked for.
2. The introduction is *long-winded*.
3. Her emails get more *long-winded* every year.

---

### longitudinal

**Pronunciation:** /ˌlɑːndʒəˈtuːdənəl/ &middot; *lahn-juh-TOO-duh-nuhl*
**Русский:** продольный; долговременный (об исследовании)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable

Longitudinal has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Running lengthwise, from end to end.** (продольный)

1. The crack runs in a *longitudinal* direction.
2. *Longitudinal* beams support the deck.

**2. Of research: following the same people over many years.** (лонгитюдный, длительный)

3. A *longitudinal* study tracked the children into their forties.
4. The data are *longitudinal*, not a single snapshot.

---

### longstanding

**Pronunciation:** /ˌlɔːŋˈstændɪŋ/ &middot; *lawng-STAN-ding*
**Русский:** давний, давнишний
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more longstanding*, *most longstanding*
**Contrast:** Written as one word or as *long-standing*; both are accepted.

Existing for a long time.

1. They have a *longstanding* agreement about the driveway.
2. This is a *longstanding* problem in the industry.
3. Their friendship is *longstanding* and survived the move.

---

### longtime

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɔːŋtaɪm/ &middot; *LAWNG-tym*
**Русский:** давний, многолетний
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Before a noun only: *a longtime customer*, but *a customer for years*.

Having been that for many years.

1. She is a *longtime* friend of the family.
2. He was the company's *longtime* lawyer.
3. *Longtime* residents still talk about the flood.

---

### loony

**Pronunciation:** /ˈluːni/ &middot; *LOO-nee*
**Русский:** чокнутый, безумный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *loonier*, *looniest*
**Form:** shortened from *lunatic*
**Contrast:** Informal and joking; it is rude about real mental illness.

Crazy or very strange.

1. It sounded like a *loony* idea at first.
2. The plan gets *loonier* every time he explains it.
3. Some *loony* guy was shouting at the traffic.

---

### loopy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈluːpi/ &middot; *LOO-pee*
**Русский:** чокнутый; одурманенный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *loopier*, *loopiest*

Loopy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Silly or slightly crazy.** (чудаковатый, чокнутый)

1. She has a *loopy* sense of humor.
2. The whole idea is *loopy*.

**2. Dazed, usually from medicine or lack of sleep.** (одурманенный, не в себе)

3. The painkillers made me *loopy*.
4. By midnight we were all a little *loopier* than we should have been.

---

### loose

**Pronunciation:** /luːs/ &middot; *LOOS*
**Русский:** свободный; незакреплённый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *looser*, *loosest*
**Opposite:** *tight*
**Contrast:** *Loose* is the adjective, said with /s/; *lose* is the verb, said with /z/: *don't lose the keys*.

Loose has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Not tight, or not firmly fixed in place.** (свободный; шатающийся, незакреплённый)

1. One of the handles is *loose*.
2. These jeans are a little *loose* on me.
3. The knot turned out to be *looser* than it looked.

**2. Free, not tied up or shut in.** (выпущенный на волю, непривязанный)

4. The dog got *loose* in the park.
5. Don't leave papers *loose* in your bag.

**3. Not exact or strict.** (вольный, приблизительный)

6. That's a *loose* translation of the title.
7. We had a *loose* agreement, nothing in writing.

---

### loose-fitting

**Pronunciation:** /ˌluːsˈfɪtɪŋ/ &middot; *loos-FIT-ing*
**Русский:** свободный, не облегающий
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more loose-fitting*, *most loose-fitting*
**Form:** compound — adjective + participle
**Opposite:** *tight-fitting*

Of clothes: cut wide, not close to the body.

1. Wear *loose-fitting* clothes to the class.
2. A *loose-fitting* shirt is cooler in this heat.
3. The jacket is deliberately *loose-fitting*.

---

### lopsided

**Pronunciation:** /ˌlɑːpˈsaɪdɪd/ &middot; *lahp-SY-did*
**Русский:** кривой, перекошенный; неравный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more lopsided*, *most lopsided*

Lopsided has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Lower or heavier on one side; crooked.** (кривобокий, перекошенный)

1. The cake came out *lopsided* but tasted fine.
2. He gave a *lopsided* grin.

**2. Very uneven between two sides.** (неравный, с большим перевесом)

3. It was a *lopsided* game — 42 to 7.
4. The deal looks *lopsided* in their favor.

---

### loquacious

**Pronunciation:** /loʊˈkweɪʃəs/ &middot; *loh-KWAY-shuhs*
**Русский:** болтливый, словоохотливый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more loquacious*, *most loquacious*
**Opposite:** *taciturn*

Talking a great deal.

1. Our driver was *loquacious* the entire trip.
2. One glass of wine makes him *loquacious*.
3. She is the most *loquacious* person on the team.

---

### lossless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɔːsləs/ &middot; *LAWS-luhs*
**Русский:** без потерь (о сжатии данных)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -less from *loss*
**Opposite:** *lossy*

Of compression: keeping every bit of the original data.

1. FLAC is a *lossless* audio format.
2. Use *lossless* compression for the master files.
3. The conversion is *lossless*, so nothing is thrown away.

---

### lost

**Pronunciation:** /lɔːst/ &middot; *LAWST*
**Русский:** потерянный; заблудившийся
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *Lost* is also the past of *lose*: *we lost the game*.

Lost has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Not able to find the way.** (заблудившийся)

1. We got *lost* twice on the way here.
2. A *lost* dog was wandering the parking lot.

**2. Missing; nowhere to be found.** (потерянный, утраченный)

3. The keys are *lost* somewhere in the house.
4. *Lost* luggage can be claimed at the desk.

**3. Not understanding what is going on.** (растерянный, ничего не понимающий)

5. After the second chapter I was completely *lost*.
6. He looked *lost* through the whole presentation.

---

### loud

**Pronunciation:** /laʊd/ &middot; *LOWD*
**Русский:** громкий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *louder*, *loudest*
**Opposite:** *quiet*

Loud has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Making a lot of noise.** (громкий, шумный)

1. The music was too *loud* to talk over.
2. The TV is *louder* than it needs to be.
3. Theirs is the *loudest* table in the restaurant.

**2. Of colors or patterns: too bright and showy.** (кричащий, броский)

4. He wore a *loud* Hawaiian shirt to the wedding.
5. The wallpaper is a little *loud* for my taste.

---

### lousy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlaʊzi/ &middot; *LOW-zee*
**Русский:** паршивый, отвратительный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *lousier*, *lousiest*
**Form:** from *louse*
**Contrast:** Informal but very common in speech.

Lousy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Very bad or of poor quality.** (паршивый, никудышный)

1. We had *lousy* weather all week.
2. He is a *lousy* driver and he knows it.
3. The pay was even *lousier* than I expected.

**2. Full of something, in *lousy with*.** (кишащий чем-л.)

4. In July the town is *lousy* with tourists.
5. That neighborhood is *lousy* with coffee shops.

---

### lovable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlʌvəbəl/ &middot; *LUHV-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** милый, располагающий к себе
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more lovable*, *most lovable*
**Form:** -able from *love*
**Contrast:** American spelling drops the *e*: *lovable*, not *loveable*.

Easy to love, even with the faults.

1. He plays a *lovable* grump on the show.
2. Their puppy is completely *lovable*.
3. She has a *lovable* way of getting everything slightly wrong.

---

### loveless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlʌvləs/ &middot; *LUHV-luhs*
**Русский:** без любви, нелюбящий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -less from *love*

Without love in it.

1. They stayed in a *loveless* marriage for years.
2. It was a cold, *loveless* household.
3. She writes about a *loveless* childhood in the suburbs.

---

### lovesick

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlʌvsɪk/ &middot; *LUHV-sik*
**Русский:** томящийся от любви
**Type:** emotion; also compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more lovesick*, *most lovesick*
**Form:** compound — noun + adjective

Miserable because of love for someone.

1. He has been *lovesick* since she left for school.
2. A *lovesick* teenager wrote it in the back of a notebook.
3. They wander around the house *lovesick* and useless.

---

### loving

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlʌvɪŋ/ &middot; *LUHV-ing*
**Русский:** любящий, нежный
**Type:** participial; also emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more loving*, *most loving*

Showing love and care.

1. She grew up in a *loving* family.
2. He gave her a *loving* look across the table.
3. The old car has been restored with *loving* care.

---

### low

**Pronunciation:** /loʊ/ &middot; *LOH*
**Русский:** низкий
**Type:** size
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *lower*, *lowest*
**Opposite:** *high*
**Contrast:** *Low* is also an adverb (*the plane flew low*). *Lowly* is a separate adjective meaning 'humble'.

Low has 4 distinct senses.

**1. Not high; close to the ground.** (низкий, невысокий)

1. The clouds were *low* over the water.
2. Put the box on the *lowest* shelf.

**2. Small in amount, number or level.** (низкий (о количестве, уровне))

3. Gas prices are unusually *low* this month.
4. Keep the heat *low* for twenty minutes.
5. Turnout was even *lower* than last year.

**3. Of a sound: quiet, or deep in pitch.** (тихий; низкий (о звуке))

6. She answered in a *low* voice.
7. The engine made a *low* hum.

**4. Sad and without energy.** (подавленный, в унынии)

8. I have been feeling *low* all week.
9. His spirits were *low* after the news.

---

### low-budget

**Pronunciation:** /ˌloʊˈbʌdʒɪt/ &middot; *loh-BUHJ-it*
**Русский:** малобюджетный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable

Made or done with very little money.

1. It started as a *low-budget* horror movie.
2. We took a *low-budget* trip across the country.
3. The whole production looks *low-budget*.

---

### low-cal

**Pronunciation:** /ˌloʊˈkæl/ &middot; *loh-KAL*
**Русский:** низкокалорийный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** shortened from *low-calorie*

Containing few calories.

1. She ordered the *low-cal* dressing on the side.
2. They added a *low-cal* version of the soda.
3. The dessert is surprisingly *low-cal*.

---

### low-class

**Pronunciation:** /ˌloʊˈklæs/ &middot; *loh-KLAS*
**Русский:** низкопробный, вульгарный
**Type:** compound; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Insulting when said about people; for things the neutral word is *low-quality*.

Cheap, vulgar or badly mannered.

1. He called the whole stunt *low-class*.
2. It is a *low-class* joke that plays badly in a room like this.
3. Don't be *low-class* about it — just say thank you.

---

### low-cost

**Pronunciation:** /ˌloʊˈkɔːst/ &middot; *loh-KAWST*
**Русский:** недорогой, дешёвый
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Costing little to buy or to run.

1. They fly out of the *low-cost* terminal.
2. The city built *low-cost* housing downtown.
3. A *low-cost* option is better than none at all.

---

### low-cut

**Pronunciation:** /ˌloʊˈkʌt/ &middot; *loh-KUHT*
**Русский:** с глубоким вырезом; с низким верхом
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable

Low-cut has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of clothing: cut low at the neck.** (с глубоким вырезом)

1. She wore a *low-cut* black dress.
2. The neckline is too *low-cut* for the office.

**2. Of shoes: cut below the ankle.** (с низким верхом (об обуви))

3. *Low-cut* sneakers are back in style.
4. He prefers *low-cut* hiking shoes in the summer.

---

### low-grade

**Pronunciation:** /ˌloʊˈɡreɪd/ &middot; *loh-GRAYD*
**Русский:** низкосортный; слабый, но затяжной
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Low-grade has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of poor quality.** (низкосортный, низкокачественный)

1. The shelves are made of *low-grade* plywood.
2. They were selling *low-grade* gasoline at a discount.

**2. Mild but continuing — of a fever, pain or worry.** (слабый, но постоянный)

3. She has had a *low-grade* fever for two days.
4. There is a *low-grade* anxiety running through the whole office.

---

### low-interest

**Pronunciation:** /ˌloʊˈɪntrəst/ &middot; *loh-IN-truhst*
**Русский:** под низкий процент, льготный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Charging only a small rate of interest.

1. The bank offers *low-interest* loans to students.
2. They refinanced with a *low-interest* mortgage.
3. *Low-interest* credit cards are hard to find now.

---

### low-key

**Pronunciation:** /ˌloʊˈkiː/ &middot; *loh-KEE*
**Русский:** сдержанный, без лишнего шума
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more low-key*, *most low-key*
**Contrast:** In speech it is also used as an adverb: *I'm low-key nervous*.

Quiet and not showy.

1. They had a *low-key* wedding with ten guests.
2. He keeps his part in it very *low-key*.
3. The store's opening was deliberately *low-key*.

---

### low-level

**Pronunciation:** /ˌloʊˈlɛvəl/ &middot; *loh-LEV-uhl*
**Русский:** низкого уровня; низший
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Low-level has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Junior in rank or importance.** (низшего звена, рядовой)

1. Only *low-level* employees lost their jobs.
2. It was a *low-level* meeting with no decisions on the table.

**2. Small in strength or amount, and going on for a long time.** (слабый, фоновый)

3. *Low-level* radiation was detected near the plant.
4. He lives with *low-level* pain most days.

---

### low-lying

**Pronunciation:** /ˌloʊˈlaɪɪŋ/ &middot; *loh-LY-ing*
**Русский:** низменный, низко расположенный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — adjective + participle

Of land: close to sea level.

1. *Low-lying* areas flood first.
2. The farm sits on *low-lying* ground near the river.
3. Much of the country is *low-lying* and at risk.

---

### low-pitched

**Pronunciation:** /ˌloʊˈpɪtʃt/ &middot; *loh-PICHT*
**Русский:** низкий (о звуке); пологий (о крыше)
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more low-pitched*, *most low-pitched*
**Opposite:** *high-pitched*

Low-pitched has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of a sound or voice: deep, not high.** (низкий (о голосе, звуке))

1. He has a *low-pitched* voice that carries.
2. A *low-pitched* hum came from the basement.

**2. Of a roof: sloping gently.** (пологий (о крыше))

3. The house has a *low-pitched* roof.
4. *Low-pitched* roofs hold snow longer.

---

### low-pressure

**Pronunciation:** /ˌloʊˈprɛʃər/ &middot; *loh-PRESH-ur*
**Русский:** спокойный, без давления; низкого давления
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable

Low-pressure has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Relaxed, with nobody pushing you.** (спокойный, ненапряжный)

1. It is a *low-pressure* job with steady hours.
2. We wanted a *low-pressure* way to meet people.

**2. Of air, steam or water: at low pressure.** (низкого давления)

3. A *low-pressure* system is moving in from the west.
4. *Low-pressure* steam heats the whole building.

---

### low-priced

**Pronunciation:** /ˌloʊˈpraɪst/ &middot; *loh-PRYST*
**Русский:** дешёвый, по низкой цене
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable

Sold cheaply.

1. They sell *low-priced* furniture that lasts about a year.
2. *Low-priced* seats sell out first.
3. The tickets are *low-priced* for a show like this.

---

### low-rise

**Pronunciation:** /ˌloʊˈraɪz/ &middot; *loh-RYZ*
**Русский:** малоэтажный; с низкой посадкой
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *high-rise*

Low-rise has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of a building: with only a few floors.** (малоэтажный)

1. The block is all *low-rise* apartments.
2. They approved a *low-rise* development by the park.

**2. Of pants: cut to sit below the waist.** (с низкой посадкой (о брюках))

3. *Low-rise* jeans came back into style.
4. She never liked *low-rise* pants.

---

### low-tech

**Pronunciation:** /ˌloʊˈtɛk/ &middot; *loh-TEK*
**Русский:** технически простой, несложный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *high-tech*

Using simple equipment instead of advanced technology.

1. The fix was gloriously *low-tech*: duct tape.
2. They run a *low-tech* classroom with paper and pencils.
3. Our whole setup is pretty *low-tech*.

---

### lowbrow

**Pronunciation:** /ˈloʊbraʊ/ &middot; *LOH-brow*
**Русский:** незамысловатый, примитивный
**Type:** evaluative; also compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more lowbrow*, *most lowbrow*
**Opposite:** *highbrow*

Aimed at simple tastes rather than at educated ones.

1. He likes *lowbrow* comedies and says so proudly.
2. The magazine is cheerfully *lowbrow*.
3. It is a *lowbrow* joke, but it works every time.

---

### lower-class

**Pronunciation:** /ˌloʊərˈklæs/ &middot; *loh-ur-KLAS*
**Русский:** принадлежащий к низшему классу
**Type:** compound; also classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** It can sound dismissive; *working-class* is the usual neutral term.

Belonging to the poorest social class.

1. The novel follows a *lower-class* family in Chicago.
2. *Lower-class* neighborhoods got the least funding.
3. His background is *lower-class* and he never hides it.

---

### lower-middle-class

**Pronunciation:** /ˌloʊərˌmɪdəlˈklæs/ &middot; *loh-ur-mid-uhl-KLAS*
**Русский:** из низов среднего класса
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable

Belonging to the lower part of the middle class.

1. They grew up in a *lower-middle-class* suburb.
2. The survey targeted *lower-middle-class* households.
3. His family was solidly *lower-middle-class*.

---

### lowercase

**Pronunciation:** /ˌloʊərˈkeɪs/ &middot; *loh-ur-KAYS*
**Русский:** строчный (о буквах)
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *uppercase*
**Contrast:** *Lowercase* letters are the small ones; the capitals are *uppercase*.

Of letters: small, not capital.

1. Passwords must mix *lowercase* and capital letters.
2. The logo is all *lowercase*.
3. He writes his name in *lowercase* letters.

---

### lowland

**Pronunciation:** /ˈloʊlənd/ &middot; *LOH-luhnd*
**Русский:** низинный, равнинный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *The lowlands* is a noun; before another noun the same word works as an adjective.

Of or living in low, flat country.

1. *Lowland* gorillas live in the forests of central Africa.
2. The *lowland* farms flood every spring.
3. The *lowland* route is easier in winter.

---

### lowly

**Pronunciation:** /ˈloʊli/ &middot; *LOH-lee*
**Русский:** скромный, незначительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** more/most — *more lowly*, *most lowly*
**Form:** -ly adjective, from *low*
**Contrast:** An *-ly* adjective, not an adverb: *a lowly clerk*, never *he works lowly*.

Low in rank or importance.

1. He started as a *lowly* intern and stayed nine years.
2. Even the *lowly* potato has its defenders.
3. She rose from a *lowly* job in the mailroom to run the place.

---

### lubricated

**Pronunciation:** /ˈluːbrɪkeɪtɪd/ &middot; *LOO-bri-kay-tid*
**Русский:** смазанный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable

Covered with oil or grease so the parts move easily.

1. Keep the chain clean and *lubricated*.
2. A well-*lubricated* engine runs quieter.
3. The bearings come *lubricated* from the factory.

---

### lucid

**Pronunciation:** /ˈluːsɪd/ &middot; *LOO-sid*
**Русский:** ясный, понятный; в ясном сознании
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more lucid*, *most lucid*
**Contrast:** A *lucid* dream is one you know you are having while it goes on.

Lucid has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Clear and easy to follow.** (ясный, вразумительный)

1. She gave a *lucid* explanation of the tax change.
2. His writing is *lucid* and free of jargon.

**2. Thinking clearly, especially after illness or confusion.** (в ясном сознании)

3. He was *lucid* for a few hours in the afternoon.
4. She had *lucid* moments right up to the end.

---

### lucrative

**Pronunciation:** /ˈluːkrətɪv/ &middot; *LOO-kruh-tiv*
**Русский:** прибыльный, доходный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more lucrative*, *most lucrative*

Bringing in a lot of money.

1. Consulting turned out to be more *lucrative* than teaching.
2. They signed a *lucrative* contract with the network.
3. The side business is quietly *lucrative*.

---

### ludicrous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈluːdəkrəs/ &middot; *LOO-duh-kruhs*
**Русский:** смехотворный, нелепый
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more ludicrous*, *most ludicrous*

So silly or unreasonable that it deserves to be laughed at.

1. The price they are asking is *ludicrous*.
2. It was a *ludicrous* excuse and nobody believed it.
3. The idea sounded *ludicrous* until it worked.

---

### lukewarm

**Pronunciation:** /ˌluːkˈwɔːrm/ &middot; *look-WORM*
**Русский:** тепловатый; прохладный (о приёме)
**Type:** compound; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more lukewarm*, *most lukewarm*

Lukewarm has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Only slightly warm.** (тепловатый, чуть тёплый)

1. The coffee was *lukewarm* by the time he got to it.
2. Wash the sweater in *lukewarm* water.

**2. Not at all enthusiastic.** (прохладный, сдержанный)

3. The response from investors was *lukewarm*.
4. She gave the plan a *lukewarm* endorsement.

---

### lumbering

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlʌmbərɪŋ/ &middot; *LUHM-bur-ing*
**Русский:** неуклюжий, тяжеловесный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Nothing to do with the *lumber* business — this is the verb *lumber*, to move heavily.

Moving slowly and heavily.

1. A *lumbering* bear crossed the road ahead of us.
2. The *lumbering* old bus took an hour to climb the hill.
3. He has a *lumbering* walk you can hear from downstairs.

---

### luminous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈluːmənəs/ &middot; *LOO-muh-nuhs*
**Русский:** светящийся, сияющий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more luminous*, *most luminous*

Luminous has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Giving off light, especially glowing in the dark.** (светящийся)

1. The watch has *luminous* hands.
2. *Luminous* paint marks the exit.

**2. Bright and glowing in appearance.** (сияющий, лучезарный)

3. Her skin looked *luminous* in the photographs.
4. The painting is *luminous* even in a dim room.

---

### lumpy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlʌmpi/ &middot; *LUHM-pee*
**Русский:** комковатый; бугристый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *lumpier*, *lumpiest*

Full of lumps; not smooth.

1. The gravy came out *lumpy*.
2. We slept on a *lumpy* mattress in the back room.
3. The sauce gets *lumpier* as it cools.

---

### lunar

**Pronunciation:** /ˈluːnər/ &middot; *LOO-nur*
**Русский:** лунный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Of or having to do with the moon.

1. The *lunar* surface is covered in fine dust.
2. *Lunar* New Year falls in late January this year.
3. They are planning a *lunar* landing for the end of the decade.

---

### lunatic

**Pronunciation:** /ˈluːnətɪk/ &middot; *LOO-nuh-tik*
**Русский:** безумный, сумасбродный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *A lunatic* is a noun, and an offensive word for a mentally ill person; the adjective is used mostly about plans and behavior.

Wildly foolish or crazy.

1. It was a *lunatic* idea from the start.
2. He drives at *lunatic* speed on that road.
3. The *lunatic* fringe of the party got all the headlines.

---

### lurid

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlʊrɪd/ &middot; *LUUR-id*
**Русский:** шокирующий, сенсационный; кричаще яркий
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more lurid*, *most lurid*

Lurid has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Shocking, full of violent or sexual detail.** (шокирующий, смакующий подробности)

1. The tabloids printed every *lurid* detail.
2. He told a *lurid* story about his last job.

**2. Unpleasantly bright in color.** (кричащий, ядовито-яркий)

3. The walls were a *lurid* green.
4. *Lurid* neon lit the whole street.

---

### luscious

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlʌʃəs/ &middot; *LUHSH-uhs*
**Русский:** сочный, аппетитный; роскошный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more luscious*, *most luscious*

Luscious has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Delicious and juicy.** (сочный, аппетитный)

1. The peaches at the market were *luscious*.
2. They served a *luscious* chocolate cake.

**2. Rich and pleasing to look at or listen to.** (роскошный, насыщенный)

3. She has *luscious* dark hair.
4. The strings give the song a *luscious* sound.

---

### lush

**Pronunciation:** /lʌʃ/ &middot; *LUHSH*
**Русский:** пышный, густой; роскошный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *lusher*, *lushest*
**Contrast:** *A lush* is also slang for a heavy drinker — that one is a noun.

Lush has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Growing thickly and green.** (пышный, буйный (о растительности))

1. The valley is *lush* after the spring rains.
2. The grass is *lusher* on the shady side of the house.

**2. Rich, soft and luxurious.** (роскошный, богатый)

3. The lobby is all *lush* carpet and marble.
4. The score has a *lush* orchestral sound.

---

### lustful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlʌstfəl/ &middot; *LUHST-fuhl*
**Русский:** похотливый
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more lustful*, *most lustful*
**Form:** -ful from *lust*

Full of strong sexual desire.

1. He gave her a *lustful* look across the room.
2. The poem is frankly *lustful*.
3. *Lustful* thoughts take up half the sermon.

---

### lustrous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlʌstrəs/ &middot; *LUHS-truhs*
**Русский:** блестящий, глянцевый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more lustrous*, *most lustrous*

Shining softly; glossy.

1. Her hair was thick and *lustrous*.
2. The table has a *lustrous* finish.
3. *Lustrous* black feathers cover the bird's neck.

---

### Lutheran

**Pronunciation:** /ˈluːθərən/ &middot; *LOO-thur-uhn*
**Русский:** лютеранский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** from the name *Luther*
**Contrast:** Always capitalized. *A Lutheran* (a member of the church) is a noun.

Belonging to the Protestant church founded on Martin Luther's teaching.

1. They were married in a *Lutheran* church.
2. The town has a large *Lutheran* community.
3. Her family is *Lutheran* on both sides.

---

### luxuriant

**Pronunciation:** /lʌɡˈʒʊriənt/ &middot; *luhg-ZHUUR-ee-uhnt*
**Русский:** пышный, буйный (о растительности, волосах)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more luxuriant*, *most luxuriant*
**Contrast:** *Luxuriant* means growing thickly (*luxuriant hair*); *luxurious* means expensive and comfortable (*a luxurious hotel*).

Growing thickly and healthily.

1. *Luxuriant* ferns covered the forest floor.
2. He has grown a *luxuriant* beard since I saw him.
3. The garden is *luxuriant* by the middle of June.

---

### luxurious

**Pronunciation:** /lʌɡˈʒʊriəs/ &middot; *luhg-ZHUUR-ee-uhs*
**Русский:** роскошный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more luxurious*, *most luxurious*
**Form:** from *luxury*
**Contrast:** Not *luxuriant*, which is about thick growth.

Very comfortable and expensive.

1. They stayed in a *luxurious* suite overlooking the bay.
2. The car's interior is *luxurious*.
3. It felt *luxurious* to sleep until noon.

---

### lyrical

**Pronunciation:** /ˈlɪrɪkəl/ &middot; *LEER-i-kuhl*
**Русский:** лирический, поэтичный
**Type:** quality; also classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more lyrical*, *most lyrical*
**Contrast:** *Wax lyrical about something* means to talk about it with great enthusiasm.

Lyrical has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Expressing feeling in a beautiful, poetic way.** (лирический, поэтичный)

1. The film has a *lyrical* quality in the quiet scenes.
2. Her prose gets *lyrical* whenever she writes about the sea.

**2. Having to do with song words.** (относящийся к тексту песни)

3. The album is a *lyrical* achievement even if the singing is rough.
4. His *lyrical* gifts are better than his voice.

---

### macabre

**Pronunciation:** /məˈkɑːbrə/ &middot; *muh-KAH-bruh*
**Русский:** жуткий, мрачный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more macabre*, *most macabre*

Disturbing because it deals with death and horror.

1. The show has a *macabre* sense of humor.
2. It was a *macabre* detail to put in an obituary.
3. The whole scene was faintly *macabre*.

---

### Macedonian

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmæsəˈdoʊniən/ &middot; *mas-uh-DOH-nee-uhn*
**Русский:** македонский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Always capitalized. *A Macedonian* (a person) and *Macedonian* (the language) are nouns.

From or having to do with North Macedonia.

1. She travels on a *Macedonian* passport.
2. *Macedonian* wine is getting attention abroad.
3. The recipe is *Macedonian* in origin.

---

### Machiavellian

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmækiəˈvɛliən/ &middot; *mak-ee-uh-VEL-ee-uhn*
**Русский:** макиавеллистский, коварный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more Machiavellian*, *most Machiavellian*
**Form:** from the name *Machiavelli*
**Contrast:** Capitalized, because it comes from a person's name.

Clever and completely unscrupulous, especially in politics.

1. The plot is *Machiavellian* in its detail.
2. He has a *Machiavellian* talent for office politics.
3. Their strategy was more *Machiavellian* than anyone guessed.

---

### macho

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɑːtʃoʊ/ &middot; *MAH-choh*
**Русский:** подчёркнуто мужественный, мачо
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more macho*, *most macho*
**Form:** from Spanish *macho* 'male'

Showing off a tough, aggressive kind of masculinity.

1. He puts on a *macho* act around his friends.
2. It is a *macho* culture where nobody admits to being tired.
3. Don't be so *macho* — ask for help.

---

### macroeconomic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmækroʊˌɛkəˈnɑːmɪk/ &middot; *mak-roh-ek-uh-NAH-mik*
**Русский:** макроэкономический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** macro- + *economic*
**Opposite:** *microeconomic*

Having to do with a whole economy rather than with single firms.

1. *Macroeconomic* conditions are improving slowly.
2. The report looks at *macroeconomic* policy since 2008.
3. They blamed *macroeconomic* factors beyond their control.

---

### madcap

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmædkæp/ &middot; *MAD-kap*
**Русский:** сумасбродный, безрассудный
**Type:** evaluative; also compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Wildly funny and reckless.

1. It is a *madcap* comedy about three brothers and a boat.
2. They came up with a *madcap* scheme to drive to Alaska.
3. The show's *madcap* energy wears you out by the end.

---

### maddening

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmædənɪŋ/ &middot; *MAD-uh-ning*
**Русский:** доводящий до бешенства, раздражающий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more maddening*, *most maddening*
**Form:** -ing — what causes the feeling, from *madden*
**Contrast:** *Maddening* describes the thing; the person it happens to is *mad*.

Extremely annoying.

1. The dripping faucet is *maddening* at night.
2. She has a *maddening* habit of finishing your sentences.
3. It is *maddening* to wait this long for an answer.

---

### made-to-order

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmeɪd tə ˈɔːrdər/ &middot; *mayd-tuh-OR-dur*
**Русский:** сделанный на заказ
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — after a verb it is usually written without hyphens: *the suit was made to order*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — participle + prepositional phrase
**Opposite:** *ready-made*

Made specially for one customer instead of in standard sizes.

1. He wears *made-to-order* shirts from a little shop downtown.
2. The cabinets are *made-to-order*, so delivery takes six weeks.
3. A *made-to-order* sofa costs twice as much as one off the floor.

---

### made-up

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmeɪd ˈʌp/ &middot; *mayd-UHP*
**Русский:** выдуманный; накрашенный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — from the phrasal verb *make up*
**Contrast:** As a verb the two words stay apart: *she made up the whole story*.

Made-up has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Invented, not true or not real.** (выдуманный, вымышленный)

1. That's a *made-up* word — you won't find it in any dictionary.
2. He told his boss a *made-up* story about a flat tire.
3. The names in the book are all *made-up*.

**2. Wearing makeup.** (накрашенный)

4. She arrived heavily *made-up* for the photo shoot.
5. His face was *made-up* to look forty years older.

---

### magenta

**Pronunciation:** /məˈdʒɛntə/ &middot; *muh-JEN-tuh*
**Русский:** пурпурный, фуксия
**Type:** color
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable

Of a bright purplish pink.

1. She painted the front door *magenta*.
2. The printer is out of *magenta* ink.
3. A row of *magenta* flowers ran along the fence.

---

### magic

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmædʒɪk/ &middot; *MAJ-ik*
**Русский:** волшебный, магический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — after a verb use *magical*: *the evening was magical*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *Magic* names the thing itself (*a magic wand*); *magical* describes how something feels (*a magical evening*). *Magic* is also a noun.

Magic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Using or produced by supernatural power.** (волшебный, магический)

1. He pulled a rabbit out of a hat — the oldest *magic* trick there is.
2. In the story a *magic* mirror answers every question.

**2. Working so well that it seems impossible.** (чудодейственный)

3. There is no *magic* formula for losing weight.
4. Her *magic* touch with angry customers saved the store.

---

### magical

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmædʒɪkəl/ &middot; *MAJ-ih-kuhl*
**Русский:** волшебный, чудесный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more magical*, *most magical*
**Form:** *magic* + -al

Magical has 2 distinct senses.

**1. So beautiful or happy that it feels like magic.** (волшебный, сказочный)

1. Venice in the fog was *magical*.
2. They spent a *magical* week on the coast.

**2. Of magic, or having magic powers.** (магический)

3. The ring is said to have *magical* properties.
4. He studies *magical* beliefs in medieval Europe.

---

### magnanimous

**Pronunciation:** /mæɡˈnænəməs/ &middot; *mag-NAN-uh-muhs*
**Русский:** великодушный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more magnanimous*, *most magnanimous*
**Takes:** *in* + noun
**Opposite:** *petty*

Generous and forgiving, especially toward someone you have beaten.

1. The champion was *magnanimous* in victory and praised her opponent.
2. It was a *magnanimous* gesture to pay for everyone's dinner.
3. He could have sued, but he chose to be *magnanimous*.

---

### magnetic

**Pronunciation:** /mæɡˈnɛtɪk/ &middot; *mag-NET-ik*
**Русский:** магнитный; притягательный
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more magnetic*, *most magnetic*

Magnetic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of a magnet, or acting like one.** (магнитный)

1. The compass needle follows the Earth's *magnetic* field.
2. The knives hang on a *magnetic* strip above the counter.

**2. So attractive that people cannot look away.** (притягательный, обаятельный)

3. She has a *magnetic* presence on stage.
4. His *magnetic* personality filled the room.

---

### magnetized

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmæɡnətaɪzd/ &middot; *MAG-nuh-tyzd*
**Русский:** намагниченный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable

Made to act like a magnet.

1. Rub the needle on a magnet until it is *magnetized*.
2. The screwdriver has a *magnetized* tip so screws don't fall.
3. Keep the card away from anything *magnetized*.

---

### magnificent

**Pronunciation:** /mæɡˈnɪfəsənt/ &middot; *mag-NIF-uh-suhnt*
**Русский:** великолепный, величественный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more magnificent*, *most magnificent*

Extremely impressive in size, beauty or quality.

1. The view from the ridge was *magnificent*.
2. She gave a *magnificent* performance in the second act.
3. They restored the old theater to its *magnificent* original state.

---

### majestic

**Pronunciation:** /məˈdʒɛstɪk/ &middot; *muh-JES-tik*
**Русский:** величественный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more majestic*, *most majestic*

Grand and dignified enough to command respect.

1. A *majestic* elk stepped out of the trees.
2. The cathedral looks *majestic* from the river.
3. The organ opened with a slow, *majestic* chord.

---

### major

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmeɪdʒər/ &middot; *MAY-jur*
**Русский:** главный, крупный
**Type:** quality; also classifying — in music
**Position:** attributive only — for *the problem is big* English does not say *the problem is major*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *minor*

Major has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Large or important, more so than the others.** (главный, крупный, значительный)

1. Traffic is a *major* problem downtown.
2. She played a *major* role in the merger.
3. The storm did *major* damage to the pier.

**2. In music, of the scale that sounds bright rather than sad.** (мажорный)

4. The piece is in C *major*.
5. He switched from a minor key to a *major* one for the chorus.

---

### make-believe

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmeɪk bɪliːv/ &middot; *MAYK-bih-leev*
**Русский:** выдуманный, воображаемый
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — verb + verb
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *a world of make-believe*.

Existing only in play or imagination.

1. The kids served us *make-believe* tea in plastic cups.
2. He grew up in a *make-believe* world of knights and dragons.
3. It was a *make-believe* store with cardboard shelves.

---

### makeshift

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmeɪkʃɪft/ &middot; *MAYK-shift*
**Русский:** самодельный, временный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — verb + noun

Put together quickly out of whatever was at hand, as a temporary fix.

1. They built a *makeshift* shelter out of tarps and rope.
2. The gym became a *makeshift* hospital during the flood.
3. A crate served as a *makeshift* table.

---

### maladjusted

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmæləˈdʒʌstɪd/ &middot; *mal-uh-JUS-tid*
**Русский:** плохо приспособленный, неуравновешенный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more maladjusted*, *most maladjusted*
**Form:** mal- + *adjusted*

Unable to fit in with other people or with normal life.

1. The school takes in kids other districts call *maladjusted*.
2. He was lonely and *maladjusted* his whole first year.
3. Years abroad left her *maladjusted* to small-town life.

---

### Malaysian

**Pronunciation:** /məˈleɪʒən/ &middot; *muh-LAY-zhuhn*
**Русский:** малайзийский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Always capitalized. *A Malaysian* is the noun for a person.

Of Malaysia or its people.

1. We ate at a *Malaysian* restaurant near the station.
2. The team's coach is *Malaysian*.
3. She studies *Malaysian* trade policy.

---

### male

**Pronunciation:** /meɪl/ &middot; *MAYL*
**Русский:** мужской; самец
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *female*
**Contrast:** *Male* is about sex; *masculine* is about qualities felt to belong to men. Also a noun: *the males of the species*.

Male has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of the sex that fathers young; of men and boys.** (мужской, мужского пола)

1. A *male* lion can weigh four hundred pounds.
2. Most of the applicants were *male*.
3. The clinic offers a *male* nurse on every shift.

**2. Of a plug or part that fits into another.** (штыревой, вставляемый (о разъёме))

4. You need a cable with a *male* connector on both ends.
5. The *male* end goes into the wall socket.

---

### malevolent

**Pronunciation:** /məˈlɛvələnt/ &middot; *muh-LEV-uh-luhnt*
**Русский:** злобный, недоброжелательный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more malevolent*, *most malevolent*
**Opposite:** *benevolent*

Wanting to do harm to other people.

1. The novel's villain is quietly *malevolent*.
2. She gave him a *malevolent* stare across the table.
3. Nothing in his face was *malevolent*, only tired.

---

### malformed

**Pronunciation:** /mælˈfɔːrmd/ &middot; *mal-FORMD*
**Русский:** деформированный, неправильной формы
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** mal- + *formed*

Shaped wrongly, usually from birth or from a fault in making.

1. The puppy was born with a *malformed* paw.
2. A *malformed* valve caused the leak.
3. Several of the seedlings came up *malformed*.

---

### malfunctioning

**Pronunciation:** /mælˈfʌŋkʃənɪŋ/ &middot; *mal-FUHNGK-shuh-ning*
**Русский:** неисправный, работающий со сбоями
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** mal- + *functioning*

Not working the way it should.

1. A *malfunctioning* sensor shut the whole line down.
2. The elevator has been *malfunctioning* since Tuesday.
3. They traced the fire to a *malfunctioning* space heater.

---

### malicious

**Pronunciation:** /məˈlɪʃəs/ &middot; *muh-LISH-uhs*
**Русский:** злонамеренный, злобный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more malicious*, *most malicious*
