Meant to hurt someone, and done on purpose.

1. He was charged with *malicious* damage to property.
2. Someone spread a *malicious* rumor about her.
3. The email carried *malicious* software.

---

### malign

**Pronunciation:** /məˈlaɪn/ &middot; *muh-LYN*
**Русский:** пагубный, зловредный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** more/most — *more malign*, *most malign*
**Opposite:** *benign*
**Contrast:** Also a verb with the same sound — *to malign someone* is to say evil things about them. For illness use *malignant*.

Harmful in a quiet, lasting way.

1. Money had a *malign* influence on the whole election.
2. He blamed the crash on the *malign* neglect of the regulators.
3. The war left a *malign* mark on two generations.

---

### malignant

**Pronunciation:** /məˈlɪɡnənt/ &middot; *muh-LIG-nuhnt*
**Русский:** злокачественный; злобный
**Type:** classifying; also evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more malignant*, *most malignant*
**Opposite:** *benign*

Malignant has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of a tumor: cancerous and likely to spread.** (злокачественный)

1. The biopsy showed the growth was *malignant*.
2. Doctors removed the *malignant* tissue last week.

**2. Full of hatred and wanting to do harm.** (злобный, исполненный ненависти)

3. There was something *malignant* in the way he smiled.
4. The city fought a *malignant* rumor for months.

---

### malleable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmæliəbəl/ &middot; *MAL-ee-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** ковкий; податливый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more malleable*, *most malleable*

Malleable has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of metal: soft enough to be hammered or pressed into shape.** (ковкий, пластичный)

1. Gold is the most *malleable* of all metals.
2. Heat the copper until it turns *malleable*.

**2. Of a person: easily influenced or changed.** (податливый, легко поддающийся влиянию)

3. New recruits are young and *malleable*.
4. He wanted a *malleable* deputy, not one with opinions.

---

### malnourished

**Pronunciation:** /mælˈnɜːrɪʃt/ &middot; *mal-NUR-isht*
**Русский:** истощённый, страдающий от недоедания
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more malnourished*, *most malnourished*
**Form:** mal- + *nourished*

Weak and unhealthy from not getting enough good food.

1. The dog was *malnourished* when the shelter took it in.
2. One child in five in the region is *malnourished*.
3. He treated *malnourished* patients for two years in the camp.

---

### malodorous

**Pronunciation:** /mælˈoʊdərəs/ &middot; *mal-OH-dur-uhs*
**Русский:** зловонный, дурно пахнущий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more malodorous*, *most malodorous*
**Form:** mal- + *odorous*, from *odor*
**Contrast:** Formal or joking. Everyday American English says *smelly*.

Smelling very bad.

1. A *malodorous* pile of trash sat by the loading dock.
2. The locker room was warm and *malodorous*.
3. He opened a *malodorous* container from the back of the fridge.

---

### malted

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɔːltɪd/ &middot; *MAWL-tid*
**Русский:** солодовый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Made from grain that has been soaked and dried to bring out its sugar.

1. He ordered a *malted* milkshake.
2. The recipe calls for two cups of *malted* barley.
3. *Malted* milk powder gives the cookies their flavor.

---

### Maltese

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɔːlˈtiːz/ &middot; *mawl-TEEZ*
**Русский:** мальтийский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Always capitalized. The same form serves for one person and for many: *two Maltese sailors*.

Of Malta, its people or its language.

1. *Maltese* is the only Semitic language written in the Latin alphabet.
2. She married a *Maltese* engineer.
3. The harbor is lined with *Maltese* fishing boats.

---

### mammalian

**Pronunciation:** /məˈmeɪliən/ &middot; *muh-MAY-lee-uhn*
**Русский:** относящийся к млекопитающим
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Of mammals.

1. Whales share the basic *mammalian* body plan.
2. The lab studies *mammalian* cells.
3. Milk is a *mammalian* invention.

---

### mammoth

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmæməθ/ &middot; *MAM-uhth*
**Русский:** гигантский, громадный
**Type:** size
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun — the extinct elephant.

Enormous.

1. Rebuilding the bridge is a *mammoth* undertaking.
2. The company posted a *mammoth* loss last quarter.
3. They hauled in a *mammoth* Christmas tree.

---

### man-made

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmæn ˈmeɪd/ &middot; *man-MAYD*
**Русский:** искусственный, рукотворный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + participle
**Opposite:** *natural*

Made by people rather than occurring in nature.

1. The lake is *man-made*.
2. Nylon is a *man-made* fiber.
3. The famine was *man-made*, not the result of drought.

---

### man-to-man

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmæn tə ˈmæn/ &middot; *man-tuh-MAN*
**Русский:** откровенный, мужской (о разговоре); персональный (в защите)
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** The same form works as an adverb: *they played man-to-man all night*.

Man-to-man has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of a talk: frank and direct between two men.** (откровенный, начистоту)

1. His dad sat him down for a *man-to-man* talk.
2. What they needed was a *man-to-man* conversation, not another email.

**2. In sports, with each player guarding one opponent.** (персональная (защита))

3. The coach switched to a *man-to-man* defense in the fourth quarter.
4. They lost because their *man-to-man* coverage broke down.

---

### manageable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmænɪdʒəbəl/ &middot; *MAN-ij-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** выполнимый, посильный, управляемый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more manageable*, *most manageable*
**Form:** *manage* + -able
**Opposite:** *unmanageable*

Small or simple enough to deal with.

1. Break the project into *manageable* pieces.
2. The debt is large but still *manageable*.
3. Short hair is more *manageable* in the summer.

---

### managerial

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmænəˈdʒɪriəl/ &middot; *man-uh-JEER-ee-uhl*
**Русский:** управленческий, руководящий
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** from *manager*

Of managers or the work of managing.

1. She moved into a *managerial* role after five years on the floor.
2. The job calls for *managerial* experience.
3. It was a *managerial* decision, not a technical one.

---

### mandatory

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmændətɔːri/ &middot; *MAN-duh-tor-ee*
**Русский:** обязательный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *for* + noun
**Opposite:** *optional*

Required by a rule or a law.

1. Helmets are *mandatory* for all riders.
2. The training is *mandatory*, not a suggestion.
3. The state has a *mandatory* five-day waiting period.

---

### maneuverable

**Pronunciation:** /məˈnuːvərəbəl/ &middot; *muh-NOO-vur-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** маневренный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more maneuverable*, *most maneuverable*
**Form:** *maneuver* + -able
**Contrast:** American spelling *maneuverable*; British writing has *manoeuvrable*.

Easy to steer and turn.

1. The little boat is far more *maneuverable* than the ferry.
2. A short wheelbase makes the truck *maneuverable* in the city.
3. They wanted a *maneuverable* fighter, not a fast one.

---

### mangled

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmæŋɡəld/ &middot; *MANG-guhld*
**Русский:** искорёженный, изувеченный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable

Badly crushed, torn or twisted out of shape.

1. A *mangled* bicycle lay under the guardrail.
2. His hand was *mangled* in the machine.
3. She read out a *mangled* version of my name.

---

### mangy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmeɪndʒi/ &middot; *MAYN-jee*
**Русский:** шелудивый; облезлый, паршивый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *mangier*, *mangiest*
**Form:** from the noun *mange*, a skin disease of animals

Mangy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of an animal: having mange, with patches of missing fur.** (шелудивый, покрытый паршой)

1. A *mangy* stray dog followed us down the alley.
2. The fox looked thin and *mangy*.

**2. Shabby and dirty.** (облезлый, потрёпанный)

3. He sat on a *mangy* couch in the corner.
4. The second motel was even *mangier* than the first.

---

### maniacal

**Pronunciation:** /məˈnaɪəkəl/ &middot; *muh-NY-uh-kuhl*
**Русский:** маниакальный, безумный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more maniacal*, *most maniacal*
**Form:** from *maniac*

Behaving in a wild, crazy way.

1. He let out a *maniacal* laugh.
2. There was a *maniacal* gleam in her eye.
3. The pace in the kitchen was close to *maniacal*.

---

### manic

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmænɪk/ &middot; *MAN-ik*
**Русский:** лихорадочный; маниакальный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more manic*, *most manic*

Manic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Full of wild, restless energy.** (лихорадочный, взвинченный)

1. December is *manic* at the post office.
2. He gets a little *manic* before a deadline.

**2. In medicine, of the excited phase of bipolar disorder.** (маниакальный)

3. The drug controls her *manic* episodes.
4. A *manic* phase can last for weeks.

---

### manic-depressive

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmænɪk dɪˈprɛsɪv/ &middot; *man-ik-dih-PRES-iv*
**Русский:** маниакально-депрессивный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *manic* + *depressive*
**Contrast:** Doctors now say *bipolar*; *manic-depressive* is the older term you still meet in books.

Swinging between wild highs and deep lows of mood.

1. He was diagnosed as *manic-depressive* in his twenties.
2. The memoir describes a *manic-depressive* year in painful detail.
3. Her moods that spring were almost *manic-depressive*.

---

### manifest

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmænəfɛst/ &middot; *MAN-uh-fest*
**Русский:** явный, очевидный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more manifest*, *most manifest*
**Contrast:** Formal. Also a verb (*to manifest itself*) and a noun (a ship's *manifest*), both spelled the same.

Plain to see, needing no proof.

1. His impatience was *manifest* to everyone in the room.
2. The report describes a *manifest* failure of oversight.
3. Their joy became *manifest* the moment the doors opened.

---

### manifold

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmænəfoʊld/ &middot; *MAN-uh-fohld*
**Русский:** многообразный, многочисленный
**Type:** quantity
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Formal and literary; everyday English says *many*. Also a noun — the pipe on a car engine.

Many and of many kinds.

1. The benefits of the treaty were *manifold*.
2. She listed the *manifold* problems with the design.
3. His talents are *manifold*.

---

### manipulative

**Pronunciation:** /məˈnɪpjələtɪv/ &middot; *muh-NIP-yuh-luh-tiv*
**Русский:** манипулятивный, склонный манипулировать
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more manipulative*, *most manipulative*
**Form:** from *manipulate*

Controlling people by clever, dishonest means.

1. Her ex was charming and deeply *manipulative*.
2. The ad is *manipulative*, and children can't see through it.
3. He uses guilt in a *manipulative* way.

---

### manly

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmænli/ &middot; *MAN-lee*
**Русский:** мужественный, мужской
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *manlier*, *manliest*
**Form:** *man* + -ly — here -ly makes an adjective, not an adverb

Having the qualities people expect of a man, such as strength or courage.

1. He thought crying wasn't *manly*.
2. She admired his quiet, *manly* patience.
3. No sport looked *manlier* to him than boxing.

---

### manned

**Pronunciation:** /mænd/ &middot; *MAND*
**Русский:** пилотируемый, с экипажем
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *unmanned*
**Contrast:** NASA now writes *crewed*, but *manned* is still everywhere in older texts.

Having people on board or on duty.

1. The first *manned* flight to the Moon left in 1969.
2. There is a *manned* guard post at the gate.
3. They compared *manned* and unmanned missions.

---

### manual

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmænjuəl/ &middot; *MAN-yoo-uhl*
**Русский:** ручной; физический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *automatic*
**Contrast:** Also a noun — the book of instructions.

Manual has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Worked by hand rather than automatically.** (ручной, с ручным управлением)

1. The car has a *manual* transmission.
2. Switch the camera to *manual* focus.

**2. Of work done with the hands and the body.** (физический (о труде))

3. He spent twenty years in *manual* labor.
4. The town lost most of its *manual* jobs when the mill closed.

---

### manufactured

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmænjəˈfæktʃərd/ &middot; *man-yuh-FAK-churd*
**Русский:** промышленного производства; сфабрикованный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Manufactured has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Made in a factory, usually in quantity.** (промышленный, заводского изготовления)

1. *Manufactured* goods make up most of the country's exports.
2. They live in a *manufactured* home outside town.

**2. Created on purpose to look real when it is not.** (сфабрикованный, искусственно созданный)

3. The scandal was pure *manufactured* outrage.
4. Critics called the shortage a *manufactured* crisis.

---

### Maoist

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmaʊɪst/ &middot; *MOW-ist*
**Русский:** маоистский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** *Mao* + -ist
**Contrast:** Capitalized, and *a Maoist* is also a noun.

Following the communist ideas of Mao Zedong.

1. A *Maoist* faction controlled the countryside for a decade.
2. He wrote a history of *Maoist* economic policy.
3. Her politics in college were frankly *Maoist*.

---

### marauding

**Pronunciation:** /məˈrɔːdɪŋ/ &middot; *muh-RAW-ding*
**Русский:** мародёрствующий, совершающий набеги
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Roaming around looking for things to steal or destroy.

1. *Marauding* bands burned three villages that winter.
2. Farmers lost half the crop to *marauding* elk.
3. The town built its wall against *marauding* raiders.

---

### marbled

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɑːrbəld/ &middot; *MAHR-buhld*
**Русский:** мраморный, с прожилками
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable

Streaked with lines of another color, the way marble is.

1. A well *marbled* steak stays juicy on the grill.
2. The book has *marbled* endpapers in blue and gold.
3. The soap came out *marbled* green and white.

---

### marginal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɑːrdʒənəl/ &middot; *MAHR-juh-nuhl*
**Русский:** незначительный; краевой
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more marginal*, *most marginal*
**Form:** from *margin*

Marginal has 2 distinct senses.

**1. So small that it hardly matters.** (незначительный, ничтожный)

1. The new engine gave only a *marginal* gain in mileage.
2. Her influence on the decision was *marginal*.

**2. At the edge of a group or an area, and not part of the center.** (периферийный, маргинальный)

3. The party was a *marginal* force until the last election.
4. Wheat grows poorly on this *marginal* land.

---

### marine

**Pronunciation:** /məˈriːn/ &middot; *muh-REEN*
**Русский:** морской
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Capitalized, *a Marine* is a noun — a member of the Marine Corps.

Marine has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Living in or belonging to the sea.** (морской (о жизни в море))

1. She studies *marine* mammals off the coast of Maine.
2. Plastic is choking *marine* life.

**2. Having to do with ships and shipping.** (морской, судоходный)

3. The cargo is covered by *marine* insurance.
4. He runs a *marine* supply store at the harbor.

---

### marital

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɛrɪtəl/ &middot; *MER-ih-tuhl*
**Русский:** супружеский, брачный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Don't mix up *marital* (of marriage) with *martial* (of war) — the letters are nearly the same.

Of marriage or of husband and wife.

1. The form asks for your *marital* status.
2. They went through years of *marital* trouble.
3. The state divides *marital* property evenly.

---

### maritime

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɛrətaɪm/ &middot; *MER-uh-tym*
**Русский:** морской; приморский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Maritime has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Connected with the sea, ships and sailing.** (морской, судоходный)

1. The museum tells the city's *maritime* history.
2. Two navies patrol the *maritime* border.

**2. Near the sea, or typical of places near it.** (приморский)

3. The island has a mild *maritime* climate.
4. He grew up in a small *maritime* town.

---

### marked

**Pronunciation:** /mɑːrkt/ &middot; *MAHRKT*
**Русский:** заметный, явный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** more/most — *more marked*, *most marked*
**Contrast:** The adverb *markedly* gains a syllable: MAHR-kid-lee.

Marked has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Clear enough that anyone would notice it.** (заметный, ощутимый)

1. There has been a *marked* improvement since March.
2. She spoke with a *marked* Southern accent.

**2. Singled out, usually for harm.** (меченый, обречённый)

3. After testifying he was a *marked* man.
4. The informant knew he was *marked*.

---

### marketable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɑːrkətəbəl/ &middot; *MAHR-kuh-tuh-buhl*
**Русский:** ходовой, пользующийся спросом
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more marketable*, *most marketable*
**Form:** *market* + -able

Easy to sell, or likely to find buyers.

1. Coding is a *marketable* skill in almost any town.
2. The house needs paint before it will be *marketable*.
3. He picked a degree he thought was more *marketable*.

---

### marooned

**Pronunciation:** /məˈruːnd/ &middot; *muh-ROOND*
**Русский:** брошенный, отрезанный от мира
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *on* or *in* + noun

Left in a place you cannot get away from.

1. The sailors were *marooned* on a bare island for nine months.
2. We were *marooned* in the airport all night.
3. The flood left a dozen *marooned* families on their roofs.

---

### marred

**Pronunciation:** /mɑːrd/ &middot; *MAHRD*
**Русский:** испорченный, подпорченный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *by* + noun

Spoiled by something that damages it.

1. The ceremony was *marred* by a sudden storm.
2. He ran his hand over the *marred* surface of the desk.
3. Her record is *marred* by one bad season.

---

### marshy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɑːrʃi/ &middot; *MAHR-shee*
**Русский:** болотистый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *marshier*, *marshiest*
**Form:** *marsh* + -y

Wet and soft, like a marsh.

1. The ground near the creek is *marshy* all spring.
2. They lost a boot crossing the *marshy* field.
3. The far end of the meadow is *marshier* than this one.

---

### martial

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɑːrʃəl/ &middot; *MAHR-shuhl*
**Русский:** военный, воинский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Don't mix up *martial* (of war) with *marital* (of marriage).

Of war, soldiers or fighting.

1. The country has been under *martial* law since June.
2. She has studied *martial* arts since she was six.
3. The band struck up a *martial* tune.

---

### Martian

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɑːrʃən/ &middot; *MAHR-shuhn*
**Русский:** марсианский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Capitalized. Also a noun — *a Martian*.

Of the planet Mars.

1. The rover sent back photos of the *Martian* surface.
2. A *Martian* day is about forty minutes longer than ours.
3. The film opens with a *Martian* dust storm.

---

### marvelous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɑːrvələs/ &middot; *MAHR-vuh-luhs*
**Русский:** изумительный, чудесный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more marvelous*, *most marvelous*
**Form:** -ous from *marvel*
**Contrast:** American spelling *marvelous*, with one l; British writing has *marvellous*.

Extremely good or pleasing.

1. We had a *marvelous* time in Chicago.
2. She is a *marvelous* cook.
3. The weather that week was simply *marvelous*.

---

### Marxist

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɑːrksɪst/ &middot; *MAHRK-sist*
**Русский:** марксистский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** *Marx* + -ist
**Contrast:** Capitalized, and also a noun — *she is a Marxist*.

Following the political and economic ideas of Karl Marx.

1. He teaches a course on *Marxist* theory.
2. The paper takes a *Marxist* view of the strike.
3. Two of the parties in the coalition are openly *Marxist*.

---

### masculine

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmæskjələn/ &middot; *MAS-kyuh-lin*
**Русский:** мужской, мужественный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more masculine*, *most masculine*
**Opposite:** *feminine*
**Contrast:** *Masculine* is about qualities and style; *male* is about biological sex.

Masculine has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having qualities or a look traditionally thought of as belonging to men.** (мужественный, мужского склада)

1. The room is dark and heavily *masculine*.
2. He has a deep, *masculine* voice.

**2. In grammar, belonging to the gender that goes with words like *he*.** (мужского рода)

3. In Spanish, *libro* is *masculine*.
4. German has *masculine*, feminine and neuter nouns.

---

### masked

**Pronunciation:** /mæskt/ &middot; *MASKT*
**Русский:** в маске; скрытый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Masked has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Wearing a mask.** (в маске, замаскированный)

1. Two *masked* men walked into the bank.
2. Guests arrived for a *masked* ball.

**2. Hidden behind something that covers it up.** (скрытый, замаскированный)

3. Painkillers can leave *masked* symptoms that fool the doctor.
4. Under the compliment was a *masked* threat.

---

### masochistic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmæsəˈkɪstɪk/ &middot; *mas-uh-KIS-tik*
**Русский:** мазохистский
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more masochistic*, *most masochistic*
**Form:** from *masochist*

Seeming to enjoy pain or suffering, your own most of all.

1. Running a marathon in August is a little *masochistic*.
2. He has a *masochistic* habit of rereading his bad reviews.
3. Staying in that job was plainly *masochistic*.

---

### Masonic

**Pronunciation:** /məˈsɑːnɪk/ &middot; *muh-SAH-nik*
**Русский:** масонский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Capitalized; it belongs to the Freemasons, not to stonework.

Of the Freemasons.

1. The meeting was held in the old *Masonic* temple.
2. He wore a ring with a *Masonic* symbol.
3. The town's *Masonic* lodge dates from 1870.

---

### mass

**Pronunciation:** /mæs/ &middot; *MAS*
**Русский:** массовый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun (*a mass of people*) and, capitalized, the church service.

Involving or aimed at very large numbers of people.

1. Television is still the great *mass* medium.
2. The factory turned to *mass* production in 1913.
3. There was a *mass* evacuation of the coast.

---

### mass-produced

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmæs prəˈduːst/ &middot; *mas-pruh-DOOST*
**Русский:** серийный, массового производства
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + participle
**Opposite:** *handmade*

Made in large numbers by machine, all copies alike.

1. The chairs are *mass-produced* and cost twelve dollars each.
2. He hates *mass-produced* furniture.
3. Once the vaccine was *mass-produced*, the price fell.

---

### massive

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmæsɪv/ &middot; *MAS-iv*
**Русский:** массивный; огромный
**Type:** size
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more massive*, *most massive*

Massive has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Very large and heavy.** (массивный, громоздкий)

1. A *massive* oak door closed behind us.
2. The bridge rests on two *massive* concrete piers.

**2. Very great in amount or degree.** (огромный, колоссальный)

3. He suffered a *massive* heart attack.
4. The campaign raised a *massive* amount of money.

---

### masterful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmæstərfəl/ &middot; *MAS-tur-fuhl*
**Русский:** мастерский; властный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more masterful*, *most masterful*
**Form:** -ful from *master*
**Contrast:** *Masterful* now covers both skill and command; *masterly* means skill only.

Masterful has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Done with great skill.** (мастерский, виртуозный)

1. It was a *masterful* piece of editing.
2. Her handling of the reporters was *masterful*.

**2. Used to giving orders and expecting them to be obeyed.** (властный, повелительный)

3. He took the wheel in a *masterful* way that annoyed everyone.
4. Her voice turned *masterful* the moment the crew arrived.

---

### matched

**Pronunciation:** /mætʃt/ &middot; *MACHT*
**Русский:** подобранный, равный по силам
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable

Fitting or equal to something else, so the two go together.

1. The two fighters were evenly *matched*.
2. She brought out a set of *matched* silver candlesticks.
3. Badly *matched* tires wear out fast.

---

### matching

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmætʃɪŋ/ &middot; *MACH-ing*
**Русский:** подходящий, в тон, парный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Of the same color, pattern or kind as something else.

1. She wore a green dress and a *matching* hat.
2. The twins showed up in *matching* sweaters.
3. The company offers a *matching* contribution to your savings.

---

### material

**Pronunciation:** /məˈtɪriəl/ &middot; *muh-TEER-ee-uhl*
**Русский:** материальный; существенный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more material*, *most material*
**Takes:** *to* + noun — in the sense *important*
**Preposition patterns:** [material to](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#material-to)
**Contrast:** Also a noun — cloth, or the stuff something is made of.

Material has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of physical things, money and possessions rather than the mind or spirit.** (материальный, вещественный)

1. They have every *material* comfort and still aren't happy.
2. The scholarship covers her *material* needs.

**2. Important enough to affect the outcome.** (существенный, имеющий значение)

3. The witness had no *material* evidence to offer.
4. That detail is not *material* to the case.

---

### materialistic

**Pronunciation:** /məˌtɪriəˈlɪstɪk/ &middot; *muh-teer-ee-uh-LIS-tik*
**Русский:** материалистичный, помешанный на вещах
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more materialistic*, *most materialistic*
**Form:** from *materialist*

Caring too much about money and possessions.

1. He calls the whole holiday shallow and *materialistic*.
2. She grew less *materialistic* after the move.
3. It was a *materialistic* decade, and the ads showed it.

---

### maternal

**Pronunciation:** /məˈtɜːrnəl/ &middot; *muh-TUR-nuhl*
**Русский:** материнский; по материнской линии
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more maternal*, *most maternal*
**Opposite:** *paternal*

Maternal has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of a mother, or like a mother.** (материнский)

1. She felt a sudden *maternal* urge to protect him.
2. The hospital tracks *maternal* health for a year after birth.

**2. Related to you through your mother.** (по матери, по материнской линии)

3. My *maternal* grandfather came from Naples.
4. The gene came down the *maternal* line.

---

### mathematical

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmæθəˈmætɪkəl/ &middot; *math-uh-MAT-ih-kuhl*
**Русский:** математический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Of mathematics, or done with numbers and calculation.

1. The proof rests on a simple *mathematical* argument.
2. He has real *mathematical* ability.
3. Their chance of winning is a *mathematical* certainty now.

---

### matriarchal

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmeɪtriˈɑːrkəl/ &middot; *may-tree-AHR-kuhl*
**Русский:** матриархальный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *patriarchal*

Ruled or led by women, especially by mothers.

1. Elephant herds are *matriarchal*.
2. She studies a *matriarchal* society in southern China.
3. The family is quietly *matriarchal*: grandma decides everything.

---

### matrimonial

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmætrəˈmoʊniəl/ &middot; *ma-truh-MOH-nee-uhl*
**Русский:** брачный, супружеский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Formal and legal; in everyday speech people say *marriage* as a modifier or *marital*.

Of marriage or of the wedding.

1. The lawyer handles *matrimonial* cases.
2. They signed a *matrimonial* agreement before the wedding.
3. The paper still runs *matrimonial* ads.

---

### matronly

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmeɪtrənli/ &middot; *MAY-truhn-lee*
**Русский:** солидный, почтенный (о женщине средних лет)
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more matronly*, *most matronly*
**Form:** *matron* + -ly — here -ly makes an adjective, not an adverb

Of a middle-aged woman: plump, settled and rather old-fashioned.

1. A *matronly* woman in a flowered dress took our tickets.
2. She thought the coat made her look *matronly*.
3. He remembered her as *matronly* and endlessly kind.

---

### matte

**Pronunciation:** /mæt/ &middot; *MAT*
**Русский:** матовый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *glossy*
**Contrast:** Spelled *matte* but pronounced exactly like *mat*.

Dull rather than shiny.

1. We painted the ceiling a flat *matte* white.
2. She prefers a *matte* lipstick.
3. Print the photos *matte*, not glossy.

---

### matted

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmætɪd/ &middot; *MAT-id*
**Русский:** свалявшийся, спутанный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable

Stuck together in a thick, tangled mass.

1. The dog's fur was *matted* with mud.
2. His hair lay *matted* against his forehead.
3. We pulled up a layer of *matted* leaves.

---

### matter-of-fact

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmætər əv ˈfækt/ &middot; *mat-ur-uhv-FAKT*
**Русский:** будничный, бесстрастный, деловитый
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more matter-of-fact*, *most matter-of-fact*
**Form:** compound — a whole phrase used as one adjective

Saying something calmly, as plain fact, with no feeling shown.

1. She announced the diagnosis in a *matter-of-fact* voice.
2. He was surprisingly *matter-of-fact* about losing the house.
3. The report is short and *matter-of-fact*.

---

### mature

**Pronunciation:** /məˈtʊr/ &middot; *muh-TUUR*
**Русский:** зрелый, взрослый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est or more/most — *maturer* or *more mature*, *maturest* or *most mature*
**Opposite:** *immature*

Mature has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Grown up, and sensible in the way adults are expected to be.** (зрелый, взрослый)

1. She is very *mature* for fourteen.
2. His brother is three years older but no *more mature*.

**2. Fully grown or fully developed.** (созревший, выдержанный)

3. The yard has two *mature* maples.
4. The cheese is aged until it is *mature*.

---

### maudlin

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɔːdlɪn/ &middot; *MAWD-lin*
**Русский:** слезливо-сентиментальный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more maudlin*, *most maudlin*

Sentimental and tearful in a silly way, often after drinking.

1. After the third beer he turned *maudlin* about his college years.
2. The song is *maudlin*, but the crowd loves it.
3. She cut the speech before it got *maudlin*.

---

### mauve

**Pronunciation:** /moʊv/ &middot; *MOHV*
**Русский:** сиреневый, лиловый
**Type:** color
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable

Of a pale purple color.

1. The bridesmaids wore *mauve*.
2. She chose a *mauve* wallpaper for the hall.
3. The sky went *mauve* just after sunset.

---

### maverick

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmævərɪk/ &middot; *MAV-ur-ik*
**Русский:** независимый, идущий против течения
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *he's a maverick*.

Refusing to follow the group and doing things your own way.

1. A *maverick* senator broke with her party on the vote.
2. He built the company with a *maverick* streak and a lot of luck.
3. The film follows a *maverick* detective who ignores every rule.

---

### maximum

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmæksəməm/ &middot; *MAK-suh-muhm*
**Русский:** максимальный, предельный
**Type:** quantity
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *minimum*
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *a maximum of ten people*.

The greatest that is possible or allowed.

1. The *maximum* speed on this road is 55.
2. He got the *maximum* sentence.
3. Set the oven to its *maximum* temperature.

---

### mayoral

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmeɪərəl/ &middot; *MAY-ur-uhl*
**Русский:** мэрский, относящийся к мэру
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** from *mayor*

Of a mayor or a mayor's office.

1. The *mayoral* race drew nine candidates.
2. She announced her *mayoral* campaign in March.
3. The *mayoral* term here is four years.

---

### meager

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmiːɡər/ &middot; *MEE-gur*
**Русский:** скудный, жалкий
**Type:** quantity
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more meager*, *most meager*
**Contrast:** American spelling *meager*; British writing has *meagre*.

Much too small in amount.

1. They lived on his *meager* pension.
2. The results after six months were *meager*.
3. Dinner was a *meager* bowl of soup.

---

### mealy-mouthed

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmiːli maʊðd/ &middot; *MEE-lee-mowthd*
**Русский:** уклончивый, неискренний
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more mealy-mouthed*, *most mealy-mouthed*
**Form:** compound — noun + *-ed*

Afraid to say plainly what you mean.

1. Don't be *mealy-mouthed* — tell them the project failed.
2. The statement was a *mealy-mouthed* non-apology.
3. Voters are tired of *mealy-mouthed* answers.

---

### meandering

**Pronunciation:** /miˈændərɪŋ/ &middot; *mee-AN-dur-ing*
**Русский:** извилистый; бессвязный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more meandering*, *most meandering*

Meandering has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Following a long, winding course.** (извилистый, петляющий)

1. A *meandering* creek cuts through the pasture.
2. We took the *meandering* coast road instead of the highway.

**2. Going on and on without getting to the point.** (бессвязный, растекающийся)

3. He gave a *meandering* answer that lasted ten minutes.
4. The novel's *meandering* middle section loses most readers.

---

### meaningful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmiːnɪŋfəl/ &middot; *MEE-ning-fuhl*
**Русский:** значимый, содержательный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more meaningful*, *most meaningful*
**Form:** -ful from *meaning*
**Opposite:** *meaningless*

Meaningful has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Serious and important, worth doing or having.** (значимый, важный)

1. She wanted *meaningful* work, not another spreadsheet.
2. There has been no *meaningful* progress since July.

**2. Of a look or a pause: carrying a message that is not said out loud.** (многозначительный)

3. He gave his wife a *meaningful* glance across the table.
4. There was a *meaningful* silence after the question.

---

### meaningless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmiːnɪŋləs/ &middot; *MEE-ning-luhs*
**Русский:** бессмысленный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more meaningless*, *most meaningless*
**Form:** -less from *meaning*
**Opposite:** *meaningful*

Having no meaning, purpose or value.

1. Without enforcement the rule is *meaningless*.
2. He filled the page with *meaningless* jargon.
3. The last game of the season was *meaningless* for both teams.

---

### measly

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmiːzli/ &middot; *MEEZ-lee*
**Русский:** жалкий, мизерный
**Type:** quantity
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** more/most — *more measly*, *most measly*
**Contrast:** Informal, and always a complaint about how small something is.

Annoyingly small in amount.

1. All that work for a *measly* twelve dollars.
2. They gave us a *measly* two days' notice.
3. He left a *measly* tip on a hundred-dollar check.

---

### measurable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɛʒərəbəl/ &middot; *MEZH-ur-uh-buhl*
**Русский:** измеримый, ощутимый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** *measure* + -able
**Opposite:** *immeasurable*

Big enough to be measured or clearly noticed.

1. The program produced a *measurable* drop in absences.
2. Set goals that are *measurable*.
3. There was no *measurable* rainfall all month.

---

### measured

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɛʒərd/ &middot; *MEZH-urd*
**Русский:** взвешенный; размеренный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** more/most — *more measured*, *most measured*

Measured has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Careful and controlled, not rushed or angry.** (взвешенный, сдержанный)

1. The governor gave a *measured* response to the attack.
2. He answered in a *measured* tone.

**2. Slow and regular in rhythm.** (размеренный, мерный)

3. We heard the *measured* tread of boots on the stairs.
4. She read the poem at a *measured* pace.

---

### meat-eating

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmiːt iːtɪŋ/ &middot; *MEET-ee-ting*
**Русский:** плотоядный, мясоедящий
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + *-ing*
**Opposite:** *plant-eating*

Living on meat.

1. *Meat-eating* dinosaurs walked on two legs.
2. The pitcher plant is the garden's one *meat-eating* resident.
3. She cooks for a house of *meat-eating* teenagers.

---

### meatless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmiːtləs/ &middot; *MEET-luhs*
**Русский:** без мяса, постный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -less from *meat*

Containing no meat.

1. The diner has a *meatless* chili on Fridays.
2. We try to have two *meatless* days a week.
3. She brought a *meatless* lasagna to the potluck.

---

### meaty

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmiːti/ &middot; *MEE-tee*
**Русский:** мясистый; содержательный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *meatier*, *meatiest*
**Form:** *meat* + -y

Meaty has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Full of meat, or tasting strongly of it.** (мясистый, мясной)

1. The sauce is thick and *meaty*.
2. These ribs are *meatier* than the ones we got last time.

**2. Full of substance and worth thinking about.** (содержательный, весомый)

3. She finally got a *meaty* role in a serious film.
4. It was a *meaty* article, not the usual summer filler.

---

### mechanical

**Pronunciation:** /məˈkænɪkəl/ &middot; *muh-KAN-i-kuhl*
**Русский:** механический; машинальный
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more mechanical*, *most mechanical*

Mechanical has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Connected with machines or the way they work.** (механический)

1. The flight was delayed by a *mechanical* problem.
2. She studied *mechanical* engineering.
3. The old clock is purely *mechanical* — there is no battery in it.

**2. Done automatically, without thought or feeling.** (машинальный, бездумный)

4. He gave a *mechanical* nod and kept reading.
5. Her apology sounded *mechanical*.

---

### mechanized

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɛkənaɪzd/ &middot; *MEK-uh-nyzd*
**Русский:** механизированный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *mechanize*

Using machines in place of people or animals.

1. Most of the harvest is now fully *mechanized*.
2. The army sent in a *mechanized* infantry unit.
3. Sorting the mail used to be done by hand; today it is *mechanized*.

---

### meddlesome

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɛdəlsəm/ &middot; *MED-uhl-suhm*
**Русский:** назойливый, лезущий не в своё дело
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more meddlesome*, *most meddlesome*
**Form:** -some from *meddle*

Always interfering in other people's business.

1. His *meddlesome* aunt rearranged the whole kitchen.
2. Reporters called the agency *meddlesome* and slow.
3. She has a *meddlesome* streak, but she means well.

---

### meddling

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɛdlɪŋ/ &middot; *MED-ling*
**Русский:** вмешивающийся, назойливый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ing from *meddle*

Interfering in things that are none of your business.

1. He blamed *meddling* bureaucrats for the delay.
2. Her *meddling* neighbors report every car on the street.
3. The play is about a *meddling* mother-in-law.

---

### median

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmiːdiən/ &middot; *MEE-dee-uhn*
**Русский:** срединный, медианный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** The *median* is the middle value, not the average: half the numbers fall below it. The average is the *mean*.

In the middle of a set of values, with half above and half below.

1. *Median* household income rose by two percent.
2. *Median* home prices fell for the first time in a decade.
3. The *median* age in the town is forty-one.

---

### medical

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɛdɪkəl/ &middot; *MED-i-kuhl*
**Русский:** медицинский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — nearly always before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable

Connected with medicine and the treatment of illness.

1. She is in her second year of *medical* school.
2. The company covers *medical* expenses.
3. The cause of the fainting turned out to be *medical*, not stress.

---

### medicinal

**Pronunciation:** /məˈdɪsənəl/ &middot; *muh-DIS-uh-nuhl*
**Русский:** лечебный, лекарственный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** from *medicine*
**Contrast:** *Medicinal* is about healing power; *medical* is about doctors and hospitals.

Used as medicine, or having the effect of medicine.

1. The plant has *medicinal* properties.
2. He keeps a bottle of whiskey for *medicinal* purposes.
3. The tea was bitter and faintly *medicinal*.

---

### medieval

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmiːdiˈiːvəl/ &middot; *mee-dee-EE-vuhl*
**Русский:** средневековый
**Type:** age; also classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable

Medieval has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Belonging to the Middle Ages, roughly 500 to 1500.** (средневековый)

1. The town has a *medieval* cathedral.
2. She teaches *medieval* history.

**2. Harsh and old-fashioned, used to criticize.** (допотопный, варварский)

3. Their attitude toward women is frankly *medieval*.
4. Conditions in the prison were *medieval*.

---

### mediocre

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmiːdiˈoʊkər/ &middot; *mee-dee-OH-kur*
**Русский:** посредственный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more mediocre*, *most mediocre*
**Opposite:** *excellent*

Not bad, but clearly not good — only average.

1. The food was *mediocre* and the service was slow.
2. He had a *mediocre* season and lost his place on the team.
3. The movie is *mediocre* at best.

---

### meditative

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɛdəteɪtɪv/ &middot; *MED-uh-tay-tiv*
**Русский:** задумчивый; медитативный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more meditative*, *most meditative*
**Form:** -ive from *meditate*

Quiet and thoughtful, or making you feel that way.

1. He was in a *meditative* mood all evening.
2. The music is slow and *meditative*.
3. She sat by the window with a *meditative* look on her face.

---

### Mediterranean

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɛdətəˈreɪniən/ &middot; *med-uh-tuh-RAY-nee-uhn*
**Русский:** средиземноморский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Always capitalized. As a noun, *the Mediterranean* is the sea itself.

Belonging to the Mediterranean Sea and the countries around it.

1. We ate at a *Mediterranean* restaurant downtown.
2. The house has a *Mediterranean* feel, with white walls and blue shutters.
3. The climate here is *Mediterranean*: hot dry summers and mild winters.

---

### medium

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmiːdiəm/ &middot; *MEE-dee-uhm*
**Русский:** средний
**Type:** size
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun: a *medium* is a means of communication, or a person who claims to speak with the dead. The plural of the first is *media*.

Medium has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Between large and small, or halfway between two extremes.** (средний)

1. I'll take a *medium* coffee.
2. She is of *medium* height.
3. He has *medium* brown hair.

**2. Of meat: cooked so that the inside is still slightly pink.** (средней прожарки)

4. I ordered my steak *medium*.
5. He likes his burgers *medium* rather than well done.

---

### medium-sized

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmiːdiəm saɪzd/ &middot; *MEE-dee-uhm-syzd*
**Русский:** среднего размера
**Type:** compound; also size
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *medium* + *sized*

Neither large nor small.

1. They live in a *medium-sized* town in Ohio.
2. Chop two *medium-sized* onions.
3. It is a *medium-sized* firm with about sixty employees.

---

### meek

**Pronunciation:** /miːk/ &middot; *MEEK*
**Русский:** кроткий, робкий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *meeker*, *meekest*
**Opposite:** *assertive*

Quiet and gentle, and unwilling to argue or stand up for yourself.

1. She is too *meek* to complain about the noise.
2. He offered a *meek* apology and sat down.
3. With every year he grew *meeker* and quieter.

---

### megalomaniacal

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɛɡəloʊməˈnaɪəkəl/ &middot; *meg-uh-loh-muh-NY-uh-kuhl*
**Русский:** страдающий манией величия
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more megalomaniacal*, *most megalomaniacal*
**Form:** from *megalomania*

Obsessed with your own power and greatness.

1. The film's villain is a *megalomaniacal* tech billionaire.
2. His plans grew more *megalomaniacal* with every meeting.
3. Critics called the whole project *megalomaniacal*.

---

### melancholic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɛlənˈkɑːlɪk/ &middot; *mel-uhn-KOL-ik*
**Русский:** меланхоличный, грустный
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more melancholic*, *most melancholic*
**Form:** from *melancholy*

Sad in a quiet, lasting way.

1. The songs are slow and *melancholic*.
2. He gets *melancholic* every November.
3. There is a *melancholic* beauty to the empty beach.

---

### melancholy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɛlənkɑːli/ &middot; *MEL-uhn-kol-ee*
**Русский:** грустный, печальный
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more melancholy*, *most melancholy*
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *a deep melancholy settled over him*. After a verb, *melancholic* is more common.

Full of quiet sadness.

1. She played a *melancholy* tune on the piano.
2. The letter has a *melancholy* tone.
3. He was in a *melancholy* mood after the funeral.

---

### mellifluous

**Pronunciation:** /məˈlɪfluəs/ &middot; *muh-LIF-loo-uhs*
**Русский:** сладкозвучный, медоточивый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more mellifluous*, *most mellifluous*

Smooth and pleasant to listen to.

1. The announcer has a *mellifluous* voice.
2. Her prose is *mellifluous* and easy to read aloud.
3. He sang the last verse in a low, *mellifluous* tone.

---

### mellow

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɛloʊ/ &middot; *MEL-oh*
**Русский:** мягкий, спокойный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est or more/most — *mellower*, *mellowest*

Mellow has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Calm and relaxed, and not easily upset.** (спокойный, расслабленный)

1. He has gotten *mellower* since he retired.
2. It was a *mellow* Sunday afternoon.

**2. Soft and rich — of a sound, a light, a taste or a color.** (мягкий, приглушённый)

3. The wine is smooth and *mellow*.
4. A *mellow* light came through the curtains.

---

### melodic

**Pronunciation:** /məˈlɑːdɪk/ &middot; *muh-LOD-ik*
**Русский:** мелодичный; мелодический
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more melodic*, *most melodic*

Having a pleasant tune, or having to do with melody.

1. The band's new album is more *melodic* than the last one.
2. She writes the *melodic* line first and the words later.
3. His voice is deep and *melodic*.

---

### melodious

**Pronunciation:** /məˈloʊdiəs/ &middot; *muh-LOH-dee-uhs*
**Русский:** мелодичный, благозвучный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more melodious*, *most melodious*
**Contrast:** *Melodious* describes a sound that is pleasant; *melodic* also covers anything to do with melody (*the melodic line*).

Pleasant to hear; full of melody.

1. A *melodious* birdsong woke us at dawn.
2. Her laugh is low and *melodious*.
3. The choir sounded *melodious* even without the organ.

---

### melodramatic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɛlədrəˈmætɪk/ &middot; *mel-uh-druh-MAT-ik*
**Русский:** мелодраматичный, наигранный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more melodramatic*, *most melodramatic*
**Form:** from *melodrama*

Showing far more emotion than the situation deserves.

1. Don't be so *melodramatic* — it's only a scratch.
2. The ending is *melodramatic*, but the acting saves it.
3. She made a *melodramatic* exit, slamming the door behind her.

---

### melted

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɛltɪd/ &middot; *MEL-tid*
**Русский:** растопленный, расплавленный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *melt*
**Contrast:** *Melted* is the finished state (*melted butter*); *molten* is used for metal and rock that is hot and liquid (*molten lava*).

Turned into liquid by heat.

1. Pour the *melted* butter over the popcorn.
2. The sandwich comes with *melted* cheese.
3. By noon the snow was *melted* and the streets were wet.

---

### memorable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɛmərəbəl/ &middot; *MEM-uh-ruh-buhl*
**Русский:** запоминающийся, памятный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more memorable*, *most memorable*
**Form:** -able from *memory*
**Opposite:** *forgettable*

Worth remembering, or easy to remember.

1. It was a *memorable* evening.
2. The speech was short but *memorable*.
3. Few songs are as *memorable* as that one.

---

### menacing

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɛnəsɪŋ/ &middot; *MEN-uh-sing*
**Русский:** угрожающий, грозный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more menacing*, *most menacing*
**Form:** -ing from *menace*

Looking or sounding as if harm is coming.

1. A *menacing* dog stood at the gate.
2. The sky turned dark and *menacing*.
3. He took a step forward and said something in a *menacing* voice.

---

### menial

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmiːniəl/ &middot; *MEE-nee-uhl*
**Русский:** чёрный (о работе), низкоквалифицированный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** more/most — *more menial*, *most menial*

Of work: boring, low-paid and seen as having little status.

1. He took a *menial* job to pay for school.
2. New interns get the most *menial* tasks.
3. She was tired of *menial* work and went back to college.

---

### menopausal

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɛnəˈpɔːzəl/ &middot; *men-uh-PAW-zuhl*
**Русский:** климактерический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** from *menopause*

Connected with menopause, the end of a woman's monthly periods.

1. She is being treated for *menopausal* symptoms.
2. The study followed two hundred *menopausal* women.
3. Hot flashes are a common *menopausal* complaint.

---

### menstrual

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɛnstruəl/ &middot; *MEN-stroo-uhl*
**Русский:** менструальный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** from *menstruation*

Connected with the monthly bleeding of a woman's body.

1. Her *menstrual* cycle is very regular.
2. The app tracks *menstrual* symptoms.
3. *Menstrual* cramps kept her home from school.

---

### mental

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɛntəl/ &middot; *MEN-tuhl*
**Русский:** умственный, психический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** In American English *mental* is not used to mean *crazy* — that is British slang.

Mental has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Connected with the mind and how it works.** (психический, умственный)

1. *Mental* health is finally being taken seriously.
2. He did the whole calculation in his head — pure *mental* arithmetic.

**2. Existing in the mind rather than on paper.** (мысленный)

3. I made a *mental* note to call her back.
4. She keeps a *mental* list of everything the kids need.

---

### mentally ill

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɛntəli ˈɪl/ &middot; *MEN-tuh-lee IL*
**Русский:** психически больной
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable — degree goes in the adverb: *severely mentally ill*
**Form:** compound — adverb *mentally* + adjective *ill*
**Contrast:** As a group noun it takes *the* and a plural verb: *the mentally ill are often homeless*. Many writers now prefer *people with mental illness*.

Suffering from an illness of the mind.

1. He was found to be *mentally ill* and sent for treatment.
2. The shelter serves homeless and *mentally ill* adults.
3. She is not *mentally ill*; she is exhausted.

---

### mercenary

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɜːrsənɛri/ &middot; *MUR-suh-ner-ee*
**Русский:** корыстный, продажный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more mercenary*, *most mercenary*
**Contrast:** Also a noun: a *mercenary* is a soldier who fights for whoever pays him.

Interested only in money, not in doing what is right.

1. His motives were purely *mercenary*.
2. Critics accused the studio of *mercenary* decisions.
3. There is something *mercenary* about the way he picks his friends.

---

### merciful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɜːrsɪfəl/ &middot; *MUR-si-fuhl*
**Русский:** милосердный; спасительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more merciful*, *most merciful*
**Takes:** *to* + noun
**Form:** -ful from *mercy*
**Opposite:** *merciless*

Merciful has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Willing to forgive, or to spare someone pain or punishment.** (милосердный, сострадательный)

1. The judge was *merciful* and gave him probation.
2. Be *merciful* to the ones who made an honest mistake.

**2. Of an event: a relief, because it ends something painful.** (спасительный, избавляющий от страданий)

3. His death was quick and *merciful*.
4. After ten days of heat, the rain was *merciful*.

---

### merciless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɜːrsɪləs/ &middot; *MUR-si-luhs*
**Русский:** безжалостный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more merciless*, *most merciless*
**Form:** -less from *mercy*
**Opposite:** *merciful*

Showing no pity at all.

1. The teasing at school was *merciless*.
2. She is a *merciless* editor — half my draft is gone.
3. The August sun was *merciless*.

---

### mercurial

**Pronunciation:** /mərˈkjʊriəl/ &middot; *mur-KYUUR-ee-uhl*
**Русский:** непостоянный, переменчивый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more mercurial*, *most mercurial*

Changing mood or behavior suddenly and often.

1. The coach is brilliant but *mercurial*.
2. Her *mercurial* temper kept the whole office on edge.
3. Stock prices have been *mercurial* all week.

---

### meritocratic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɛrɪtəˈkrætɪk/ &middot; *mer-i-tuh-KRAT-ik*
**Русский:** меритократический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more meritocratic*, *most meritocratic*
**Form:** from *meritocracy*

Based on ability and effort rather than on money or family.

1. The company claims to have a *meritocratic* promotion system.
2. Admissions are supposed to be *meritocratic*.
3. He argues the country is less *meritocratic* than it pretends.

---

### merry

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɛri/ &middot; *MER-ee*
**Русский:** весёлый, радостный
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *merrier*, *merriest*
**Contrast:** Outside *Merry Christmas* and a few set phrases, Americans say *cheerful* or *happy*.

Cheerful and full of good spirits.

1. *Merry* Christmas and a happy New Year!
2. A *merry* crowd filled the square.
3. The more people come, the *merrier* the party.

---

### mesmerized

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɛzməraɪzd/ &middot; *MEZ-muh-ryzd*
**Русский:** заворожённый, загипнотизированный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable — say *completely mesmerized*
**Takes:** *by* + noun
**Form:** -ed — how the person feels
**Opposite:** *bored*
**Contrast:** *Mesmerized* is the person; *mesmerizing* is the thing that does it to them.

Unable to look away or think about anything else.

1. The kids sat *mesmerized* in front of the fire.
2. I was *mesmerized* by her voice.
3. He watched the waves, completely *mesmerized*.

---

### mesmerizing

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɛzməraɪzɪŋ/ &middot; *MEZ-muh-ry-zing*
**Русский:** завораживающий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more mesmerizing*, *most mesmerizing*
**Form:** -ing — what causes the feeling
**Contrast:** Pairs with *mesmerized*: the film is *mesmerizing*, so the audience is *mesmerized*.

So beautiful or strange that you cannot look away.

1. The dancer was *mesmerizing*.
2. It is a *mesmerizing* film about life at sea.
3. There is something *mesmerizing* about watching bread rise.

---

### messianic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɛsiˈænɪk/ &middot; *mes-ee-AN-ik*
**Русский:** мессианский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more messianic*, *most messianic*
**Form:** from *Messiah*

Messianic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Acting as if you had been chosen to save everyone.** (мессианский, одержимый великой миссией)

1. He talks about the company with *messianic* zeal.
2. The movement took on a *messianic* tone.

**2. Connected with the Messiah in religion.** (мессианский)

3. The professor writes about *messianic* prophecy.
4. *Messianic* expectations ran high in the first century.

---

### messy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɛsi/ &middot; *MES-ee*
**Русский:** неопрятный; запутанный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *messier*, *messiest*
**Form:** -y from *mess*
**Opposite:** *tidy*

Messy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Dirty, or not in any order.** (неопрятный, грязный)

1. His room is *messier* than mine.
2. Sorry, the kitchen is a little *messy* right now.

**2. Complicated and unpleasant to deal with.** (запутанный, неприятный)

3. They went through a *messy* divorce.
4. The negotiations got *messy*.

---

### metabolic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɛtəˈbɑːlɪk/ &middot; *met-uh-BOL-ik*
**Русский:** метаболический, обменный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** from *metabolism*

Connected with the chemical processes that keep a body alive.

1. She was diagnosed with a rare *metabolic* disorder.
2. Exercise raises your *metabolic* rate.
3. The drug affects *metabolic* function in the liver.

---

### metaphorical

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɛtəˈfɔːrɪkəl/ &middot; *met-uh-FOR-i-kuhl*
**Русский:** метафорический, переносный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** from *metaphor*
**Opposite:** *literal*

Using an image to mean something else; not meant literally.

1. The wall he keeps talking about is *metaphorical*, not made of bricks.
2. It was a *metaphorical* way of saying he was worn out.
3. Her poems are dense and *metaphorical*.

---

### metaphysical

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɛtəˈfɪzɪkəl/ &middot; *met-uh-FIZ-i-kuhl*
**Русский:** метафизический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable

Concerned with what exists and what is real, beyond the physical world.

1. The book asks *metaphysical* questions about time and the self.
2. We got into a long *metaphysical* argument at dinner.
3. The problem is practical, not *metaphysical*.

---

### meteoric

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmiːtiˈɔːrɪk/ &middot; *mee-tee-OR-ik*
**Русский:** стремительный, головокружительный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** from *meteor*
**Contrast:** Almost always in *a meteoric rise*: fast and bright, never falling.

Of success: very fast and spectacular.

1. Her *meteoric* rise took her from intern to partner in six years.
2. The band's *meteoric* success surprised everyone, including the band.
3. After a *meteoric* start, the company ran out of money.

---

### meteorological

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmiːtiərəˈlɑːdʒɪkəl/ &middot; *mee-tee-ur-uh-LOJ-i-kuhl*
**Русский:** метеорологический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** from *meteorology*

Connected with the weather and the study of it.

1. The delay was caused by *meteorological* conditions.
2. He works at a *meteorological* station in Alaska.
3. *Meteorological* records here go back to 1890.

---

### methodical

**Pronunciation:** /məˈθɑːdɪkəl/ &middot; *muh-THOD-i-kuhl*
**Русский:** методичный, планомерный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more methodical*, *most methodical*
**Form:** from *method*

Doing things in a careful, ordered way, one step at a time.

1. She is *methodical* about everything, from packing to paying bills.
2. The search was slow and *methodical*.
3. He keeps *methodical* notes on every patient.

---

### Methodist

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɛθədɪst/ &middot; *METH-uh-dist*
**Русский:** методистский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Always capitalized. Also a noun: *she was raised a Methodist*.

Belonging to the Methodist branch of the Protestant church.

1. They were married in a *Methodist* church.
2. His grandfather was a *Methodist* minister.
3. The town has a small *Methodist* congregation.

---

### methodological

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɛθədəˈlɑːdʒɪkəl/ &middot; *meth-uh-duh-LOJ-i-kuhl*
**Русский:** методологический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** from *methodology*
**Contrast:** *Methodological* is about the method of a study; *methodical* is about a careful person.

Connected with the methods used in research or work.

1. The paper has serious *methodological* problems.
2. Reviewers raised *methodological* objections.
3. One *methodological* change gave them a completely different result.

---

### meticulous

**Pronunciation:** /məˈtɪkjələs/ &middot; *muh-TIK-yuh-luhs*
**Русский:** дотошный, скрупулёзный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more meticulous*, *most meticulous*
**Takes:** *about* + noun or *-ing*; *in* + noun
**Opposite:** *careless*

Very careful about small details.

1. He is *meticulous* about his tools.
2. The restoration took years of *meticulous* work.
3. She kept *meticulous* records of every dollar spent.

---

### metric

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɛtrɪk/ &middot; *MET-rik*
**Русский:** метрический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun in business English: a *metric* is a number you measure success by.

Using meters, grams and liters.

1. Most of the world uses the *metric* system.
2. The recipe gives *metric* measurements.
3. A *metric* ton is a thousand kilograms.

---

### metropolitan

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɛtrəˈpɑːlətən/ &middot; *met-ruh-POL-uh-tuhn*
**Русский:** столичный; относящийся к крупному городу
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** from *metropolis*

Belonging to a large city and the area around it.

1. Three million people live in the *metropolitan* area.
2. The *metropolitan* police handled the case.
3. *Metropolitan* voters went the other way.

---

### microeconomic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmaɪkroʊˌɛkəˈnɑːmɪk/ &middot; *my-kroh-ek-uh-NOM-ik*
**Русский:** микроэкономический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** micro- + *economic*
**Opposite:** *macroeconomic*

Concerned with single firms, households and markets rather than the whole economy.

1. The paper takes a *microeconomic* approach to health care.
2. *Microeconomic* theory starts with supply and demand.
3. She teaches a *microeconomic* analysis course.

---

### microscopic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmaɪkrəˈskɑːpɪk/ &middot; *my-kruh-SKOP-ik*
**Русский:** микроскопический
**Type:** size
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** from *microscope*

Microscopic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Far too small to see without a microscope.** (микроскопический)

1. *Microscopic* cracks had appeared in the metal.
2. The water is full of *microscopic* life.

**2. Extremely small, in everyday exaggeration.** (крошечный, мизерный)

3. They served us *microscopic* portions.
4. The chance of winning is *microscopic*.

---

### Mid-Atlantic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɪd ətˈlæntɪk/ &middot; *mid-uht-LAN-tik*
**Русский:** среднеатлантический (о штатах США)
**Type:** origin
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *mid* + *Atlantic*
**Contrast:** In American use it means the states from New York down to Virginia, not the middle of the ocean.

Belonging to the middle part of the US Atlantic coast.

1. Snow is expected across the *Mid-Atlantic* states.
2. She grew up in a *Mid-Atlantic* suburb.
3. The chain has thirty *Mid-Atlantic* stores.

---

### middle

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɪdəl/ &middot; *MID-uhl*
**Русский:** средний, срединный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** The adjective only goes before a noun. After a verb use the noun: *The house is in the middle of the block*, never *the house is middle*.

In the center, between two other things or points.

1. She is the *middle* child of three.
2. Put the box on the *middle* shelf.
3. We met in *middle* school.

---

### Middle Eastern

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɪdəl ˈiːstərn/ &middot; *MID-uhl EE-sturn*
**Русский:** ближневосточный
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Both words are capitalized, and there is no hyphen.

Belonging to the countries of the Middle East.

1. They opened a *Middle Eastern* restaurant downtown.
2. He covers *Middle Eastern* politics for the paper.
3. The spice blend is distinctly *Middle Eastern*.

---

### middle-class

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɪdəl ˈklæs/ &middot; *mid-uhl-KLAS*
**Русский:** относящийся к среднему классу
**Type:** compound; also classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — *middle* + *class*
**Contrast:** Hyphenated before a noun; as a noun phrase it is two words: *the middle class is shrinking*.

Belonging to the group of people between the rich and the poor.

1. They grew up in a comfortable *middle-class* neighborhood.
2. The bill offers a tax cut to *middle-class* families.
3. She writes about *middle-class* anxiety.

---

### middle-of-the-road

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɪdəl əv ðə ˈroʊd/ &middot; *mid-uhl-uhv-thuh-ROHD*
**Русский:** умеренный, центристский
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — a whole phrase used as one adjective

Moderate; avoiding either extreme, especially in politics or taste.

1. He ran as a *middle-of-the-road* Democrat.
2. The station plays *middle-of-the-road* pop.
3. Her views are pretty *middle-of-the-road*.

---

### middling

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɪdlɪŋ/ &middot; *MID-ling*
**Русский:** посредственный, средненький
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Often in the set phrase *fair to middling*, meaning so-so.

Of only average quality or size — neither good nor bad.

1. The movie got *middling* reviews.
2. He had a *middling* career and retired quietly.
3. The team is coming off a *middling* season.

---

### Midwestern

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɪdˈwɛstərn/ &middot; *mid-WES-turn*
**Русский:** среднезападный (США)
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Capitalized. The region is *the Midwest*; a person from there is a *Midwesterner*.

Belonging to the Midwest of the United States.

1. She has a flat *Midwestern* accent.
2. The company started in a small *Midwestern* town.
3. His politeness is very *Midwestern*.

---

### miffed

**Pronunciation:** /mɪft/ &middot; *MIFT*
**Русский:** обиженный, раздосадованный
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** predicative only
**Comparison:** more/most — *more miffed*, *most miffed*
**Takes:** *at* or *about* + noun; *that*-clause
**Form:** -ed from the verb *miff*
**Contrast:** Informal and mild — being *miffed* is much weaker than being *angry*.

Slightly annoyed, usually at being left out or overlooked.

1. She was *miffed* that nobody told her about the meeting.
2. He looked a little *miffed* at being asked to wait.
3. I was *miffed* about the seating, but I got over it.

---

### mighty

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmaɪti/ &middot; *MY-tee*
**Русский:** могучий, мощный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *mightier*, *mightiest*
**Form:** -y from *might*
**Contrast:** In the South and in older writing *mighty* is also an adverb meaning *very* (*mighty fine*). That is not the adjective.

Very strong and powerful.

1. The *mighty* Mississippi runs past the town.
2. The pen is *mightier* than the sword.
3. He swung at the ball with a *mighty* effort.

---

### migrant

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmaɪɡrənt/ &middot; *MY-gruhnt*
**Русский:** сезонный, кочующий; мигрантский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Far more common as a noun (*migrants crossed the border*); the adjective only goes before a noun.

Moving from place to place, especially in search of work.

1. *Migrant* workers pick most of the state's fruit.
2. The county opened a clinic for *migrant* families.
3. The film follows a *migrant* family across three states.

---

### migratory

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmaɪɡrətɔːri/ &middot; *MY-gruh-tor-ee*
**Русский:** перелётный, мигрирующий
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** from *migrate*

Regularly moving from one region to another with the seasons.

1. The lake is a stop for *migratory* birds.
2. *Migratory* patterns have shifted with the warmer winters.
3. Some of these whales are *migratory*; others stay all year.

---

### mild

**Pronunciation:** /maɪld/ &middot; *MYLD*
**Русский:** мягкий, слабый; неострый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *milder*, *mildest*
**Opposite:** *severe*; *spicy* (of food)

Mild has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Not strong and not severe.** (лёгкий, слабый)

1. He had a *mild* case of the flu.
2. This winter is *milder* than last year's.

**2. Of food: not hot or spicy.** (неострый)

3. I'll take the *mild* salsa, please.
4. The cheese is *mild* and creamy.

**3. Of a person: gentle and not easily angered.** (кроткий, мягкий)

5. He is a *mild* man who never raises his voice.
6. She answered in a *mild* tone.

---

### mild-mannered

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmaɪld ˈmænərd/ &middot; *myld-MAN-urd*
**Русский:** кроткий, мягкий по характеру
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more mild-mannered*, *most mild-mannered*
**Form:** compound — *mild* + *mannered*, from *manner*

Quiet and gentle in the way you deal with people.

1. He is a *mild-mannered* accountant from Ohio.
2. Our *mild-mannered* neighbor turned out to be a champion boxer.
3. She is *mild-mannered* until you insult her cooking.

---

### militant

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɪlətənt/ &middot; *MIL-uh-tuhnt*
**Русский:** воинствующий, боевой
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more militant*, *most militant*
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *militants attacked the base*.

Willing to use forceful or violent methods for a cause.

1. A *militant* faction split off from the party.
2. He became more *militant* after the strike failed.
3. She is a *militant* defender of public libraries.

---

### militaristic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɪlətəˈrɪstɪk/ &middot; *mil-uh-tuh-RIS-tik*
**Русский:** милитаристский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more militaristic*, *most militaristic*
**Form:** from *militarism*

Believing a country should build up its army and be ready to use it.

1. The regime grew steadily more *militaristic*.
2. Textbooks described the empire as *militaristic*.
3. He objects to the *militaristic* tone of the ceremony.

---

### militarized

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɪlətəraɪzd/ &middot; *MIL-uh-tuh-ryzd*
**Русский:** милитаризованный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *militarize*
**Opposite:** *demilitarized*

Supplied with soldiers, weapons or military methods.

1. The border has become heavily *militarized*.
2. Critics say local police are too *militarized*.
3. They crossed a *militarized* zone to reach the camp.

---

### military

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɪlətɛri/ &middot; *MIL-uh-ter-ee*
**Русский:** военный, воинский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *the military* means the armed forces and always takes *the*.

Connected with soldiers and the armed forces.

1. He spent four years in *military* service.
2. The base is under *military* control.
3. *Military* families move often.

---

### milky

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɪlki/ &middot; *MIL-kee*
**Русский:** молочный, молочно-белый
**Type:** quality; also color
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *milkier*, *milkiest*
**Form:** -y from *milk*

Milky has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Pale white and not clear, like milk.** (молочно-белый, мутноватый)

1. The stone turns *milky* when it gets wet.
2. A *milky* haze hung over the bay.

**2. Containing or tasting of milk.** (молочный, с молоком)

3. She likes her tea *milky*.
4. The sauce came out *milkier* than I expected.

---

### millennial

**Pronunciation:** /məˈlɛniəl/ &middot; *muh-LEN-ee-uhl*
**Русский:** относящийся к поколению миллениалов; тысячелетний
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Two *l*s and two *n*s. Also a noun: *millennials* are people born roughly between 1981 and 1996.

Millennial has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Belonging to the generation born in the 1980s and 1990s.** (относящийся к миллениалам)

1. The ad is aimed at *millennial* shoppers.
2. Her taste in music is very *millennial*.

**2. Connected with a period of a thousand years.** (тысячелетний)

3. The cathedral held a *millennial* celebration.
4. He writes about *millennial* prophecies.

---

### mind-altering

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmaɪnd ˌɔːltərɪŋ/ &middot; *MYND-awl-tuh-ring*
**Русский:** изменяющий сознание
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun *mind* + *-ing* participle

Changing the way your mind works — of a drug or an experience.

1. The study looked at *mind-altering* drugs.
2. He described the trip as a *mind-altering* experience.
3. Some *mind-altering* substances are legal in the state.

---

### mind-bending

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmaɪnd ˌbɛndɪŋ/ &middot; *MYND-ben-ding*
**Русский:** умопомрачительный, выносящий мозг
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more mind-bending*, *most mind-bending*
**Form:** compound — noun *mind* + *-ing* participle

So strange or complicated that it is hard to take in.

1. The last episode has a *mind-bending* twist.
2. The math involved is frankly *mind-bending*.
3. It is a *mind-bending* movie that rewards a second viewing.

---

### mind-blowing

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmaɪnd ˌbloʊɪŋ/ &middot; *MYND-bloh-ing*
**Русский:** потрясающий, сносящий крышу
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more mind-blowing*, *most mind-blowing*
**Form:** compound — noun *mind* + *-ing* participle
**Contrast:** Informal, and usually praise. *Mind-boggling* is about size or confusion instead.

Amazing; far beyond what you expected.

1. The view from the top is *mind-blowing*.
2. She gave a *mind-blowing* performance.
3. The price of housing here is *mind-blowing*.

---

### mind-boggling

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmaɪnd ˌbɑːɡəlɪŋ/ &middot; *MYND-bog-uh-ling*
**Русский:** невообразимый, не укладывающийся в голове
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more mind-boggling*, *most mind-boggling*
**Form:** compound — noun *mind* + *-ing* participle

So large or complicated that you cannot really imagine it.

1. The numbers are *mind-boggling*: nine billion dollars a year.
2. It took a *mind-boggling* amount of paperwork.
3. The scale of the project is *mind-boggling*.

---

### mindful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmaɪndfəl/ &middot; *MYND-fuhl*
**Русский:** помнящий, внимательный; осознанный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more mindful*, *most mindful*
**Takes:** *of* + noun; *that*-clause
**Preposition patterns:** [mindful of](../../06-prepositions/catalog/15-dependent-adjectives.md#mindful-of)
**Form:** -ful from *mind*
**Opposite:** *heedless*

Mindful has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Keeping something in mind; aware of it.** (помнящий, учитывающий)

1. Be *mindful* of the step on your way out.
2. She spoke slowly, *mindful* that children were listening.

**2. Paying calm, deliberate attention to the present moment.** (осознанный)

3. He practices *mindful* breathing every morning.
