4. The clinic teaches *mindful* eating.

---

### mindless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmaɪndləs/ &middot; *MYND-luhs*
**Русский:** бездумный; бессмысленный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more mindless*, *most mindless*
**Form:** -less from *mind*

Mindless has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Needing no thought, or done without any.** (бездумный, механический)

1. After work I want some *mindless* television.
2. The job is *mindless*, but it pays.

**2. Senseless and violent, with no reason behind it.** (бессмысленный, дикий)

3. The riot was *mindless* destruction.
4. Police called it a *mindless* attack.

---

### mineral

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɪnərəl/ &middot; *MIN-uh-ruhl*
**Русский:** минеральный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun (*iron is a mineral*); as an adjective it only goes before a noun.

Made of minerals, or containing them.

1. She drinks sparkling *mineral* water.
2. The state sold the *mineral* rights to the land.
3. *Mineral* deposits line the inside of the kettle.

---

### miniature

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɪniətʃər/ &middot; *MIN-ee-uh-chur*
**Русский:** миниатюрный, крошечный
**Type:** size
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *a portrait painted in miniature*.

Much smaller than usual, but the same in every other way.

1. He collects *miniature* trains.
2. They keep a *miniature* poodle.
3. The museum has a *miniature* version of the whole city.

---

### minimal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɪnəməl/ &middot; *MIN-uh-muhl*
**Русский:** минимальный, ничтожный
**Type:** quantity
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *maximal*

As small as possible; very little.

1. The damage to the car was *minimal*.
2. The treatment has *minimal* side effects.
3. We finished with *minimal* help from the main office.

---

### minimalist

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɪnəməlɪst/ &middot; *MIN-uh-muh-list*
**Русский:** минималистский
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more minimalist*, *most minimalist*
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *she is a minimalist*.

Using very few and very simple elements.

1. The apartment has a *minimalist* design — a bed, a lamp, nothing else.
2. His style is deliberately *minimalist*.
3. They gave the album a *minimalist* cover.

---

### minimum

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɪnəməm/ &middot; *MIN-uh-muhm*
**Русский:** минимальный
**Type:** quantity
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *maximum*
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *a minimum of three years*. The fixed phrase is *minimum wage*, never *minimal wage*.

The smallest that is allowed or required.

1. He works two jobs at *minimum* wage.
2. There is a *minimum* age of eighteen.
3. The order carries a *minimum* charge of twenty dollars.

---

### minor

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmaɪnər/ &middot; *MY-nur*
**Русский:** незначительный, мелкий
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more minor*, *most minor*
**Opposite:** *major*
**Contrast:** Also a noun: a *minor* is a person under eighteen. In music the word follows the note: *A minor*.

Small and not very important or serious.

1. She had a *minor* injury and played the next day.
2. There are a few *minor* changes to the schedule.
3. The problem is *minor* compared with what we faced last year.

---

### mint

**Pronunciation:** /mɪnt/ &middot; *MINT*
**Русский:** новенький, как новый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun — the plant, the candy, and the place where coins are made. The flavor adjective is *minty*, not *mint*.

In perfect, unused condition, as if just made.

1. The comic is in *mint* condition.
2. He bought a *mint* 1965 Mustang.
3. Collectors pay extra for a *mint* copy.

---

### minty

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɪnti/ &middot; *MIN-tee*
**Русский:** мятный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *mintier*, *mintiest*
**Form:** -y from *mint*

Tasting or smelling of mint.

1. The toothpaste has a strong *minty* taste.
2. She made a cool, *minty* drink.
3. This gum is *mintier* than the other brand.

---

### minuscule

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɪnəskjuːl/ &middot; *MIN-uh-skyool*
**Русский:** крошечный, ничтожный
**Type:** size
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Spelled *minu-*, not *mini-*, even though *miniscule* is now common in print.

Extremely small.

1. The print on the contract is *minuscule*.
2. He got a *minuscule* raise.
3. The risk of that happening is *minuscule*.

---

### minute

**Pronunciation:** /maɪˈnuːt/ &middot; *my-NOOT*
**Русский:** мельчайший, крошечный; подробнейший
**Type:** size
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more minute*, *most minute*
**Contrast:** The adjective is my-NOOT; the noun *minute* (sixty seconds) is MIN-it. Same spelling, different word.

Minute has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Very small indeed.** (мельчайший, ничтожно малый)

1. *Minute* traces of the metal were found in the water.
2. The difference between the two samples is *minute*.

**2. Going into the smallest details.** (детальнейший, скрупулёзный)

3. She described the room in *minute* detail.
4. The report gives a *minute* account of the accident.

---

### miraculous

**Pronunciation:** /məˈrækjələs/ &middot; *muh-RAK-yuh-luhs*
**Русский:** чудесный, чудодейственный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more miraculous*, *most miraculous*
**Form:** from *miracle*

So lucky or surprising that it seems like a miracle.

1. She made a *miraculous* recovery.
2. It is *miraculous* that nobody was hurt.
3. The team's comeback was nothing short of *miraculous*.

---

### mired

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmaɪərd/ &middot; *MY-urd*
**Русский:** увязший, погрязший
**Type:** participial
**Position:** predicative only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *in* + noun
**Form:** -ed from *mire*

Stuck in a bad situation you cannot get out of.

1. The bill is *mired* in committee.
2. The company is *mired* in debt.
3. For two years the project was *mired* in lawsuits.

---

### mirrored

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɪrərd/ &middot; *MIR-urd*
**Русский:** зеркальный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from the noun *mirror*

Covered with mirrors, or made of mirror glass.

1. The gym has one *mirrored* wall.
2. He wore *mirrored* sunglasses.
3. The tower's *mirrored* windows reflect the whole skyline.

---

### misanthropic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɪsənˈθrɑːpɪk/ &middot; *mis-uhn-THROP-ik*
**Русский:** мизантропический, человеконенавистнический
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more misanthropic*, *most misanthropic*
**Form:** from *misanthrope*

Disliking people in general and avoiding them.

1. He has grown *misanthropic* since he moved to the cabin.
2. The novel's narrator is funny and deeply *misanthropic*.
3. She is not *misanthropic*, just tired of small talk.

---

### miscellaneous

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɪsəˈleɪniəs/ &middot; *mis-uh-LAY-nee-uhs*
**Русский:** разный, разнообразный, прочий
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable

Of many different kinds, grouped together because they fit nowhere else.

1. The drawer is full of *miscellaneous* junk.
2. *Miscellaneous* expenses came to sixty dollars.
3. She filed the letters under *miscellaneous* correspondence.

---

### mischievous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɪstʃɪvəs/ &middot; *MIS-chi-vuhs*
**Русский:** озорной, шаловливый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more mischievous*, *most mischievous*
**Form:** from *mischief*
**Contrast:** Three syllables, MIS-chi-vuhs — not *mis-CHEE-vee-uhs* — and spelled *-vous*, not *-vious*.

Enjoying causing small amounts of trouble, in a playful way.

1. He gave her a *mischievous* grin.
2. The puppy is *mischievous* but harmless.
3. She had a *mischievous* streak as a child.

---

### miserable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɪzərəbəl/ &middot; *MIZ-uh-ruh-buhl*
**Русский:** несчастный; отвратительный
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more miserable*, *most miserable*
**Form:** from *misery*

Miserable has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Extremely unhappy or uncomfortable.** (несчастный, подавленный)

1. She was *miserable* the whole first year at the new school.
2. I had a cold and felt *miserable*.

**2. Very bad or unpleasant — of weather or conditions.** (отвратительный, скверный)

3. It was a *miserable* November day, cold and wet.
4. They played the whole game in *miserable* conditions.

---

### miserly

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmaɪzərli/ &middot; *MY-zur-lee*
**Русский:** скупой, скаредный; мизерный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both — mostly before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more miserly*, *most miserly*
**Form:** -ly from the noun *miser* — an adjective, not an adverb
**Opposite:** *generous*
**Contrast:** *Miserly* ends in *-ly* but is an adjective, like *friendly* and *lovely*.

Miserly has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Hating to spend money.** (скупой, скаредный)

1. His *miserly* uncle heated only one room of the house.
2. She is careful with money; he is simply *miserly*.

**2. Of an amount: much too small.** (мизерный)

3. The raise was a *miserly* one percent.
4. They offered a *miserly* two days off.

---

### misguided

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɪsˈɡaɪdɪd/ &middot; *mis-GY-did*
**Русский:** ошибочный; заблуждающийся
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more misguided*, *most misguided*
**Form:** mis- + *guided*

Based on bad judgment or wrong information, though usually well meant.

1. It was a *misguided* attempt to help.
2. He is sincere but badly *misguided*.
3. The policy was *misguided* from the start.

---

### mislabeled

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɪsˈleɪbəld/ &middot; *mis-LAY-buhld*
**Русский:** неправильно маркированный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** mis- + *labeled* — American spelling has one *l*

Given the wrong name or description on its label.

1. The jars were *mislabeled* and had to be pulled from the shelves.
2. He bought a *mislabeled* bottle of olive oil.
3. One *mislabeled* file cost us two days.

---

### misleading

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɪsˈliːdɪŋ/ &middot; *mis-LEE-ding*
**Русский:** вводящий в заблуждение, обманчивый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more misleading*, *most misleading*
**Form:** mis- + *leading*, from *mislead*

Giving a wrong idea, whether on purpose or not.

1. The ad was found to be *misleading*.
2. That chart is *misleading* because the scale starts at fifty.
3. He gave a *misleading* answer about where he had been.

---

### mismatched

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɪsˈmætʃt/ &middot; *mis-MACHT*
**Русский:** несочетающийся, неподходящий друг другу
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** mis- + *matched*

Not going together well; wrongly paired.

1. He showed up in *mismatched* socks.
2. The two fighters were badly *mismatched*.
3. The furniture is *mismatched*, but somehow it works.

---

### misogynistic

**Pronunciation:** /mɪˌsɑːdʒəˈnɪstɪk/ &middot; *mi-sah-juh-NIS-tik*
**Русский:** женоненавистнический
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more misogynistic*, *most misogynistic*
**Form:** from *misogyny*

Hating women, or treating them with contempt.

1. The lyrics were widely called *misogynistic*.
2. He posted a string of *misogynistic* comments.
3. She left a workplace culture she described as deeply *misogynistic*.

---

### misplaced

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɪsˈpleɪst/ &middot; *mis-PLAYST*
**Русский:** неуместный; положенный не туда
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** mis- + *placed*

Misplaced has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Given to the wrong person or cause, and so wasted.** (неуместный, напрасный)

1. Her trust in him turned out to be *misplaced*.
2. The company showed a *misplaced* confidence in its own product.
3. His loyalty was admirable but *misplaced*.

**2. Put somewhere it does not belong, so that it cannot be found.** (затерявшийся, положенный не туда)

4. The *misplaced* file finally turned up in the wrong drawer.
5. A *misplaced* comma changed the meaning of the whole sentence.

---

### misshapen

**Pronunciation:** /mɪsˈʃeɪpən/ &middot; *mis-SHAY-puhn*
**Русский:** бесформенный, деформированный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more misshapen*, *most misshapen*
**Form:** mis- + *shapen*, an old participle of *shape*

Badly or unnaturally shaped.

1. The tree's branches were twisted and *misshapen*.
2. He pulled a *misshapen* loaf out of the oven.
3. Years of ballet had left her toes *misshapen*.

---

### missing

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɪsɪŋ/ &middot; *MIS-ing*
**Русский:** отсутствующий; пропавший без вести
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both — also after the noun: *the money missing from the safe*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *Missing* can follow its noun: *the pages missing from the file*.

Missing has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Not in the place where it should be.** (отсутствующий, недостающий)

1. Two pieces of the puzzle are *missing*.
2. She filled in the *missing* words.
3. There is a button *missing* from your coat.

**2. Gone and not found, especially a person after a disappearance.** (пропавший без вести)

4. Three hikers have been *missing* since Sunday.
5. The poster showed a photo of the *missing* girl.

---

### mistaken

**Pronunciation:** /mɪˈsteɪkən/ &middot; *mi-STAY-kuhn*
**Русский:** ошибающийся; ошибочный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Takes:** *about* + noun
**Form:** from the participle of *mistake*
**Contrast:** A person is *mistaken*; the thing itself is *a mistake*, a noun.

Mistaken has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Wrong about something, of a person.** (ошибающийся, неправый)

1. If you think this is easy, you are badly *mistaken*.
2. I was *mistaken* about the date.
3. Unless I am *mistaken*, that is her car.

**2. Based on a wrong idea, of a belief or an action.** (ошибочный)

4. It was a case of *mistaken* identity.
5. They acted out of a *mistaken* sense of duty.

---

### mistreated

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɪsˈtriːtɪd/ &middot; *mis-TREE-tid*
**Русский:** подвергающийся плохому обращению
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** mis- + *treated*

Treated badly or cruelly.

1. The *mistreated* dogs were taken to a shelter.
2. Workers who feel *mistreated* rarely stay long.
3. She testified that she had been *mistreated* in custody.

---

### mistrustful

**Pronunciation:** /mɪsˈtrʌstfəl/ &middot; *mis-TRUHST-fuhl*
**Русский:** недоверчивый
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both — usually after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more mistrustful*, *most mistrustful*
**Takes:** *of* + noun
**Form:** -ful from *mistrust*
**Opposite:** *trusting*

Not trusting someone or something.

1. He grew *mistrustful* of everyone around him.
2. Voters are increasingly *mistrustful* of polls.
3. She gave the stranger a *mistrustful* look.

---

### misty

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɪsti/ &middot; *MIS-tee*
**Русский:** туманный; затуманенный слезами
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *mistier*, *mistiest*
**Form:** -y from *mist*

Misty has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Full of mist, and so hard to see through.** (туманный)

1. It was a cold, *misty* morning on the lake.
2. The hills looked *mistier* than they had at dawn.

**2. Filled with tears, so that you cannot see clearly.** (затуманенный слезами)

3. Her eyes went *misty* when the music started.
4. He got a little *misty* talking about his father.

---

### misty-eyed

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɪstiˈaɪd/ &middot; *mis-tee-YD*
**Русский:** растроганный до слёз
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more misty-eyed*, *most misty-eyed*
**Form:** compound — noun + *-ed*

With tears in your eyes from emotion or fond memory.

1. Grandma got *misty-eyed* over the old photographs.
2. He turns *misty-eyed* whenever anyone mentions the 1970s.
3. There were a few *misty-eyed* parents in the audience.

---

### misunderstood

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɪsʌndərˈstʊd/ &middot; *mis-uhn-dur-STUUD*
**Русский:** непонятый, неверно понятый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more misunderstood*, *most misunderstood*
**Form:** mis- + *understood*

Not correctly understood by other people.

1. He felt lonely and *misunderstood* in high school.
2. It is one of the *most misunderstood* laws on the books.
3. Her remark was widely *misunderstood*.

---

### mixed

**Pronunciation:** /mɪkst/ &middot; *MIKST*
**Русский:** смешанный; неоднозначный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable

Mixed has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Made up of different kinds or different people.** (смешанный, разнородный)

1. We ordered a plate of *mixed* vegetables.
2. She teaches a *mixed* group of beginners and advanced students.

**2. Partly good and partly bad.** (неоднозначный, противоречивый)

3. The movie got *mixed* reviews.
4. I have *mixed* feelings about moving.
5. Reaction to the plan was *mixed*.

---

### mixed-up

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɪkstˈʌp/ &middot; *mikst-UHP*
**Русский:** сбитый с толку; перепутанный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more mixed-up*, *most mixed-up*
**Form:** compound — participle + adverb

Mixed-up has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Confused, or emotionally troubled.** (растерянный, запутавшийся)

1. He was a *mixed-up* kid who needed help, not punishment.
2. I got completely *mixed-up* and took the wrong exit.

**2. Jumbled together or out of order.** (перепутанный)

3. The pages of the report were all *mixed-up*.
4. Our suitcases got *mixed-up* at the airport.

---

### mobile

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmoʊbəl/ &middot; *MOH-buhl*
**Русский:** подвижный; передвижной
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more mobile*, *most mobile*
**Contrast:** Americans say *cell phone*, not *mobile phone*. A *mobile home* is a house that was towed to its lot.

Mobile has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Able to move, or to be moved, easily.** (подвижный, передвижной)

1. The clinic runs a *mobile* unit that visits small towns.
2. After the surgery she was much *more mobile*.

**2. Able to move to another place or another social class.** (мобильный)

3. Young engineers are a highly *mobile* workforce.
4. The country is less socially *mobile* than it used to be.

---

### mocking

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɑːkɪŋ/ &middot; *MAH-king*
**Русский:** насмешливый, издевательский
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more mocking*, *most mocking*
**Form:** -ing from *mock*

Making fun of someone in an unkind way.

1. He answered in a *mocking* tone.
2. She gave a *mocking* little bow.
3. The laughter from the crowd was openly *mocking*.

---

### moderate

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɑːdərət/ &middot; *MAH-dur-uht*
**Русский:** умеренный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more moderate*, *most moderate*
**Opposite:** *extreme*
**Contrast:** The adjective ends in a weak *-ate*, *MAH-dur-uht*; the verb *moderate* ends in *-ayt*, *MAH-duh-rayt*.

Moderate has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Medium in size, amount or strength.** (умеренный, средний)

1. Bake at a *moderate* temperature for an hour.
2. A *moderate* amount of exercise is enough.

**2. Holding political opinions that are not extreme.** (умеренный (о взглядах))

3. She won by appealing to *moderate* voters.
4. His views are far *more moderate* than his party's.

---

### modern

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɑːdərn/ &middot; *MAH-durn*
**Русский:** современный
**Type:** age
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more modern*, *most modern*
**Opposite:** *ancient*; *old-fashioned*

Modern has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Belonging to the present time or to recent times.** (современный, нынешний)

1. *Modern* medicine has changed what a broken hip means.
2. It is one of the great problems of *modern* life.

**2. Using the newest styles, methods or equipment.** (современный, новейший)

3. The kitchen is bright and *modern*.
4. Their equipment is *more modern* than ours.

---

### modern-day

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɑːdərnˈdeɪ/ &middot; *mah-durn-DAY*
**Русский:** современный, сегодняшний
**Type:** age
**Position:** attributive only — never after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — adjective + noun

Of the present time, especially set against the past.

1. He has been called a *modern-day* Robin Hood.
2. The play sets the old story in a *modern-day* office.
3. *Modern-day* slavery still exists in several industries.

---

### modernized

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɑːdərnaɪzd/ &middot; *MAH-dur-nyzd*
**Русский:** модернизированный, обновлённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ized from *modern*

Brought up to date with newer methods or equipment.

1. The *modernized* factory runs with half the staff.
2. Their tax system is fully *modernized*.
3. We moved into a *modernized* apartment in an old building.

---

### modest

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɑːdɪst/ &middot; *MAH-dist*
**Русский:** скромный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more modest*, *most modest*
**Takes:** *about* + noun, in the first sense
**Opposite:** *boastful*; *lavish*

Modest has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Not talking proudly about your own abilities.** (скромный, непритязательный)

1. He was *modest* about his part in the rescue.
2. She is famously *modest* for someone with two Olympic medals.

**2. Not large and not expensive.** (скромный, небольшой)

3. They live on a *modest* income.
4. The plan brought a *modest* improvement in test scores.

**3. Not showing much of the body.** (скромный (об одежде))

5. The dress code asks for *modest* clothing.
6. She wore a *modest* dark-blue suit.

---

### modified

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɑːdɪfaɪd/ &middot; *MAH-duh-fyd*
**Русский:** изменённый, модифицированный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *modify*

Changed a little, usually to fit a new purpose.

1. The team used a *modified* version of the standard engine.
2. He follows a *modified* diet after the surgery.
3. Genetically *modified* corn is common in the Midwest.

---

### modular

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɑːdʒələr/ &middot; *MAH-juh-lur*
**Русский:** модульный, блочный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** from *module*

Built from separate parts that can be rearranged or replaced.

1. The office uses *modular* furniture that can be rearranged.
2. The course is *modular*, so you can take the units in any order.
3. They build *modular* homes in a factory and truck them to the site.

---

### moist

**Pronunciation:** /mɔɪst/ &middot; *MOYST*
**Русский:** влажный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *moister*, *moistest*
**Opposite:** *dry*

Slightly wet, usually in a pleasant or intended way.

1. Keep the soil *moist* but not soaked.
2. Sour cream makes the cake *moister*.
3. Wipe the counter with a *moist* cloth.

---

### moldy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmoʊldi/ &middot; *MOHL-dee*
**Русский:** заплесневелый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *moldier*, *moldiest*
**Form:** -y from *mold*
**Contrast:** American *moldy* and *mold*; the British spellings are *mouldy* and *mould*.

Covered with mold.

1. Throw out the *moldy* bread.
2. The basement smells *moldy* after a rain.
3. The cheese at the back was even *moldier* than I feared.

---

### molecular

**Pronunciation:** /məˈlɛkjələr/ &middot; *muh-LEK-yuh-lur*
**Русский:** молекулярный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** from *molecule*

Relating to molecules.

1. She studies *molecular* biology.
2. The drug works at the *molecular* level.
3. They measured the *molecular* weight of the compound.

---

### molten

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmoʊltən/ &middot; *MOHL-tuhn*
**Русский:** расплавленный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** an old participle of *melt*
**Contrast:** Use *molten* only for metal, rock, glass or chocolate made liquid by great heat. Butter and ice are *melted*.

Made liquid by very high heat.

1. *Molten* steel poured out of the furnace.
2. The volcano sent *molten* rock down the slope.
3. He ordered the *molten* chocolate cake.

---

### momentary

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmoʊmənˌtɛri/ &middot; *MOH-muhn-ter-ee*
**Русский:** мгновенный, кратковременный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** from *moment*
**Contrast:** *Momentary* means lasting only a moment; *momentous* means enormously important.

Lasting only a very short time.

1. There was a *momentary* silence before anyone answered.
2. He felt a *momentary* pang of guilt.
3. A *momentary* loss of power shut down the servers.

---

### momentous

**Pronunciation:** /moʊˈmɛntəs/ &middot; *moh-MEN-tuhs*
**Русский:** знаменательный, судьбоносный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more momentous*, *most momentous*
**Form:** from *moment* in its old sense of importance

Very important because of the effects it will have.

1. It was a *momentous* day for the whole country.
2. The court reached a *momentous* decision.
3. Nothing in her career was *more momentous* than that first flight.

---

### monastic

**Pronunciation:** /məˈnæstɪk/ &middot; *muh-NAS-tik*
**Русский:** монашеский; аскетичный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more monastic*, *most monastic*
**Form:** from *monastery*

Monastic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Relating to monks, nuns or monasteries.** (монашеский, монастырский)

1. The library holds *monastic* records from the 1300s.
2. He entered a *monastic* order at twenty.

**2. Very plain and solitary, the way a monk lives.** (аскетичный, затворнический)

3. During finals he lives an almost *monastic* life.
4. Her apartment is *monastic* — a bed, a desk and nothing else.

---

### monetary

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɑːnəˌtɛri/ &middot; *MAH-nuh-ter-ee*
**Русский:** денежный, валютный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** related to *money*

Relating to money, especially a country's supply of it.

1. The Fed tightened *monetary* policy.
2. The painting has little *monetary* value but great sentimental value.
3. They agreed on a *monetary* settlement.

---

### moneyed

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmʌnid/ &middot; *MUHN-eed*
**Русский:** состоятельный, денежный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** from *money*
**Contrast:** Also spelled *monied*. It goes only before a noun: *a moneyed family*, but *the family is rich*.

Having a lot of money, often inherited money.

1. The club draws the town's *moneyed* families.
2. She grew up among the *moneyed* class on the East Side.
3. *Moneyed* interests fought the bill.

---

### moneymaking

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmʌniˌmeɪkɪŋ/ &middot; *MUHN-ee-may-king*
**Русский:** прибыльный, доходный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + *-ing*

Meant to make money, or good at making it.

1. The gift shop is the museum's best *moneymaking* operation.
2. He is always chasing some new *moneymaking* scheme.
3. Streaming became their main *moneymaking* business.

---

### Mongolian

**Pronunciation:** /mɑːŋˈɡoʊliən/ &middot; *mahng-GOH-lee-uhn*
**Русский:** монгольский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Always a capital *M*. *A Mongolian* meaning the person is a noun, not this adjective.

From Mongolia, or relating to Mongolia and its language.

1. We ate at a *Mongolian* barbecue place.
2. He is learning the *Mongolian* alphabet.
3. Her grandmother is *Mongolian*.

---

### monochromatic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɑːnəkroʊˈmætɪk/ &middot; *mah-nuh-kroh-MAT-ik*
**Русский:** одноцветный, монохромный
**Type:** color
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** mono- + *chromatic*

Using only one color, or shades of one color.

1. She painted the room in a *monochromatic* gray scheme.
2. His outfit was entirely *monochromatic*.
3. The laser produces *monochromatic* light.

---

### monochrome

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɑːnəkroʊm/ &middot; *MAH-nuh-krohm*
**Русский:** чёрно-белый; одноцветный
**Type:** color
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable

In black and white, or in shades of a single color.

1. The show opened with a series of *monochrome* portraits.
2. Early television was *monochrome*.
3. He prefers a *monochrome* wardrobe.

---

### monogamous

**Pronunciation:** /məˈnɑːɡəməs/ &middot; *muh-NAH-guh-muhs*
**Русский:** моногамный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** mono- + Greek *gamos*, marriage
**Opposite:** *polygamous*

Having only one partner or mate at a time.

1. Swans are largely *monogamous*.
2. They had agreed to be *monogamous*.
3. The book traces the history of *monogamous* marriage.

---

### monolingual

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɑːnəˈlɪŋɡwəl/ &middot; *mah-nuh-LING-gwuhl*
**Русский:** одноязычный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** mono- + *lingual*
**Opposite:** *bilingual*

Speaking, or written in, only one language.

1. Most of the students arrived *monolingual*.
2. He recommends a *monolingual* dictionary once you reach B2.
3. The town stayed *monolingual* until the 1990s.

---

### monolithic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɑːnəˈlɪθɪk/ &middot; *mah-nuh-LITH-ik*
**Русский:** монолитный, единообразный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more monolithic*, *most monolithic*
**Form:** mono- + Greek *lithos*, stone

Monolithic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Large, uniform and slow to change, of an organization or a system.** (монолитный, единообразный)

1. The party is less *monolithic* than outsiders assume.
2. He described the agency as a *monolithic* bureaucracy.

**2. Made of one huge block of stone.** (монолитный, цельнокаменный)

3. The temple's *monolithic* columns were cut from a single quarry.
4. A *monolithic* slab marks the grave.

---

### monopolistic

**Pronunciation:** /məˌnɑːpəˈlɪstɪk/ &middot; *muh-nah-puh-LIS-tik*
**Русский:** монополистический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more monopolistic*, *most monopolistic*
**Form:** from *monopoly*

Having, or trying to get, complete control of a market.

1. Regulators called the practice *monopolistic*.
2. The suit accuses the company of *monopolistic* behavior.
3. Local cable service was effectively *monopolistic*.

---

### monosyllabic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɑːnəsɪˈlæbɪk/ &middot; *mah-nuh-si-LAB-ik*
**Русский:** односложный; немногословный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more monosyllabic*, *most monosyllabic*
**Form:** mono- + *syllabic*, from *syllable*

Monosyllabic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having only one syllable.** (односложный)

1. *Cat*, *dog* and *tree* are *monosyllabic* words.
2. The poem is almost entirely *monosyllabic*.

**2. Answering in very few words, because you are sulky or unfriendly.** (немногословный, отвечающий односложно)

3. He was *monosyllabic* all through dinner.
4. Teenagers can be maddeningly *monosyllabic*.

---

### monotheistic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɑːnəθiˈɪstɪk/ &middot; *mah-nuh-thee-IS-tik*
**Русский:** монотеистический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** mono- + Greek *theos*, god
**Opposite:** *polytheistic*

Believing in one god.

1. Judaism, Christianity and Islam are *monotheistic* religions.
2. The shift to a *monotheistic* faith took centuries.
3. Their tradition is strictly *monotheistic*.

---

### monotonous

**Pronunciation:** /məˈnɑːtənəs/ &middot; *muh-NAH-tuh-nuhs*
**Русский:** однообразный, монотонный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more monotonous*, *most monotonous*
**Form:** mono- + Greek *tonos*, tone

Monotonous has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Boring because nothing about it ever changes.** (однообразный, нудный)

1. The work is steady but *monotonous*.
2. Nothing is *more monotonous* than a long drive across Kansas.

**2. Staying at one pitch, of a voice or a sound.** (монотонный (о голосе))

3. He read the list in a *monotonous* voice.
4. The *monotonous* hum of the fan put me to sleep.

---

### monounsaturated

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɑːnoʊʌnˈsætʃəreɪtɪd/ &middot; *mah-noh-uhn-SACH-uh-ray-tid*
**Русский:** мононенасыщенный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** mono- + *unsaturated*

Of a fat: having one double bond, and considered the healthier kind.

1. Olive oil is high in *monounsaturated* fat.
2. Avocados supply *monounsaturated* fats.
3. Most of the fat in almonds is *monounsaturated*.

---

### monstrous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɑːnstrəs/ &middot; *MAHN-struhs*
**Русский:** чудовищный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more monstrous*, *most monstrous*
**Form:** from *monster*

Monstrous has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Shockingly cruel or wrong.** (чудовищный, изуверский)

1. The court called the crime *monstrous*.
2. It would be *monstrous* to blame the children.

**2. Huge and frightening.** (громадный, устрашающий)

3. A *monstrous* wave hit the boat.
4. They put up a *monstrous* concrete tower on the corner.

---

### monumental

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɑːnjəˈmɛntəl/ &middot; *mah-nyuh-MEN-tuhl*
**Русский:** грандиозный; монументальный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more monumental*, *most monumental*
**Form:** from *monument*

Monumental has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Very great in size, importance or degree.** (грандиозный, колоссальный)

1. Rebuilding the archive was a *monumental* task.
2. It was a *monumental* mistake.

**2. Like a monument, built to last and to impress.** (монументальный)

3. The bank's *monumental* stone front dominates the square.
4. He is known for his *monumental* bronze sculptures.

---

### moody

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmuːdi/ &middot; *MOO-dee*
**Русский:** угрюмый; переменчивый в настроении
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *moodier*, *moodiest*
**Form:** -y from *mood*

Moody has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Often bad-tempered, or changing mood without warning.** (угрюмый, капризный)

1. He gets *moody* when he has not slept.
2. She was even *moodier* than usual that week.

**2. Suggesting a strong, usually dark mood, of light, music or a place.** (мрачный, атмосферный)

3. The film has a *moody* black-and-white look.
4. They played *moody* jazz all evening.

---

### moonless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmuːnləs/ &middot; *MOON-luhs*
**Русский:** безлунный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -less from *moon*

With no moon showing in the sky.

1. They crossed the field on a *moonless* night.
2. It was pitch dark under a *moonless*, starless sky.
3. A *moonless* week is the best time to watch for meteors.

---

### moonlit

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmuːnlɪt/ &middot; *MOON-lit*
**Русский:** залитый лунным светом
**Type:** compound
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + *lit*

Lit by the moon.

1. They walked along the *moonlit* beach.
2. The *moonlit* snow looked blue.
3. She painted a *moonlit* harbor.

---

### Moorish

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmʊrɪʃ/ &middot; *MUUR-ish*
**Русский:** мавританский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ish from *Moor*

Of the Moors, the Muslim people who once ruled parts of Spain and North Africa.

1. The palace is a famous example of *Moorish* architecture.
2. The tiles follow a *Moorish* pattern.
3. Much of the old quarter is still *Moorish* in style.

---

### moot

**Pronunciation:** /muːt/ &middot; *MOOT*
**Русский:** утративший актуальность; спорный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** In American English *a moot point* is one that no longer matters; British English leans toward still open to debate. A *moot court* is a law-school practice trial.

No longer worth arguing about, because it makes no practical difference.

1. Once she withdrew, the whole question became *moot*.
2. Whether we can afford it is now a *moot* point.
3. The appeal is *moot* — the building is already gone.

---

### moral

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɔːrəl/ &middot; *MOR-uhl*
**Русский:** моральный, нравственный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more moral*, *most moral*
**Opposite:** *immoral*
**Contrast:** *Moral* is the adjective; *morale*, *muh-RAL*, is a noun meaning how confident a group feels.

Moral has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Concerning right and wrong behavior.** (нравственный, моральный)

1. It raises a difficult *moral* question.
2. She felt a *moral* obligation to speak up.

**2. Behaving in the way most people consider right.** (нравственный, порядочный)

3. He is a deeply *moral* man.
4. Each side claims to be *more moral* than the other.

---

### moralistic

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɔːrəˈlɪstɪk/ &middot; *mor-uh-LIS-tik*
**Русский:** нравоучительный, морализаторский
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more moralistic*, *most moralistic*
**Form:** from *moralist*
**Contrast:** *Moral* is neutral; *moralistic* is a complaint — it means preachy.

Judging other people's behavior in a preachy way.

1. The editorial struck a *moralistic* tone.
2. I do not want a *moralistic* lecture about my diet.
3. His later novels grew *more moralistic*.

---

### morbid

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɔːrbɪd/ &middot; *MOR-bid*
**Русский:** нездоровый, мрачный
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more morbid*, *most morbid*

Morbid has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Too interested in death, illness or other unpleasant things.** (нездоровый, мрачный)

1. He has a *morbid* fascination with plane crashes.
2. Sorry for the *morbid* question, but who inherits the house?

**2. Relating to disease, in medical use.** (патологический, болезненный)

3. The report lists *morbid* obesity as a risk factor.
4. Pathologists study *morbid* changes in tissue.

---

### moribund

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɔːrɪbʌnd/ &middot; *MOR-i-buhnd*
**Русский:** умирающий, отживающий
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more moribund*, *most moribund*
**Form:** from Latin *mori*, to die

Moribund has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Near the end of its useful life, of an industry, a language or an organization.** (умирающий, приходящий в упадок)

1. The party was *moribund* by the time he took it over.
2. They bought a *moribund* steel mill and turned it around.

**2. Close to death, of a living thing, in formal use.** (умирающий, при смерти)

3. The patient was already *moribund* when he arrived.
4. A *moribund* colony of bees clung to the frame.

---

### Mormon

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɔːrmən/ &middot; *MOR-muhn*
**Русский:** мормонский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Always a capital *M*. *A Mormon* meaning a member of the church is a noun; the church itself prefers *Latter-day Saint*.

Belonging to the Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints.

1. He grew up in a *Mormon* family in Utah.
2. The *Mormon* temple is the city's landmark.
3. Both of her roommates are *Mormon*.

---

### Moroccan

**Pronunciation:** /məˈrɑːkən/ &middot; *muh-RAH-kuhn*
**Русский:** марокканский
**Type:** origin
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable

From Morocco, or relating to Morocco.

1. We had *Moroccan* mint tea after dinner.
2. She bought a *Moroccan* rug in Fez.
3. The chef is *Moroccan*.

---

### moronic

**Pronunciation:** /məˈrɑːnɪk/ &middot; *muh-RAH-nik*
**Русский:** идиотский, тупой
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more moronic*, *most moronic*
**Form:** from *moron*

Extremely stupid — an insult.

1. That was a *moronic* thing to do.
2. The plot gets *more moronic* with every sequel.
3. He laughed at his own *moronic* joke.

---

### morose

**Pronunciation:** /məˈroʊs/ &middot; *muh-ROHS*
**Русский:** угрюмый, мрачный
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more morose*, *most morose*

Gloomy and silent, in a bad mood.

1. He sat *morose* and quiet at the end of the bar.
2. She has been *morose* ever since the team lost.
3. A *morose* silence settled over the table.

---

### mortal

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɔːrtəl/ &middot; *MOR-tuhl*
**Русский:** смертный; смертельный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Opposite:** *immortal*

Mortal has 3 distinct senses.

**1. That must die one day.** (смертный)

1. The gods in the story envy their *mortal* lovers.
2. We are all *mortal*.

**2. Causing death.** (смертельный)

3. He suffered a *mortal* wound.
4. Smoke, not flame, is the *mortal* danger in a house fire.

**3. Very great, of fear or of an enemy.** (смертельный (о страхе, враге))

5. She lived in *mortal* fear of being found out.
6. The two networks are *mortal* enemies.

---

### mortified

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɔːrtɪfaɪd/ &middot; *MOR-tuh-fyd*
**Русский:** сгорающий от стыда
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both — mostly after a verb
**Comparison:** more/most — *more mortified*, *most mortified*
**Takes:** *by* or *at* + noun; *that*-clause
**Form:** -ed — how the person feels
**Contrast:** The person is *mortified*; the event that shames them is *mortifying*.

Deeply embarrassed and ashamed.

1. I was *mortified* when my phone went off during the service.
2. She looked *mortified* that anyone had noticed.
3. He was *mortified* by his own mistake.

---

### mortifying

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɔːrtɪfaɪɪŋ/ &middot; *MOR-tuh-fy-ing*
**Русский:** унизительный, постыдный
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more mortifying*, *most mortifying*
**Form:** -ing — what causes the feeling
**Contrast:** The event is *mortifying*; the person is *mortified*.

Causing deep embarrassment.

1. It was a *mortifying* way to lose.
2. Nothing is *more mortifying* than forgetting a name mid-introduction.
3. She told the whole *mortifying* story at dinner.

---

### mossy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɔːsi/ &middot; *MAW-see*
**Русский:** мшистый, поросший мхом
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *mossier*, *mossiest*
**Form:** -y from *moss*

Covered with moss.

1. We sat on a *mossy* log by the creek.
2. The north side of the roof is *mossier* than the south.
3. The steps were green and *mossy*.

---

### moth-eaten

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɔːθˌiːtən/ &middot; *MAWTH-ee-tuhn*
**Русский:** изъеденный молью; обветшалый
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — noun + participle

Moth-eaten has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Full of holes made by moths.** (изъеденный молью)

1. He pulled a *moth-eaten* sweater out of the trunk.
2. The curtains were gray and *moth-eaten*.

**2. Old and shabby.** (обветшалый, потрёпанный)

3. They stayed in a *moth-eaten* hotel by the station.
4. It is the same *moth-eaten* argument he makes every year.

---

### motherless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmʌðərləs/ &middot; *MUHTH-ur-luhs*
**Русский:** оставшийся без матери
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -less from *mother*

Having no mother.

1. The film follows two *motherless* boys in rural Georgia.
2. He was *motherless* by the age of six.
3. The shelter takes in *motherless* kittens.

---

### motionless

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmoʊʃənləs/ &middot; *MOH-shuhn-luhs*
**Русский:** неподвижный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -less from *motion*
**Opposite:** *moving*

Completely still.

1. The heron stood *motionless* in the shallows.
2. He lay *motionless* until the noise stopped.
3. A *motionless* crowd watched the screen.

---

### motivated

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmoʊtɪveɪtɪd/ &middot; *MOH-tuh-vay-tid*
**Русский:** мотивированный; вызванный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more motivated*, *most motivated*
**Takes:** *to*-infinitive; *by* + noun
**Form:** -ed — how the person feels
**Opposite:** *unmotivated*

Motivated has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Eager and willing to work at something.** (мотивированный, целеустремлённый)

1. She is the *most motivated* student in the class.
2. The team came back *motivated* after the break.

**2. Caused by a particular aim or reason.** (вызванный, продиктованный)

3. Police called it a racially *motivated* attack.
4. The decision was politically *motivated*.

---

### motivating

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmoʊtɪveɪtɪŋ/ &middot; *MOH-tuh-vay-ting*
**Русский:** мотивирующий, побуждающий
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more motivating*, *most motivating*
**Form:** -ing — what causes the feeling
**Contrast:** The coach is *motivating*; the players are *motivated*.

Making someone want to act or work harder.

1. Money turned out to be less *motivating* than praise.
2. Fear was the *motivating* factor behind the move.
3. He gave a short, *motivating* talk before the game.

---

### motivational

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmoʊtɪˈveɪʃənəl/ &middot; *moh-tuh-VAY-shuh-nuhl*
**Русский:** мотивационный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** from *motivation*

Meant to encourage people to try harder.

1. The company hired a *motivational* speaker.
2. Her office is covered in *motivational* posters.
3. He listens to *motivational* podcasts on the drive in.

---

### motley

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɑːtli/ &middot; *MAHT-lee*
**Русский:** разношёрстный, пёстрый
**Type:** quality
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Almost always in *a motley crew* or *a motley collection*.

Made up of very different, badly matched parts.

1. A *motley* crew of volunteers showed up.
2. The garage held a *motley* collection of bikes.
3. They were a *motley* group of students, retirees and tourists.

---

### motor

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmoʊtər/ &middot; *MOH-tur*
**Русский:** двигательный, моторный
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** In *motor oil* or *motor home*, *motor* is a noun used as a modifier. As a true adjective it means relating to muscle movement: *motor skills*, *motor neurons*.

Relating to the muscles and nerves that produce movement.

1. Toddlers develop fine *motor* skills through play.
2. The disease attacks the *motor* neurons.
3. She is regaining *motor* control in her left hand.

---

### motorized

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmoʊtəraɪzd/ &middot; *MOH-tuh-ryzd*
**Русский:** моторизованный, с мотором
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ized from *motor*

Fitted with a motor, or using motor vehicles.

1. He rides a *motorized* scooter to work.
2. *Motorized* boats are banned on the lake.
3. Her wheelchair is *motorized*.

---

### mottled

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɑːtəld/ &middot; *MAHT-uhld*
**Русский:** пятнистый, крапчатый
**Type:** color
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more mottled*, *most mottled*

Marked with patches of different colors.

1. The cat has a *mottled* gray coat.
2. His hands were *mottled* with age spots.
3. *Mottled* light fell through the leaves.

---

### mountainous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmaʊntənəs/ &middot; *MOWN-tuh-nuhs*
**Русский:** гористый; огромный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more mountainous*, *most mountainous*
**Form:** -ous from *mountain*

Mountainous has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having many mountains.** (гористый, горный)

1. The road runs through the *mountainous* part of the state.
2. Western Montana is far *more mountainous* than the east.

**2. Enormous.** (огромный, громадный)

3. *Mountainous* waves swamped the deck.
4. They are buried under *mountainous* debt.

### mounted

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmaʊntɪd/ &middot; *MOWN-tid*
**Русский:** конный; закреплённый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -ed from *mount*

Mounted has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Riding a horse.** (конный, верхом)

1. *Mounted* police lined the parade route.
2. A *mounted* patrol rode along the beach.

**2. Fixed onto a wall or another support.** (закреплённый, смонтированный)

3. The TV is wall-*mounted*.
4. Each photo is *mounted* on stiff card.

---

### mournful

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɔːrnfəl/ &middot; *MORN-fuhl*
**Русский:** скорбный, печальный
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more mournful*, *most mournful*
**Form:** -ful from *mourn*

Full of sadness, or sounding sad.

1. A *mournful* train whistle carried across the valley.
2. The dog gave me a *mournful* look.
3. Her voice was low and *mournful*.

---

### mousy

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmaʊsi/ &middot; *MOW-see*
**Русский:** мышиного цвета; робкий, незаметный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** -er/-est — *mousier*, *mousiest*
**Form:** -y from *mouse*

Mousy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Of hair: a dull light brown.** (мышиного цвета (о волосах))

1. She dyed her *mousy* hair dark red.
2. He had *mousy* brown curls.

**2. Shy, quiet and easy to overlook.** (серый, робкий, незаметный)

3. The new clerk seemed *mousy* at first.
4. Her older sister was even *mousier*.

### mouth-watering

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmaʊθˌwɔːtərɪŋ/ &middot; *MOWTH-waw-tur-ing*
**Русский:** аппетитный
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more mouth-watering*, *most mouth-watering*
**Form:** compound — noun + *-ing* participle

Looking or smelling so good that it makes you hungry.

1. The bakery window was full of *mouth-watering* pastries.
2. That smells *mouth-watering*.
3. They sent over a *mouth-watering* photo of the ribs.

### movable

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmuːvəbəl/ &middot; *MOO-vuh-buhl*
**Русский:** передвижной, переносной
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** -able from *move*
**Opposite:** *fixed*
**Contrast:** Also spelled *moveable*; American editors prefer *movable*. Easter is *a movable feast* — its date changes every year.

Able to be moved from one place to another.

1. The room has *movable* partitions.
2. The shelves are *movable*, so you can fit tall books.
3. He built a *movable* ramp for the van.

### moved

**Pronunciation:** /muːvd/ &middot; *MOOVD*
**Русский:** растроганный, тронутый
**Type:** emotion
**Position:** predicative only — never before a noun
**Comparison:** more/most — *more moved*, *most moved*
**Takes:** *by* + noun; *to*-infinitive
**Form:** -ed — how the person feels
**Contrast:** The person is *moved*; the film that does it is *moving*.

Feeling strong emotion, usually sadness or tenderness.

1. I was deeply *moved* by her letter.
2. The audience sat in silence, clearly *moved*.
3. He was *moved* to tears by the ovation.

---

### moving

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmuːvɪŋ/ &middot; *MOO-ving*
**Русский:** трогательный; движущийся
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more moving*, *most moving*
**Form:** -ing — what causes the feeling

Moving has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Causing strong feeling, especially sadness or sympathy.** (трогательный, волнующий)

1. It was a *moving* tribute to her teacher.
2. The last chapter is even *more moving* than the first.

**2. In motion.** (движущийся, подвижный)

3. Never jump off a *moving* train.
4. The camera tracks a *moving* target.

### mucous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmjuːkəs/ &middot; *MYOO-kuhs*
**Русский:** слизистый
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *Mucous* is the adjective and *mucus* the noun, and the two sound exactly alike. Write *the mucous membrane*, but *too much mucus*.

Relating to mucus, or covered with it.

1. The virus enters through the *mucous* membrane.
2. Smoke irritates the *mucous* lining of the throat.
3. They swabbed a *mucous* surface for the sample.

**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more muddled*, *most muddled*
**Opposite:** *clear*

Confused, or badly mixed up.

1. His explanation was *muddled* and hard to follow.
2. I got *muddled* halfway through the instructions.
3. The policy is a *muddled* compromise.

Muddy has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Covered with mud, or full of mud.** (грязный, покрытый грязью)

1. Take off those *muddy* boots.
2. The trail gets *muddier* after a storm.

**2. Not clear, of a color, a sound or an idea.** (мутный, неясный)

3. The recording is *muddy* at the low end.
4. He paints in *muddy* browns and greens.

Made quieter, as if covered by something.

3. There was *muffled* laughter in the back row.

### muggy

**Comparison:** -er/-est — *muggier*, *muggiest*

Unpleasantly warm and damp, of weather.

2. It feels *muggier* today than it did yesterday.
3. We sat on the porch in the *muggy* evening air.

### multicolored

**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** multi- + *colored*

Having many colors.

3. Her sneakers are *multicolored*.

Including or relating to several cultures.

Made up of people from several ethnic groups.

Involving more than two countries or groups.

Having several levels or stages.

Speaking, or written in, several languages.

More than one; several.

Having several floors.

Ordinary and dull, with nothing to make it interesting.

Belonging to the government of a city or town.

Killed deliberately and unlawfully.

### murderous

**Form:** -ous from *murder*

Murderous has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Ready to kill, or full of the wish to kill.** (кровожадный, смертоносный)

1. He gave me a *murderous* look and walked out.

**2. Extremely hard or unpleasant to get through.** (убийственный, изматывающий)

3. The training schedule that spring was *murderous*.

**2. Unclear and probably hiding something dishonest.** (тёмный, сомнительный, неясный)

Having big, stiff muscles from too much weight training, so that moving is awkward.

2. He has a *muscular* build from years of rowing.

4. She was diagnosed with *muscular* dystrophy as a child.

### mutant

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmjuːtənt/ &middot; *MYOO-tuhnt*
**Русский:** мутантный, мутировавший
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *a mutant gene*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** from the verb *mutate*
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *the mutants in the comic*. As an adjective it goes only before the noun.

Changed by a mutation; carrying an altered gene.

1. The lab isolated a *mutant* strain of the virus.
2. *Mutant* genes can be passed to the next generation.
3. The show is full of *mutant* superheroes.

---

### mute

**Pronunciation:** /mjuːt/ &middot; *MYOOT*
**Русский:** безмолвный, немой
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more mute*, *most mute*
**Contrast:** Calling a person *mute* is dated and can offend; say *nonverbal* or *unable to speak*. *Mute* is also a verb — *mute the TV* means turn the sound off.

Saying nothing, especially when you could speak.

1. She stood *mute* while he shouted at the clerk.
2. He gave a *mute* nod of agreement.
3. The whole room went *mute* when she said it.

---

### muted

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmjuːtɪd/ &middot; *MYOO-tid*
**Русский:** приглушённый; сдержанный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more muted*, *most muted*

Muted has 3 distinct senses.

**1. Made quieter or softer in sound.** (приглушённый (о звуке))

1. We heard the *muted* sound of traffic through the glass.
2. He spoke in a *muted* voice so the baby wouldn't wake.

**2. Soft and not bright, of a color.** (приглушённый (о цвете))

3. The living room is done in *muted* grays and browns.
4. She prefers *muted* colors to bright ones.

**3. Held back, of a reaction or a feeling.** (сдержанный, вялый)

5. The announcement got a *muted* response from investors.
6. Their celebration was *muted* by the news from home.

---

### mutilated

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmjuːtəleɪtɪd/ &middot; *MYOO-tuh-lay-tid*
**Русский:** изувеченный, изуродованный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more mutilated*, *most mutilated*

Badly damaged, with parts cut off or torn away.

1. Rangers found the *mutilated* body of a deer near the trail.
2. Vandals left the statue *mutilated* and covered in paint.
3. The library won't lend out *mutilated* copies.

---

### mutinous

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmjuːtənəs/ &middot; *MYOO-tuh-nuhs*
**Русский:** мятежный, бунтарский
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more mutinous*, *most mutinous*
**Form:** -ous from *mutiny*

Refusing to obey the people in charge; ready to rebel.

1. The crew grew *mutinous* after weeks without pay.
2. She shot him a *mutinous* look and kept typing.
3. The manager faced a *mutinous* staff on Monday morning.

---

### mutual

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmjuːtʃuəl/ &middot; *MYOO-choo-uhl*
**Русский:** взаимный; общий
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both — usually before a noun; predicative in *The feeling is mutual*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** *A mutual friend* is a friend the two of you share. A Russian speaker may reach for *common friend*, but *common* in English suggests *ordinary*.

Mutual has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Felt or done by each of two people toward the other.** (взаимный, обоюдный)

1. They parted by *mutual* agreement.
2. The respect between them was *mutual*.

**2. Shared by two or more people.** (общий)

3. We were introduced by a *mutual* friend.
4. The two countries have a *mutual* interest in trade.

---

### mutually exclusive

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmjuːtʃuəli ɪkˈskluːsɪv/ &middot; *MYOO-choo-uh-lee ik-SKLOO-siv*
**Русский:** взаимоисключающий
**Type:** compound
**Position:** both — usually after a verb
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Form:** compound — adverb + adjective: *mutually* + *exclusive*

Such that if one of two things is true or chosen, the other cannot be.

1. The two options are not *mutually exclusive* — you can do both.
2. Being careful and being fast aren't *mutually exclusive*.
3. He treats fun and hard work as *mutually exclusive* categories.

---

### myopic

**Pronunciation:** /maɪˈɑːpɪk/ &middot; *my-AH-pik*
**Русский:** недальновидный; близорукий
**Type:** evaluative
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more myopic*, *most myopic*
**Opposite:** *farsighted*

Myopic has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Thinking only about the short term, with no view of what comes later.** (недальновидный, близорукий (о политике, решении))

1. The policy is *myopic* — it ignores what happens after the next election.
2. Cutting research is a *myopic* way to save money.

**2. Able to see near things clearly but not distant ones.** (близорукий (о зрении))

3. Most *myopic* children need glasses by middle school.
4. He's been *myopic* since he was eight.

---

### myriad

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɪriəd/ &middot; *MEER-ee-uhd*
**Русский:** бесчисленный, несметный
**Type:** quantity
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *myriad problems*
**Comparison:** not gradable
**Contrast:** Also a noun: *a myriad of choices*. As an adjective it takes no *of*: *myriad choices*.

Very many; too many to count.

1. The city faces *myriad* problems at once.
2. There are *myriad* ways to cook an egg.
3. She listed the *myriad* reasons the plan would fail.

---

### mysterious

**Pronunciation:** /mɪˈstɪriəs/ &middot; *mih-STEER-ee-uhs*
**Русский:** загадочный, таинственный
**Type:** quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more mysterious*, *most mysterious*
**Form:** -ous from *mystery*

Mysterious has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Strange and hard to explain or understand.** (загадочный, необъяснимый)

1. A *mysterious* package showed up on the porch.
2. Her illness has a *mysterious* cause that no test has found.

**2. Keeping things secret, so that others cannot guess what you mean.** (таинственный, скрытный)

3. He was *mysterious* about where the money came from.
4. She gave me a *mysterious* smile and changed the subject.

---

### mystical

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɪstɪkəl/ &middot; *MIS-ti-kuhl*
**Русский:** мистический; таинственный, загадочный
**Type:** classifying; also quality
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more mystical*, *most mystical*

Mystical has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Having to do with direct spiritual experience of God or the divine.** (мистический (о религиозном опыте))

1. He writes about the *mystical* traditions of medieval Spain.
2. She had a *mystical* experience during the retreat.

**2. Strange and full of awe, as if beyond ordinary explanation.** (таинственный, загадочный)

3. There's something *mystical* about the light in that valley.
4. The old house has a *mystical* pull on him.

---

### mystified

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɪstəfaɪd/ &middot; *MIS-tuh-fyd*
**Русский:** сбитый с толку, озадаченный
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more mystified*, *most mystified*
**Takes:** *by* + noun
**Contrast:** *-ed* for the person who feels it, *-ing* for the thing that causes it: *I was mystified by the mystifying instructions*.

Completely puzzled by something.

1. I was *mystified* by the bill from the clinic.
2. Doctors were *mystified* when her fever broke on its own.
3. He gave me a *mystified* look, so I explained it again.

---

### mystifying

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɪstəfaɪɪŋ/ &middot; *MIS-tuh-fy-ing*
**Русский:** озадачивающий, непостижимый
**Type:** participial
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** more/most — *more mystifying*, *most mystifying*
**Contrast:** *Mystifying* is the thing; *mystified* is the person: *the mystifying rules left us mystified*.

Hard to understand or explain, in a way that leaves people puzzled.

1. The instructions that came with the shelf are *mystifying*.
2. She has a *mystifying* habit of answering questions with questions.
3. The ending of the movie was *mystifying* to everyone in the theater.

---

### mythical

**Pronunciation:** /ˈmɪθɪkəl/ &middot; *MITH-i-kuhl*
**Русский:** мифический; вымышленный, несуществующий
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** both
**Comparison:** not gradable

Mythical has 2 distinct senses.

**1. Coming from myths and old stories.** (мифический)

1. The griffin is a *mythical* creature, half eagle and half lion.
2. He collects stories about *mythical* beasts.

**2. Talked about but not real.** (вымышленный, несуществующий)

3. That *mythical* raise never showed up.
4. Everyone keeps chasing the *mythical* perfect job.

---

### mythological

**Pronunciation:** /ˌmɪθəˈlɑːdʒɪkəl/ &middot; *mith-uh-LAH-ji-kuhl*
**Русский:** мифологический
**Type:** classifying
**Position:** attributive only — before a noun: *mythological figures*
**Comparison:** not gradable

Belonging to the myths of a people, or to the study of them.

1. The museum has a room of *mythological* figures in marble.
2. Her name comes from a *mythological* goddess.
3. He teaches a course on *mythological* themes in film.
